## 1. Introduction

### 1.1 Purpose

This specification records, as verifiable requirements, what I&D Quality Demand Planning (formerly the Demand Planning Hub) does in the build named above. It is the reference for verification ([TEST-PLAN.md](TEST-PLAN.md)), for UAT, and for judging later changes.

### 1.2 Scope and system boundary

The **system** ("the app") is the I&D Quality Demand Planning single-page web application. It runs:

- in a browser from GitHub Pages; and
- inside the desktop shell (Electron; installer and update feed still named Demand-Planning-Hub) on Windows and macOS.

External systems, which the system uses but does not control:

| External system | Interaction |
|---|---|
| Azure DevOps Services / Azure DevOps Server | Reads work items, iterations, areas and fields over REST. Writes a constrained set of fields. PAT authentication. |
| GitHub (repository contents API) | Reads and writes one planning-data JSON file (baselines, scopes, resource mapping). |
| GitHub Releases (`desktop-latest`) | Source of desktop installers and `latest.json`, for download and self-update. |
| Microsoft Entra ID + Microsoft Graph (SharePoint) | *(Backlog — not used: SharePoint is not functional.)* Snapshots go to the Git data repository instead. |
| Browser storage (IndexedDB, localStorage, sessionStorage) | Local persistence of app data, settings and credentials. |

Out of scope:

- server-side components;
- role-based authorization (all audit entries are attributed to the single local actor `u-owner`);
- rendered PPT, PDF or PNG reports.

### 1.3 Conventions

The requirements are written to the characteristics of good requirements that the INCOSE *Guide for Writing Requirements* describes:

- necessary
- appropriate
- unambiguous
- complete
- singular
- feasible
- verifiable
- correct
- conforming

In practice:

- **Levels.** Requirements are kept at three levels, each traced to the level above:
  - **User requirements (UR-NN, §3)** state what the stakeholders need, in their words, with the use cases (§2) they come from. They are the design inputs in the sense of ISO 13485 §7.3.3.
  - **System requirements (SR-NN, §4)** state what the system provides for each user requirement, one per functional area; each names the detailed-requirement prefix that refines it. They are the software requirements of IEC 62304 §5.2.
  - **Detailed requirements (prefix-NN, §5 to §7)** are the verifiable "shall" statements that the test cases (TEST-PLAN.md §6) verify.
- **Wording.** Each requirement is a single "The app shall …" statement with one result. Where a statement lists alternatives, the list is closed and named. Vague terms ("user-friendly", "fast", "etc.", "and/or") are not used. Numbers carry units.
- **Definitions.** Terms with a specific meaning are defined in §1.4 and used consistently.
- **Conditions.** These are written as "When …" or "If …" clauses at the start of the statement.
- **Attributes.** Every requirement carries:
  - **ID**: unique and stable, never reused.
  - **Rationale**: why it is needed.
  - **Verification method**:
    - **T** Test: automated or manual execution with a pass/fail result.
    - **D** Demonstration: operating the app and observing the result.
    - **I** Inspection: reading code, configuration or documents.
    - **A** Analysis: calculation or reasoning from other evidence.
  - **Priority**:
    - **M** Must: release-blocking.
    - **S** Should.
    - **C** Could.
  - **Trace**: up to the use cases (§2) and, through the prefix, the system and user requirements (§3, §4); across to test cases (TEST-PLAN.md §6). The complete chain is generated in [TRACEABILITY-MATRIX.md](TRACEABILITY-MATRIX.md).
- **Rationale placement.** Rationale is kept out of the requirement text. Where several requirements share one rationale, the rationale is given once at the head of the group.
- **UI strings.** Quoted strings are the exact UI text and are part of the requirement.

Known deviations from the INCOSE guide:

- Some requirements describe as-built behaviour that has an open question attached. These are cross-referenced to the open issues (OI-NN) in [KNOWN-ISSUES.md](KNOWN-ISSUES.md) instead of being changed silently.
- This document is a self-assessment against the guide's characteristics. It has not been reviewed against a specific edition of the guide by an INCOSE-qualified reviewer.

### 1.4 Definitions

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Connection | A saved Azure DevOps configuration in this browser. Fields: name, view name, show-in-menu, role, base URL (collection), project, optional team, PAT, optional WIQL query, creator email (the work email, required for Time Writing), optional API version. |
| Role (of a connection) | One of: `time-tracking` (Time Writing, read & write), `backlog-view` (read only), `release-source` (DHFI release documents, read only), `documents` (read only), `reference` (read only). |
| Active Time Writing connection | The selected `time-tracking` connection whose data feeds Dashboard, Monthly Time Writing, My Tasks [Board] and Baselines. |
| Month Task | An Azure DevOps Task whose title starts or ends with a month and year (§5.4). |
| Time Writing story | A User Story that is the parent of at least one Month Task, or whose title contains "Time Writing" (case-insensitive; space, `_` or `-` allowed between the words). |
| Absence Epic | The Epic whose ID is set in Capacity settings (default 37778). Tasks under it count as absence, not work. |
| Selected month | The `YYYY-MM` month chosen in the header month picker. Defaults to the current month in UTC (NAV-51). |
| Pending | Not in the state Closed, Resolved, Done, Completed, Removed or Cut (case-insensitive). |
| Me | The identity used for "my" work: the Feature creator email of the active Time Writing connection, or a person name chosen in the app. |
| Saved data | The app state in IndexedDB database `demand-planning-data-v1`, store `snapshots`, key `current`. |
| Sample mode | The state after "Load synthetic sample" (`azureBacklogScope.sample = true`), until a fetch from Azure replaces it. |
| Build number | The CI run number (`GITHUB_RUN_NUMBER`) of the build that produced the app. |

## 2. Stakeholders and use cases

Stakeholders:

- **Team member** (QE/QMS/CAPA): writes their own time.
- **Team lead / manager**: plans capacity and reviews utilization.
- **Process owner**: sets periods, baselines and estimation rules.
- **App owner**: connections, storage, releases and deployment.

| ID | Use case | Primary actor |
|---|---|---|
| UC-01 | Navigate between pages and see data status in one place | All |
| UC-02 | Configure Azure DevOps connections and menu views | App owner |
| UC-03 | Fetch Azure DevOps data and have it saved automatically | All |
| UC-04 | Enter monthly Original Estimate and Completed Work on Month Tasks | Team member |
| UC-05 | Review gaps: items not picked up for the month | Team member, lead |
| UC-06 | Work through items assigned to me (finish, move to the current Sprint, create month tasks) | Team member |
| UC-07 | Browse and extend the Time Writing backlog (create a Feature hierarchy) | Team member, lead |
| UC-08 | View read-only query views and the Releases register | Lead, app owner |
| UC-09 | Review team or individual capacity for a month (Dashboard → This month) | Lead, manager |
| UC-10 | Review trends and problem areas over 6 or 12 months (Dashboard → Trends) | Lead, manager |
| UC-11 | Maintain estimation rules, release scopes and baselines | Process owner |
| UC-12 | Keep baselines and the resource mapping in a private Git repository | Process owner |
| UC-13 | Save LTE/PI snapshots to the Git data repository (SharePoint central storage: backlog) | Lead, app owner |
| UC-14 | Configure capacity, periods, resources, master data; review the audit | Process owner |
| UC-15 | Back up, restore and recover local data | All |
| UC-16 | Update the web app to the latest deployed build | All |
| UC-17 | Install and update the desktop app | All |
| UC-18 | Explore the app with a synthetic sample dataset | All |
| UC-19 | Build, verify and deploy the app | App owner (CI) |
| UC-20 | Read the requirements, test plan and other project documents in the app | All |
| UC-21 | Review backlog hygiene for the whole team: work left in ended Sprints, Features without DoD, unassigned work (Backlog Health) | Scrum Master, lead |
| UC-22 | Decide which menu areas each role sees and what it may do, and assign roles to people by email | Admin |
| UC-23 | Upload the existing QE mapping Excel and keep it up to date in the app — filter, edit, add and delete rows and columns — shared with the team | QE lead |
| UC-25 | See the month's key figures, exceptions, timeline health and evidence-based insights on one page beside the Dashboard, and queue the insights for the Monthly pack | Lead, manager |
| UC-26 | Open the app on my own picture — my hours against my capacity, my open work, my streams and releases — while the team's insights are shown only to the roles that lead people; look at Pulse Check over a quarter, half-year or year | All; lead, manager |
| UC-27 | Bring the planned and recorded hours of the months before Time Writing in Azure DevOps — people who have left included — into the reports from the team's planning workbook, once, without writing to Azure DevOps | Lead, manager |
| UC-28 | Record my month in one place — add this month's tasks for my recurring work, enter planned and actual hours, add a new activity and see what is left before the deadline — on a page first tried out by admins | Team member, admin |
| UC-29 | See the team's month on one page — workload against capacity, delivery against Target dates and what needs attention — filtered by region, Epic and owner, on a page first tried out by admins | Lead, manager |
| UC-30 | Compare what the team had — capacity after leave and holidays — with the hours it booked, for the month and the year before, per person and per Epic, on a page first tried out by admins | Lead, manager |
| UC-31 | See the team's KPI tracker — an Excel workbook in SharePoint — in the app, read live in the desktop app and from the copy in Git when SharePoint cannot be reached | Lead, manager |
| UC-32 | See the QE and possible backup QEs of each DP release, and create the Features of several DP releases at once — with the right DoD and User Stories for their release type — assigned to their QE | QE lead, manager |
| UC-33 | Create the Features of several Content releases at once, all assigned to one person | QE lead |
| UC-34 | Set up, per Epic, the extra fields a new Feature carries (e.g. Program, Platform) and their drop-down values, once for everyone | Tool Owner, Admin, Scrum Master |
| UC-35 | See what is left to set up and what is not yet shared, and find any setting quickly | Admin, Tool Owner |
| UC-36 | Keep User Stories as reusable templates, tag them to Epics and Release Classification values, and pick them into an Epic's setup so they stay in step | Tool Owner, Admin, Scrum Master |
| UC-37 | Set up a blank page of our own — CoNQ (Cost of Non-Quality) first — with the columns we need (text, numbers, dates, drop-downs, multi-selects), fill it in together, see it in charts and summary tables of our own, and keep it in the Git data repository | Quality lead, Tool Owner, Admin |
| UC-38 | Add a page of our own as a Tool Owner — a Table, a Table with Insights or an On-Demand Dashboard of KPI tiles, charts and summary tables — to any menu group that accepts pages or to a new group, without a release of the tool, and hand groups and pages to co-owners who run them | Tool Owner, group and page co-owners |
| UC-24 | Prepare the two monthly Pulse Check slides (2x2 and Performance Pulse) for the month before the header month from the app's data, with the month's text kept in Git, and download them as .pptx | Lead, manager |

## 3. User requirements

User requirements are what the stakeholders of §2 need from the tool, written as needs, not as solutions. Each traces to the use cases it comes from and is refined by the system requirements of §4. They are the design inputs; the system and detailed requirements are the design outputs that implement them.

| ID | User requirement | Stakeholder | Use cases | Refined by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UR-01 | I need one place that shows the pages I use, how fresh the data is, which month is selected and what is still unsaved, so that I never work on stale data without knowing it. | All | UC-01 | SR-01, SR-03 |
| UR-02 | I need the tool to read our Azure DevOps backlogs (cloud and on-premises) and our Windchill exports with my own credentials, without any server component that IT would have to host. | App owner, lead | UC-02, UC-03 | SR-02, SR-03, SR-24 |
| UR-03 | I need to record my planned and actual hours per month on Azure DevOps Tasks, with deadlines and locks that the process owner controls, so that time writing is complete and on time. | Team member, process owner | UC-04, UC-05 | SR-04, SR-05 |
| UR-04 | I need to see and work through the items assigned to me — finish them, move them to the current Sprint, create this month's tasks — in bulk where possible. | Team member | UC-06 | SR-06 |
| UR-05 | I need new Features, User Stories and Tasks created in a consistent hierarchy with the agreed titles and a Definition of Done, so that the backlog stays uniform. | Team member, lead | UC-07 | SR-07 |
| UR-06 | I need read-only views of releases and documents (Azure DevOps Server queries, Windchill exports) with the unique releases and their milestones, and the link to the Feature that tracks each release. | Lead, app owner | UC-08 | SR-08, SR-39 |
| UR-07 | I need to review capacity, utilization, non-project time (NPRA) and trends per person, region, role and stream, so that I can balance the load. | Lead, manager | UC-09, UC-10 | SR-09, SR-10 |
| UR-08 | I need estimation rules, release scopes and baselines per stream, with the history of changes, to compare planned and booked effort. | Process owner | UC-11 | SR-11 |
| UR-09 | I need the shared planning data, the connection setup and the imported exports kept in a governed private repository so that the whole team sees the same data. | Process owner, app owner | UC-12, UC-13 | SR-12, SR-13 |

## 4. System requirements

System requirements state what the system provides for the user requirements, one per functional area. Each is refined by the detailed requirements with the prefix named in **Refined by** (§5 to §7); every detailed requirement therefore traces to exactly one system requirement through its prefix.

| ID | System requirement | Derived from | Refined by |
|---|---|---|---|
| SR-01 | The system shall provide a single-page shell with a grouped sidebar, a header with the page title, month picker and data status, notices, a modal layer and a footer with storage and build information. | UR-01, UR-20 | NAV (§5.1) |
| SR-02 | The system shall manage Azure DevOps and Windchill connections per browser (servers, PATs, roles, views, presets), share the setup through Git, sync and clean duplicates, provide role-based access, Backlog Health, work-item creation rules, Feature setups and the Windchill release list. | UR-02, UR-06, UR-16, UR-17, UR-19 | CON (§5.2) |
| SR-03 | The system shall fetch work items from Azure DevOps in batches, keep previews and saved caches apart, save fetched data automatically and show the data status and notices. | UR-01, UR-02 | FET (§5.3) |
| SR-04 | The system shall recognise Month Tasks and Time Writing stories from their titles and parent chain according to the naming rules. | UR-03 | TWN (§5.4) |
| SR-05 | The system shall provide Monthly Time Writing: the month's Tasks per team or person, planning and actuals locks, staged edits validated before one save, and the not-picked-up check. | UR-03 | MON (§5.5) |
| SR-06 | The system shall provide My Tasks [Board] and Bulk actions: my pending items, close, move to the current Sprint, create month tasks, state checks and search. | UR-04 | ATM (§5.6) |
| SR-07 | The system shall provide the Time Writing backlog tree and the creation of a Feature hierarchy (Feature, optional stories, Time Writing story, month Task) with mandatory DoD and read-back. | UR-05 | BKL (§5.7) |
| SR-08 | The system shall render read-only query views (columns from the query, sort, filter, search, CSV), the DP table with milestones, column arrangement and QMS-0025 match, the Releases list and Pulse Check's Supported Releases. | UR-06 | QV, OV-10 (§5.8) |
| SR-09 | The system shall provide the Dashboard over a period with utilization, overload, rebalancing, trends, streams and insights on one utilization model. | UR-07 | OVR, OV (§5.9) |
| SR-10 | The system shall compute capacity: working days, funded and available hours, absence, shared time, NPRA, utilization status, streams and people. | UR-07, UR-10 | CAP (§5.10) |
| SR-11 | The system shall maintain baseline rules, release scopes with history, baseline vs. actual hours and the task plan check. | UR-08 | BAS (§5.11) |
| SR-12 | The system shall keep planning data in a private Git repository: validated owner/repo, token in this browser only, schema-checked file, conflict detection, automatic load. | UR-09, UR-19 | GIT (§5.12) |
| SR-13 | The system shall keep LTE and PI Review snapshots as JSON files in the Git data repository — new files only, read back before success, never in the browser. *(The SharePoint central storage of the app state is retired to the backlog: SharePoint is not functional.)* | UR-09 | SNP (§5.13) |
| SR-14 | The system shall provide Settings for capacity, periods, resources, master data and the audit trail. | UR-10 | SET (§5.14) |
| SR-15 | The system shall persist the app state in IndexedDB, migrate legacy data, report save failures, and export and restore validated JSON backups without credentials. | UR-11, UR-19 | STO (§5.15) |
| SR-16 | The system shall emit build information and update the web app only to the deployed build, blocking the reload while work is unsaved. | UR-12 | UPD (§5.16) |
| SR-17 | The system shall run as a hardened desktop shell with a read-only on-premises proxy and an updater that downloads only from the fixed release and verifies SHA-256. | UR-12, UR-19 | DSK (§5.17) |
| SR-18 | The system shall provide a deterministic synthetic sample covering every model and shall mark sample mode. | UR-13 | SMP (§5.18) |
| SR-19 | The system shall show the project documents of its own build on the Documentation page, rendered without HTML injection. | UR-15 | DOC (§5.19) |
| SR-20 | The system shall provide themes, accessible controls, visible actions and the product name and mark. | UR-20 | UI (§5.20) |
| SR-21 | The system shall be built, tested, packaged, published (installers with SHA-256 sums and `latest.json`) and deployed by CI on every pull request and push to main. | UR-14 | CI (§5.21) |
| SR-22 | The system shall keep the QE Mapping as a shared, editable, filterable table in Git with Excel import and export. | UR-18 | QEM (§5.22) |
| SR-25 | The system shall generate the Monthly pack — the 2x2 and Performance Pulse slides for the month before the header month — from the release, utilisation and Epic data, with a shared setup and the month's text in Git, as copy-ready text and a .pptx download. | UR-21 | PCK (§5.23) |
| SR-23 | The system shall meet the non-functional requirements: no server component, data volume, timeouts, confirmed writes, PAT handling, window size, no production data in the repository. | UR-11, UR-19 | NFR (§6) |
| SR-24 | The system shall use the external interfaces as specified: Azure DevOps REST 7.1 with a limited write set, the GitHub contents API, Microsoft Graph v1.0. | UR-02, UR-19 | IF (§7) |

## 5. Detailed requirements

The detailed requirements are grouped by functional area. Each group heading names its prefix and the use cases it serves; the system requirement it refines is the one that names the prefix in §4.

### 5.1 Navigation and layout (NAV) — UC-01

*Group rationale: the user asked for a compact header with refresh and data notices in one place, and a sidebar grouped by purpose.*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NAV-01 | The app shall show a sidebar with the groups, in this order: "Dashboard", "My Work", "Azure DevOps", "Data & governance". | D | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-02 | The app shall list "Dashboard" under the group "Dashboard". | D | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-03 | The app shall list "My Tasks [Board]", "Bulk actions" and "Monthly Time Writing", in that order, under the group "My Work". | D | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-04 | *(Retired — see NAV-17, SET-14)* The app shall list "Baselines", "Snapshots", "Settings" and "Documentation", in that order, under the group "Data & governance". | D | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-05 | The app shall list one entry under "Azure DevOps" for each view returned by the view rules in CON-10 to CON-12. | T | M | UC-01, UC-02 |
| NAV-06 | When no Azure DevOps view exists, the app shall hide the "Azure DevOps" group. | D | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-07 | The app shall show Azure DevOps views as separate sidebar entries, not as tabs within one page. | D | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-08 | The header shall contain only: the page title, the page's Team/Individual switch (Dashboard and Monthly Time Writing), the data-status chip with its refresh button (FET-10), the month picker on pages that use a month, and the theme toggle. | I | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-09 | The header shall show the month picker on Pulse Check, Monthly Time Writing, Baselines, Windchill export pages (CON-73), Snapshots and Settings. It shall hide the picker on My Tasks [Board], the Azure DevOps views and Documentation. | D | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-10 | The month picker shall accept only values of the form `YYYY-MM`. | T | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-11 | The month picker shall default to the current month in UTC (NAV-51), the same month for every viewer. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-12 | Every date and time that tells when data was fetched, saved or built (view bars, connection list, header status tooltip, build details, Git commit messages) shall be shown in the viewer's own time zone with the zone named (e.g. "20 Oct 2026, 21:05 CEST"), while "today" (overdue, changed in 7 days, plan checks) and the month of Time Writing are business boundaries in UTC (NAV-51). | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-35 | The page title's tooltip shall carry the page description from the page registry. Pages shall not repeat a page header inside the content. | I | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-13 | The app shall show the data notices (FET-12 to FET-16) once, directly below the header, on every page. | D | M | UC-01, UC-03 |
| NAV-14 | The sidebar footer shall show the storage label, exactly one of: "⚠ Not saved locally", "◌ Saving locally", "○ Local IndexedDB". | D | S | UC-15 |
| NAV-15 | The footer (NAV-49) shall show the app version and build as "v{x.y.z} (Build {n})"; the build ID, the build time and the source revision are in the Force Update window. | D | M | UC-16 |
| NAV-16 | A modal dialog shall render above all page content, with nothing from the page drawn over it. Page scrolling behind it shall be blocked while it is open. | D | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-17 | The sidebar shall group pages as Data Insights (My dashboard, Team overview, Pulse Check, PI Review), My Work (My Tasks [Board], Monthly Time Writing, Bulk actions), I&D Board (the Time Writing backlog, Backlog Health), Release Details (BRITE at its root, CON-84, then two lists: the default Azure DevOps streams, the default Windchill streams S&S IT and ECP), Other views (NAV-32), On-Demand (each On-Demand page by its name, OND-01), the groups added with the page builder (PGB-04), Data & governance and Settings (one entry per Settings page, SET-14). Each heading and each Release Details list shall collapse and expand on click, remembered in this browser; a collapsed heading that holds the open page shall be highlighted; the narrow sidebar shall still show every page. Access roles shall treat Pulse Check and PI Review as part of the Data Insights area. | T | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-18 | PI Review (planned) shall show a PI summary — Features and Stories planned vs. closed, hours planned vs. booked per stream and person, open work in the PI's Sprints — and further details still to be defined; until then the page says it is planned. | D | C | UC-01 |
| NAV-19 | *(Retired — see QEM-01…QEM-07)* Data & governance shall hold a QE Mapping page (planned); until its content is defined it says so. Access roles shall treat it as its own area (default: Admin, Product Owner, People Manager). | I | C | UC-23 |
| NAV-20 | Pulse Check shall have four tabs, remembered in this browser: **Releases** (the month before the selected one) and **Current month releases** (the selected month), each showing exactly the pages under Release Details — one quadrant per Azure DevOps Server view in the menu (also when not fetched yet or without releases in its paths; release connections outside Release Details are left out) and the Windchill exports with row count, import time and a link; **Last month** — utilization for the previous month (utilization metrics and Who has capacity) with the same Team / Individual choice, releases per view compared with the selected month, Features and User Stories created and closed per stream, and its Time Writing gaps; **Ongoing activities** — Features and User Stories in an Active, In Progress, Committed or Doing state per stream (absence Epic excluded), with assignee, iteration, Target Date (flagged when passed) and the hours booked on them in the selected month, filterable by stream. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-22 | The app shall know the default release streams, in this order: DP, CP, IOCC, Content and Tool Validations (each from the Azure DevOps connection whose name or view name is, or starts with as whole words, the stream's name, ignoring case and punctuation — e.g. "DP [Completed Releases]") and S&S IT (from the Windchill export named like it; ECP to follow as a Windchill export). Release Details and Pulse Check shall show exactly these, in this order, also before they are set up (a stream without a connection leads to Settings, without an export to the import page); other connections and exports are not listed under Release Details. Settings → Azure DevOps → Default connections shall list the Time Writing connection (QMS-0025, pre-filled with the team's organization, project and team; PAT and creator email left to each user) and these streams, each with its status and a Set up that pre-fills the connection: for DP, CP, IOCC and Content the team's completed-release views (on-prem DHPCollection / DHP.Vault with their queries), otherwise the name, view name, role and Show in menu. The connection presets shall also offer QMS-0025 and the DHP release register (Pedestal); a preset in a shared project disappears once a connection with the same query or name is saved. No PAT or personal email is pre-filled. | T | S | UC-08 |
| NAV-23 | DP releases shall take the release name from the Area Path (last segment) and the number from the Iteration Path (a "Release x.y.z" segment, else a segment holding a version). IOCC releases shall take their Type of Release from the Area Path segment right after "IOCC" — shown in its own column — and the release name from the document Title (a version in it is the number). For DP and IOCC the QMS-0025 Feature shall be found by a fuzzy match with a percentage (name and Type of Release 60%, version 40%: exact 1, same major.minor 0.6, same major 0.3; the version counts only when the name scores at least 50%, and numbers, one- or two-letter words and common words such as Project, Release or Notes never make a name match; below 50% no closest Feature is shown); the best match and its percentage are shown and it counts as the release's Feature — with its planned and booked hours — at or above a threshold set in Settings (default 80%). Content keeps the general path rule and exact match until its rule is defined; Tool Validations follows QV-34 and CP QV-41…QV-45. | T | S | UC-08 |
| NAV-24 | Settings → Azure DevOps shall be organised in three steps: (1) Servers and PATs — one PAT per server (an on-prem collection base URL or a cloud organization), entered once, used by every connection on that server, kept only in this browser or desktop app, with Save, Replace, Remove and Fetch all on the server; a PAT typed on a connection becomes its server's PAT; Add server adds a server (server URL, organization / collection, PAT); a new or edited connection shall choose its server from this list only (no free server address), with the server's projects offered; (2) Stream mapping — Time Writing and the default streams with their server · project, query, release rule and status, Set up from the team's defaults (saved and fetched right away when the server has a PAT) and Set up all with saved PATs; the mapping is shared through Git and edited only by roles with Manage shared settings, whose changes are written to Git at once; (3) Other views — every other connection and default (e.g. Pedestal). The team's defaults keep the IDs of the shared setup, so Set up and the setup from Git give the same connection. Connections reading the same server, project, role and query are duplicates: at start only one is kept (the one with the shared ID), the others are removed with their data here and, with a Git token that can save, in Git; a duplicate in Git or in the saved on-prem data is not added again. | T | S | UC-08 |
| NAV-25 | *(Retired — see QV-22, QV-24, QV-25 and OI-14)* DP shall combine two data sets mapped in Settings → Stream mapping: the Pedestal release register (every Release work item is a DP release; its Title names the release and version) and the DHFI documents. A DHFI document belongs to a release when its Title names the release (ignoring spaces and punctuation inside words) and its exact version; the latest Author Approval Date (else Closed Date) of those documents dates the release and places it in a month. Releases without a dated DHFI document are listed apart as "No DHFI yet". Each row shows the Pedestal Release (State, Release Intent, PI / Year), its design reviews (DRPE, DRPC, DRfH, DDT date and status) and the QMS-0025 Feature match. Pulse Check uses the same releases for DP. | T | S | UC-08 |
| NAV-26 | The fuzzy Feature match shall ignore spaces and punctuation inside words ("RSGMS" = "RS GMS" = "RS-GMS"), shall never score a Feature of another version at or above the threshold, shall prefer a Feature without extra words ("Dose Planning" over "Dose Planning Lite"), and shall not match a Feature lacking a word of the release name. | T | S | UC-08 |
| NAV-27 | Changes to work items shall be sent to Azure DevOps Services (dev.azure.com) only: the web app and the desktop proxy shall refuse create and update operations whose collection is on any other host, and a Time Writing (read & write) connection shall not be saved on an on-premises server. | T | S | UC-05 |
| NAV-28 | The PAT shall be held once per server. A connection shall keep no copy of its server's PAT (also when restored from the shared setup in Git), so removing or replacing the server's PAT shall take effect for every connection on it, and for pending writes, at once. | T | S | UC-05 |
| NAV-29 | Git sync: a connection removed in Git shall be removed here together with its fetched view data; on-prem view data shall be saved to Git only after the token's write check has answered (a read-only token saves nothing and reports it once), a save shall be retried only when it lost a race with another commit; a snapshot file or backup whose view data holds invalid work items or an unreadable fetch time shall be refused, and a snapshot without a readable fetch time shall never replace data. | T | S | UC-08 |
| NAV-30 | Duplicate connections (same server, project, role and query) shall be removed at start, keeping the copy whose ID is in the shared setup in Git, else a preset's ID, else the first saved. Saving a new or edited connection that would duplicate a saved one (Time Writing aside) shall be refused with the name of the existing connection. | T | S | UC-08 |
| NAV-32 | Every Azure DevOps view marked "Show in menu" that is neither the Time Writing backlog nor one of the default streams (DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations), from an on-prem server or the cloud, and every imported Windchill export that is not a default Windchill stream (S&S IT), shall be listed under a menu heading "Other views", after Release Details. The heading shall be hidden when it has no entry; each entry shall follow the access area of its kind (on-prem and Windchill: Release Details; cloud: I&D Board). | T | S | UC-08 |
| NAV-31 | The app shall be usable on phones and tablets: up to 760px wide the navigation shall be an off-canvas menu opened from a ☰ button in the top bar (closing on a choice, Escape or the backdrop), the content shall take the full width without horizontal page scrolling, filters, cards and forms shall stack, wide tables shall scroll inside their panel and inputs shall be at least 16px; from 761 to 1000px the sidebar shall be an icon rail with the active page labelled. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-33 | A first-time user shall get a "Get started" pop-up listing the setup steps in order, each with its live status (Done, To do, After the step above), why it matters, how to do it and a button that opens the right Settings tab: four required steps — connect the shared Git storage, check the Time Writing connection and the work email, enter the Azure DevOps PAT, fetch the data (with a Fetch now button) — and two optional ones — region and role (Settings → Resources for those who manage shared settings, otherwise ask the People Manager or Scrum Master) and the release streams on the on-premises server, counting DP's Pedestal and DHFI data sets as well ("x of y ready; missing: …", Done only when all are set up). It shall open by itself once per visit while a required step is open, unless "Don't show again automatically" was chosen in this browser; while a required step is open the top bar shall show "Setup · done/4", which reopens it. With sample data the fetch step shall say that fetching replaces the sample. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-34 | In Settings the add and edit forms shall open as pop-up windows over the page instead of inline: the Azure DevOps connection editor (add, edit, clone, set up a default) and Add server; the Git storage connection; the capacity and absence settings; mapping an Epic or Feature as holidays or a common activity (with Edit per mapping); and the Feature setup of an Epic (View setup when the token cannot save). The page shall keep the lists and a summary of the saved values with the button that opens the editor. Every pop-up form shall be one column: per field its title, the input below it and its explanation below that, with Cancel and the main action at the bottom right, in the same order in every pop-up. A click beside a form pop-up shall not close it (× and Cancel do); closing the Feature setup editor with unsaved changes shall ask first, and a successful save shall close it. The tables with inline pickers (Resources, Access roles) and one-field Add boxes stay on the page. | T | S | UC-02, UC-14 |
| NAV-36 | Long Azure DevOps writes shall run in the background instead of holding the screen: New Feature / Complete Feature children (the form closes on Save once its fields are valid), Bulk actions → Move to current PI + Sprint and Create month Time Writing tasks, Monthly Time Writing → Save, and ＋ Time Writing Task → Add n months (BKL-37). The top bar shall show the jobs of this page load — "n running" with each job's progress, then its result (done, or what failed and what was created) — with Clear finished; the results of the Release pages' actions are entries of the same list (QV-63). The same job shall not start twice while it runs (its button waits); leaving or reloading the page while a job runs shall ask first. A failed Monthly save shall keep the unsaved Tasks staged. Nothing about jobs is stored. | T | S | UC-07 |
| NAV-37 | The sidebar shall offer **Expand all** and **Collapse all** above the menu: they open or close every heading and both Release Details lists at once (pinned pages stay visible), remembered in this browser like a single heading (NAV-17); each is disabled when it has nothing to do. The icon rail (761–1000px) has no headings and shall not show them. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-38 | The Get started pop-up (NAV-33) shall let the user enter the values a step needs **in the pop-up itself**, each with a placeholder and its own button, besides the button that opens the settings: the **GitHub token** (Connect & load, to the team's data repository prefilled as in GIT-13, remembered in this browser), **Your work email** (Save email; shown while no email is set; checked to look like an email; marked as an email field so the browser can offer the address it has saved — nothing is read without the user choosing it; CON-82) and the **Azure DevOps PAT** of the Time Writing connection (Save PAT; disabled until that step can be done). Each value is saved exactly as its Settings page saves it — tokens only in this browser, never in Git — and the step's status updates; a refusal is shown in the step. | T | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-39 | The Get started required steps shall be, in order: (1) Connect the shared Git storage; (2) Check the Time Writing connection and enter your Azure DevOps token (PAT); (3) **Your work email**, after the token; (4) Fetch your data. The email step shall offer **Detect from my token**: the app reads, with the user's own PAT and read-only queries (WIQL `@Me`), the most recent work item assigned to, else created by, else changed by the token's owner and takes the email of that identity as Azure DevOps stores it (uniqueName, or the address in "Name <email>"); the found email fills the field with where it came from, and is saved only when the user presses Save email. With no such work item it says so and the email is typed. The PAT's identity service (connectionData) is not used (CON-41). | T | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-40 | The Get started pop-up shall stay short: each step shows only its number, a short title (Connect Git storage, Azure DevOps token (PAT) [QMS-0025], Your work email, Fetch data; optional: Region and role, **TFS APAC settings [DP/CP/IOCC/Content]**), its status, the field it needs and its buttons; why and how are in the step's **Info** (shown on hover or click). The token steps shall link to where the token is created — **Create a GitHub token**, **Create an Azure DevOps PAT**, **Create a TFS APAC PAT** — defaulting to GitHub's fine-grained token page and the Time Writing organization's Azure DevOps token page; a user with "Manage shared settings" can set the three links under **Token links (admin)** in the pop-up (https only; empty = default), saved with the planning data in Git (`setupLinks`) for everyone. | T | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-41 | When the Time Writing data cannot be fetched — at start or on any later fetch: no token in this browser, a wrong or expired token (401/403), no connection to Azure DevOps, or any other failure — the Get started pop-up shall open by itself (also after "Don't show again automatically"; once per failure) with "Could not fetch from Azure DevOps." and what to do (token problems: create a new token and Save PAT in step 2; no connection: check network or VPN), the error under Details, the token step open again for token problems and the Fetch step open again for every problem. The next successful fetch clears it. | T | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-42 | The app shall open on **Home**, the first entry of the menu (above the groups, open to every role, without a month picker): a page of **tiles**, one per area — My Work, My dashboard, Team Insights, I&D Board, Release Details, Planning & Baselines, QE Mapping, On-Demand (OND-01), Snapshots & Documents, Admin, My settings — each in a soft colour of its own (light and dark mode), with its name and what it holds. View as (CON-89) also starts on Home. | T | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-43 | Each Home tile shall open its area's main page (its first page the person may open; Admin its Overview, My settings its own) and carry **links straight to the area's pages** — e.g. Release Details: DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations, the Windchill exports and BRITE, a stream not set up yet marked and leading to its setup — at most six shown, **+n more** showing the rest in the tile. Tiles and links follow the person's roles: a page they may not open is not offered, and an area without any is left out. My Work shows how many open items the person has. | T | M | UC-01 |
| NAV-44 | Home shall offer a **search** of the tiles and links: a tile whose name or description has every word typed is shown with all its links; otherwise only its links whose name has every word stay, and a tile without any is hidden; nothing found says so. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-45 | **My page usage** (My settings) shall, only after the person turns it on, count how often they open each page, in this browser's local storage only — never sent anywhere, shared through Git, or put in a backup — and list the counted pages with their menu labels, most used first, with the date counting started and **Start again from zero**; it shall be off by default, and turning it off shall delete the counts. Malformed or unknown entries are ignored. | T | C | UC-01 |
| NAV-46 | Each person shall be able to **switch between the tile view and the classic view** with **Classic view / Tile view** in the profile menu (NAV-48): the **tile view** (the default) opens on Home (NAV-42) with Home first in the menu; the **classic view** opens on **My dashboard** and has no Home in the menu, as before Home. Switching opens that view's start page at once. The choice is kept in this browser only (not shared through Git); storage that cannot be written keeps it for the page load. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-47 | The **Person** picker shall sit in the top bar on the person-based pages only — Time Writing, My Tasks [Board] and Sprint check — in place of their own pickers (Show work for, Activities of): Me (your work email) or anyone assigned work, kept in this browser. **My dashboard** shall not offer it and always shows you (MYD-01). | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-48 | A **profile** button (the person's initial) shall sit at the top right of every page; it opens a menu with the person's name and work email (or how to set it), **My settings**, **Classic view / Tile view** (NAV-46), **View as…** (for roles that may), **Search settings** and **Customize menu**. It closes on a choice, Escape or a click outside. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-49 | A **footer** shall end every page with **only the version and build** (NAV-15), the Check for updates / Force Update button, the **desktop app download** and, in the desktop app, zoom; where data is saved is in the Force Update window — **sticky** at the bottom of the window. It shall take its own place after the page content, so no data stays hidden under it or overlaps it: at the end of a page the content ends where the footer starts; the bars fixed at the bottom (Unsaved changes, the update message) sit above it, measured to its real height. Every page shall keep the full width of the page area and start at the top, whatever its content — e.g. every My settings section has the same width (KB-61). On phones it is not sticky. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-50 | Every page shall fill the width beside the menu — with no maximum width, so it widens when the menu is folded (UI-16) — inside a thin **border**, with a margin between the page and the window on each side set separately: **top, right, bottom and left**, 0 to 64 px, default **10 / 20 / 10 / 20 px**. The margins and whether the border shows are set in **Look & feel → Page layout** (SET-18), apply at once and are kept on this computer; **Back to 10 / 20 / 10 / 20** restores the defaults. | T | S | UC-01 |
| NAV-51 | **Business time is UTC** (owner decision 1, design defaults review): UTC shall decide (a) which **month** a date or timestamp belongs to wherever the app groups, filters or counts by month — the header month's default (NAV-11), the month filters and counts of the read-only views, Release Details, Pulse Check, the Dashboard, Capacity, Insights, Windchill exports and closures in a month; (b) the **month of new month Time Writing Tasks** (titles and dates) and the "later months" of a closing Feature (BKL-43); (c) which **Sprint / PI is current** and which Sprint is past (ATM-08); and (d) the **lock moment** of a month's plan and actuals (MON-06, MON-07: the lock day ends at midnight UTC), and "today" for overdue and plan checks. A plain date (YYYY-MM-DD) keeps its own calendar date and month and never shifts by a day. Times shown to the user (fetched at, saved at, history, NAV-12) stay in the viewer's own time zone. | T | M | UC-01, UC-04, UC-08 |
| OV-11 | Dashboard shall be a dashboard over a period — Year, Quarter and Month (OV-15) — with multi-select filters Region, Role, Team member and Stream after them in the same row (none ticked = all; remembered per person, MYD-08; Reset filters). Its header shall show planned and actual utilization, elapsed months recorded vs planned, the current month's projection vs plan, planned overload and unplanned capacity (capacity − plan), and say in one line which months are not finished. It shall be named **Team overview** and offer the tabs of MYD-11. | T | S | UC-01 |
| OV-12 | Rebalancing on the Dashboard shall move overload only to headroom of the same role in the same month (current or future months). People without a role shall be left out of the relief-need and receiver lists and counted apart, with their overload and headroom hours, so the dashboard says how many were left out; gross overload and headroom shall still count everyone. | T | S | UC-01 |
| OV-13 | *(Retired — see MYD-11)* The Overview page shall be named Dashboard and carry an Insights tab: a health score (balance, recording, resilience); a month-end forecast from the run rate (on track within ±10% of plan; heading over capacity); plan accuracy per person over elapsed months (error, bias, verdicts reliable / under-records / over-runs / uneven); stream momentum (latest up to 3 elapsed months against the 3 before, ±20% and 8 h); resilience (streams with one person at ≥ 80% of hours, specialists at ≥ 90% in one stream); unusual months (≥ 50% and 8 h from the person's median, 3 months needed); and releases and documents per stream and month of the period, as Pulse Check counts them. Every rule is stated in the tab's Info texts and Definitions. | T | S | UC-01 |
| OV-14 | The Dashboard shall use one utilization model everywhere, from Settings → Capacity: strictly above the overloaded threshold = ↑ red (overloaded), below the available threshold = ↓ blue (spare), green in between (balanced). Planned overload, persistent overload, the forecast's "heading over capacity" and the health score's balance component shall use the same thresholds. Differences in hours (+ / −) shall carry no colour; colour is reserved for utilization against capacity. Each tab shall show an empty state when the selection has no planned or recorded hours. | T | S | UC-01 |
| OV-15 | Team overview's period shall be chosen with three **multi-select drop-downs — Year, Quarter, Month —** first in the one filter row, before Region, Role, Team member and Stream (no second period row), as the hierarchy of OV-16. **Year** offers the years in the Time Writing data, this year and the header month's year; **Quarter** and **Month** follow it (OV-16). It starts on the header month's year and quarter; **Reset filters** returns the period to that as well as the other filters, and is disabled while nothing differs from it. A period saved in the earlier format (one year with quarters and months added up) still loads with the same months (MYD-08). The scope line names one month, "yyyy (whole year)" for one whole year, the chosen years with their quarters or months for several years, else the months. | T | S | UC-09 |
| OV-16 | Team overview's Year, Quarter and Month shall be **one hierarchy**: a quarter belongs to its year and a month to its quarter. **Year** — nothing ticked = the **current year** (UTC, NAV-51). **Quarter** offers the four quarters of each year that counts (labelled Q1…Q4, or "Q1 2026" when several years count); nothing ticked = every quarter of those years. **Month** offers the months of the quarters that count (Jan…Dec, or "Jan 2026" with several years); nothing ticked = every month of those quarters. The period is the months that count. Changing a level drops the choices below it that no longer belong to it. A period saved by an earlier version still loads with the same months. | T | S | UC-09 |

### 5.2 Azure DevOps connections and views (CON) — UC-02

*Group rationale: several Azure DevOps sources differ by query or by base URL. Only some of them are views; the rest serve as backend data.*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CON-01 | The app shall show the Azure DevOps connections as the first section of Settings, with anchor `settings-connections`. | D | M | UC-02 |
| CON-02 | The connection form shall provide these fields: "Name" (required), "View name (optional)" (max 80 characters), "Show in menu", "Used for", "Server URL (on-prem only)", "Organization / collection" (required), "Project" (required), "API version (optional)", "PAT", and "Query (optional, WIQL)". It shall also show "Team" for the time-tracking and backlog-view roles, and "Your work email (required)" for the time-tracking role (see CON-60). | D | M | UC-02 |
| CON-03 | When the role in the form changes, the form shall set "Show in menu" to on for the time-tracking and backlog-view roles, and to off for the release-source, documents and reference roles. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-04 | The app shall reject a save without a name, with "Give the connection a name, e.g. "Time Writing" or "DHFI releases"." | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-05 | The app shall reject a time-tracking connection that has neither a team nor a query, with "A Time Writing connection needs a team (its Epic backlog is read) or a query." The equivalent rule applies to a backlog-view, with "A backlog view needs a team (its Epic backlog is shown) or a query." | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-06 | The app shall reject an API version that does not match `NN.N` with an optional `-preview[.N]` suffix, with "API version looks like 7.1, 6.0 or 5.0." | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-07 | The app shall reject a new connection without a PAT, with "Enter the PAT for this connection." When an existing connection is edited and the PAT field is left empty, the saved PAT shall be kept. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-08 | The app shall accept only https base URLs without credentials. Without a server URL, the base shall be `https://dev.azure.com/{organization}`. With a server URL, the base shall be the server URL followed by the collection. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-09 | The button "Save and refresh data" shall save the connection and then fetch it immediately (FET-01). | D | M | UC-02, UC-03 |
| CON-10 | The view list shall contain the active Time Writing view when no time-tracking connection exists, or when the active one has Show in menu on. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-11 | The Time Writing view shall be named by its connection's View name, otherwise "QMS-0025". | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-12 | The view list shall contain, after the Time Writing view, every other connection with Show in menu on. Each is read only and named by its View name, otherwise its connection name. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-13 | The connection table shall show, for each connection, "In menu as "{view name}"" or "Hidden from menu (backend only)". | D | S | UC-02 |
| CON-14 | The connection table shall provide the actions Fetch, Test, Edit, Clone and Remove for each connection. | D | M | UC-02 |
| CON-15 | Remove shall ask "Remove "{name}" from this browser? Its PAT is not revoked in Azure DevOps." before deleting the connection. | D | M | UC-02 |
| CON-16 | Test shall run a read-only WIQL query that returns no items. On success it shall report "Connection works: {project} is reachable with this PAT." | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-17 | The app shall offer the built-in presets (QMS-0025 as Time Writing, view name "QMS-0025", in menu; DHP on-prem as reference, hidden from menu) until each is set up or dismissed. Dismissals shall be remembered in this browser. | D | C | UC-02 |
| CON-18 | The app shall store connections and their PATs only in localStorage key `demand-planning-azure-connection-profiles-v1`, never in the app state. | I, T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-19 | The app shall mark a connection with a base URL as "On-prem" in the connection table. | D | C | UC-02 |
| CON-20 | "Clone" shall open the connection form as a new connection with every setting of the chosen one (name and view name suffixed "(copy)"). Saving it without a PAT shall use the chosen connection's saved PAT. The chosen connection shall not change. | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-21 | Settings shall have a "Windchill host" section: host (https only, no user name or password; a pasted page address is reduced to its origin), service path (under /Windchill/, default /Windchill/servlet/odata), login (Windows single sign-on), object type, search parameters and columns to return. It shall be saved in this browser only, with no password stored. **Hidden since build 144 (CON-33): the Windchill host and views setup is no longer shown; the code is kept.** | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-22 | In the desktop app, "Test connection" shall make one read-only GET to the saved host's service path, offering Windows single sign-on to that host only, and report: connected with the listed service names, sign-on refused (401/403), redirected to a sign-in page on another host (redirects on the same host are followed, as in a browser), another status, not JSON, or not reachable. In the web version the test shall be disabled with a note that it runs in the desktop app. **Hidden since build 144 (CON-33): the Windchill host and views setup is no longer shown; the code is kept.** | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-23 | Settings → "Views in Git" shall save every Azure DevOps connection (ID, name, view name, role, shown in menu, project URL, on-prem server, API version, creator email, query) — never a PAT — as azure-views.json in the Git data repository, next to the planning data file, as one commit with a description; an unchanged file shall not be committed again. Saving shall be disabled with sample data. | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-24 | "Restore from Git" shall, after confirmation, add or overwrite the connections in the file by ID and leave other connections unchanged. | CON-25 | With Git storage connected (a token that can write), every fresh, successful fetch of an on-prem read-only connection (a server address; not Time Writing) — in the desktop app or the browser — shall be committed automatically to azure-data/<connection ID>.json in the Git data repository: the connection settings (no PAT) and the fetched snapshot. A fetch whose data is unchanged shall still be saved (its fetch time is new; the commit message says "data unchanged"). Saves run one after another and retry once after a conflicting commit. The view shall show whether it was saved, or not saved and why. Nothing shall be committed with sample data. | T | S | UC-08 |
| CON-26 | The web app shall load the on-prem view snapshots from Git once at start (Git storage connected) and from "Load on-prem data from Git": a view's data shall be replaced only by a newer fetch, connections not in this browser shall be added without a PAT (view only), and the view shall say its data came from the desktop app via Git. A connection without a PAT shall not fetch and shall say why. | T | S | UC-08 |
| CON-27 | Settings → "Windchill views" shall define searches without query syntax: a name, an object type (Domain/Set, e.g. DocMgmt/Documents), filter rows (field, condition — equals, is not, starts with, contains, after/at least, before/at most — and value; dates as YYYY-MM-DD) and the columns to show, saved in this browser; field names and the object type are validated and values escaped, and the app builds one OData query from them. Views marked "Show in the menu" appear under Windchill in the sidebar. **Hidden since build 144 (CON-33): the Windchill host and views setup is no longer shown; the code is kept.** | T | S | UC-08 |
| CON-28 | In the desktop app, Fetch shall run a Windchill view read-only (GET under the configured service root, Windows sign-on to that host only, next pages followed on the same host up to 20,000 results) and store the results with the app data; with Git storage connected they shall be committed to windchill-data/<view>.json (the view and results, no credentials) when changed. The web app shall load these at start, add views it does not have, and show the results with "(desktop app, via Git)". A view page shows the chosen columns with search and CSV export. **Hidden since build 144 (CON-33): the Windchill host and views setup is no longer shown; the code is kept.** | T | S | UC-08 |
| CON-29 | When Windchill answers with a login page, the desktop app shall offer "Sign in": a sandboxed window (no app access, https only, no pop-ups) opens the service address in a separate persistent Windchill session where the user completes the company login; after each page on the Windchill host the connection is tested, and the window closes once the service answers. Test connection and Fetch shall use that session; the app shall never read or store a password, and "Sign out" shall clear the session. A Fetch that gets a login page shall say to sign in. **Hidden since build 144 (CON-33): the Windchill host and views setup is no longer shown; the code is kept.** | T | S | UC-08 |
| CON-30 | Each Windchill view shall offer "Open in browser" (the view's search address, opened in the default browser where the company sign-in works), "Import file" (a page saved from the browser) and "Paste results" (copied text), in the web and desktop app. An import shall accept only a Windchill result list ({"value": [...]}), say so for a web page, the domain list or a Windchill error, replace the view's results, and be stored and saved to Git like a Fetch. When the result has a next-page link on the same https service, the view shall offer "Open next page" and "Import next page"/"Paste next page", which add to the results (at most 20,000). **Hidden since build 144 (CON-33): the Windchill host and views setup is no longer shown; the code is kept.** | T | S | UC-08 |
| CON-31 | In the web app, Test connection and a Windchill view's Fetch shall run from the browser itself (read-only GETs with the browser's own Windchill sign-in sent along, following next pages on the same service, at most 20,000 results), as the Azure DevOps cloud views do. When the browser cannot read the answer (Windchill does not allow the app's address, or no sign-in), the app shall say to sign in to Windchill in another tab or ask the Windchill administrator to allow the app's address, and point to Open in browser and Import. **Hidden since build 144 (CON-33): the Windchill host and views setup is no longer shown; the code is kept.** | T | S | UC-08 |
| CON-32 | In the web app, Settings → Windchill host shall offer a "Windchill → app" bookmark (drag to favourites, or copy its code), and each Windchill view "Fetch via Windchill tab": the app opens the service address in a tab; the bookmark clicked there asks the app (its opener, the app's own origin only) for the searches, runs them read-only on Windchill (next pages on the same service, at most 20,000 results) and sends the results back. The app shall accept only messages from the Windchill origin, from the tab it opened, carrying that request's one-time code and only for the requested views; results are stored and saved to Git like a Fetch, and a request without an answer within 10 minutes shall be reported. **Hidden since build 144 (CON-33): the Windchill host and views setup is no longer shown; the code is kept.** | T | S | UC-08 |
| CON-33 | The sidebar shall list the cloud Azure DevOps views under "I&D Board" and the on-prem (Azure DevOps Server) views under "Release Details", followed there by the imported Windchill exports and "Import Windchill export". At start (web and desktop) the connection setup in azure-views.json shall be every user's default: connections missing here are added (PAT from a connection to the same server, else view only), connections not changed here since the last sync follow Git, and connections changed here are kept; nothing is deleted, and saving to or restoring from Git makes the connections follow Git again. | T | S | UC-02 |

"All shared setups" shall show it under "Missing" ("Default: …"), offer "Fill missing DoDs with the default" (n setups; all marked changed, saved with Save to Git) when the token can save, and pre-fill it when a row without one is edited; the setup pop-up shall pre-fill it on "Edit setup…" and offer "Use the default: …"; the New Feature form shall pre-fill it for an Epic whose setup has no DoD. "All fields…" shall explain itself on hover (the full editor with Descriptions, Assigned to and Area / Iteration Paths). | T | S | UC-07 |
| CON-68 | A PAT shall never follow a connection to another server: when the views synced from Git (at every start) or restored by hand move a connection's URL to a different host, the connection's own PAT is not carried over — the connection is restored without a PAT (reported under "no PAT"), the old server keeps its PAT and the new host gets none unless a PAT is entered for the restore or another connection already holds one for that server. | T | M | UC-01 |
| CON-69 | Every CSV export (query views, Backlog Health, QE Mapping, Windchill views, effort export) shall neutralise cells a spreadsheet would run as a formula: a value starting with =, @, a tab or a carriage return, or with + or − when it is not a plain number, is prefixed with an apostrophe before quoting, so Excel shows it as text. Numbers such as "-3" and "+1.5" stay as they are. The header row (column names) shall be neutralised and quoted in the same way as the cells. | T | M | UC-08 |
| CON-70 | The app shall not go blank on bad data: a numeric HTML / XML entity outside the Unicode range (e.g. &amp;#1114112;) stays as written instead of failing the render; a render error in a view shows an error page naming the failure with Reload and "Try to continue" instead of an empty screen; an .xlsx entry that unpacks to more than 100 MB is refused with a message; in the desktop app the main window only ever shows the bundled app (an outside link opens in the browser, redirects elsewhere are blocked) and the Windchill sign-in window blocks non-https redirects. A Windchill snapshot from Git whose rows are not plain objects shall have those rows dropped at parse time instead of failing the view. | T | M | UC-01, UC-08 |
| CON-71 | A Windchill export page shall list the export's **unique releases** read from the document names, which hold the release name, the release number and then the document name, with any delimiters, and may be **clubbed** over several releases (name + number pairs one after the other: "Ser 1 Rel1_Ser2 Rel2_Test Plan", "Viewer 1.6 & 1.7 SRS"). Every version in the name (two or more numbers joined by dots, optionally after v, ver, version, R, Rel or Release; a single number only after one of those words; a version in brackets such as "(v2)" is the document's revision and is skipped) ends a part whose name is the text since the previous version — a version with no name before it keeps the previous name — and the text after the last version is the document name; separators (space, -, _, \|, /, :, ;, comma, +, &, "and", brackets) are dropped. A clubbed name is **one release**, kept as written ("Ser 1 Rel1_Ser2 Rel2", number "1 + 2"), counted once; spacing and delimiter variants of the same clubbed text are the same release; the number of clubbed releases is shown. Each release (name ignoring case and spaces, number ignoring trailing zeros: 3.1 = 3.1.0) is listed once with its document count (expandable to the document names) and the latest date of a date column (e.g. Last Modified); the count reads "n releases · m of k documents"; the name column ("Name", else the first column naming a name) can be changed; documents without a release number in the name are listed apart; the releases export to CSV. Pulse Check's Windchill exports table shows the number of unique releases per stream. The releases shall be listed **newest first by the latest date** of the date column, the date read as the export writes it (ISO, 27-Oct-2026, 27 Oct 2026 09:15, Oct 27, 2026, 27.10.2026, 27/10/2026 or 10/27/2026 with an optional time; a part above 12 is the day, else month/day/year is assumed), releases without a readable date last, then by name. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| CON-72 | The release source of a Windchill export page shall be, by preference, the **Location** column (the document's folder path; "Location" if present, else the first column naming a location, folder or path): the releases are read from the deepest folder that holds a version — the name and number in one folder ("Remote Diagnostics 1.6"), a **clubbed** folder ("Ser 1 Rel1_Ser2 Rel2", "Viewer 1.6 & 1.7": one release as written, number "Rel1 + Rel2", counted once), or a version-only folder under the name folder ("Remote Diagnostics/1.7", "CareLink/R3.2"), the folders after it ignored. The release number read from a folder shall be kept **exactly as written** ("R3.2", "rel1", "1.6.0"); numbers written differently are different releases. The document names (CON-71) are the fallback when the export has no such column; "Read from" shall let the viewer choose Automatic, Location or Document name, and the column for each. From Location the page shall list the **unique locations** (each distinct value, its document count and the release read from it) and offer "Export locations CSV" and, with Git storage connected and writable, **"Save locations to Git"**, which writes `windchill-analysis/<export id>-locations.json` (schema 1, kind `windchill-locations`: export, column, generated time, document count, unique locations, unique releases with their folders; no document rows, no credentials) to the Git **data** repository for analysis of the folder naming. The count shall say which source was used. | T | M | UC-08, UC-12 |
| CON-73 | A Windchill export page shall show the header month picker, and its Releases section shall by default list only the releases with **a document dated in the chosen month** (the date column of any of the release's documents — a release counts in every month one of its documents is dated in, not only the month of its newest document), with the count "n of N releases with a document {date column} in {Month YYYY} · d documents"; "Show: All months" (the period control in the page's source bar, QV-62) lists every release. The list shall be sortable by every column (Release name, Release, Latest date, Documents, Locations; a click toggles the direction; newest / most first by default, name A→Z), with the sort shown on the heading, and its column widths shall be draggable (kept per export in this browser, "Reset widths"). The CSV export shall follow the shown list and order. | T | M | UC-08 |
| CON-74 | In Settings → Access, a member shall be added by choosing the person by **name** from a list of the people in the Time Writing backlog (QMS-0025: everyone Assigned To an item there, with the email Azure DevOps gives for them; same names told apart by their email); the app shall store that email. "Other (enter an email)…" shall still allow a person who is not in that backlog, and without the backlog loaded the email field shall be shown with a hint. The members table shall show the name first and the email under it, sorted by name. Since v6.0.0 a person is added with every mapping on People & access → People (CAP-49). | D | S | UC-12 |
| CON-75 | *(Retired — see EPS-07)* A Feature setup of an Epic shall optionally name up to 5 title fields — the parts of a Feature title for that Epic (e.g. DP: Program, Service, Release; CP: Platform, Release Name, Number; ECP: Project Code, Release Name, Release Number) — each with an optional on-prem field (reference or plain name, e.g. SourceProgram). Names are unique (letter case and spaces ignored) and not {feature} or {month}; blank or repeated names are dropped on save; a setup without fields is saved as before. Each field is a placeholder {Field name} (any letter case, spaces ignored) in the setup's titles. | T | S | UC-06 |
| CON-76 | The built-in default of the DP stream ("DP [Completed Releases]", same ID as in the shared setup) shall read the DHP Release work items — project DHP, type Release, not Removed, outside the Recycle Bin — with ID, SourceProgram, PsName, Release, Title, State, ReleaseIntent and the DDT, DRfH, DRPC and DRPE dates and statuses, newest DDT Date first, because the DP page filters by DDT Date and Create Feature reads SourceProgram, PsName and Release. The approved DHFI documents (DHP.Vault) shall be their own default, "DHFI [Approved]" (reference, hidden from the menu), which feeds DP's DHFI data set. A DP connection loaded from Git keeps the team's own query. | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-77 | On-prem view data from Git (at start in the web app, Load from Git, and the fallback of a fetch that cannot run) shall be applied only when it was fetched with the query the connection has here (ignoring spaces and letter case); data fetched with another query (e.g. by a desktop app still following an older shared setup) shall not replace the data here, and Load from Git shall say so and that the connection setup should be saved to Git. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-78 | A connection's query shall be any work-item query as pasted from Azure DevOps (Edit query → WIQL): a **flat list** (SELECT … FROM WorkItems WHERE …, paged by ID as before) or a **tree** or **direct-links** query (SELECT … FROM WorkItemLinks WHERE … MODE (Recursive \| MustContain \| MayContain \| DoesNotContain)). A tree or links query is sent read-only as written (with SELECT [System.Id]) in one call of up to 20,000 links, and every work item at either end of its links is read, each once in the order met (parents before their children); the view's columns and order come from its SELECT and ORDER BY. 20,000 links or more, a historical (ASOF) query or an unknown MODE is refused with a message; the desktop proxy accepts only SELECT [System.Id] FROM WorkItems or WorkItemLinks. | T | M | UC-02 |
| CON-79 | The **stream mapping** in the shared setup (`azure-views.json` in Git) — the connections of DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations and DP's data sets (server, project, query, view name, Show in menu) — shall be **enforced** for every user: at start (Git storage connected) a user's own change to one of these connections is replaced by the one in Git (the PAT stays), and a stream's connection from Git is used before one set up only on this computer. Time Writing stays personal and other views keep a local change, as before (CON-26). A user with **Manage shared settings** saves every change to the stream mapping (Save, Set up) to Git right away, overwriting the file ("Saved to the shared setup in Git; it applies to every user at their next start"), and has **Save stream mapping to Git** to write it again at any time; a change that cannot be saved (no Git storage, read-only token, error) is kept on that computer — not undone by the next start — until it is saved, and says so. Others see that the stream mapping is set by their admin and that their changes are replaced. | T | M | UC-01, UC-02 |
| CON-80 | The **work email** of a Time Writing connection is personal: it decides who "me" is (the access role, My Tasks [Board] / My Work, the assignee of new Features). Once per computer, a Time Writing connection that came from the shared setup with an email taken from the file shall lose it, so the app asks the user for their own work email (the start message says so); a connection set up only on that computer, and any email entered afterwards, are never touched. | T | M | UC-01, UC-17 |
| CON-81 | Every work item read from Azure DevOps (on-premises or dev.azure.com, read-only) shall keep the IDs of its **Successors** (link type `System.LinkTypes.Dependency-Forward`) and **Predecessors** (`System.LinkTypes.Dependency-Reverse`) from the links returned with it, each set once and in ascending order ("501, 502"); other links (Parent, Child, Related, attachments) are not dependencies. A view of its own — a connection that is neither a stream nor a stream's data set — shall show them as the last two columns of its query view, **Successor ID** and **Predecessor ID**, when any of its items has such a link: sortable, searchable, in the CSV and saved to Git with the view's data. Stream views (DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations) and their data sets are unchanged. | T | S | UC-09 |
| CON-82 | **Your work email** shall be a setting of its own on each computer — Settings → Azure DevOps → *Your work email* — and not part of any connection, the stream mapping or the shared setup in Git: it decides who "me" is (the access role, My Tasks [Board] / My Work, the Time Writing prompts, the assignee of new Features). Saving, editing or selecting a connection, a sync or restore from Git, and an app update (Force Update) shall not change it; the Time Writing connection form shall no longer ask for it. On the first start with this setting, the email kept on the Time Writing connection becomes the setting, but only once the CON-80 clean-up has run on that computer (so an email inherited from the shared setup is never taken over); otherwise the app asks for it (Get started, *Work email missing* in Stream mapping). | T | M | UC-01, UC-17 |
| CON-83 | **ECP** (formerly QRE) shall be a default Windchill stream after S&S IT (Release Details → Windchill exports; an export named like "ECP" — or still "QRE", read as ECP; one named ECP comes first), read like S&S IT except for what a release is: a Location reads "Parent folder/Project number Project name/Phase/…", and each release is a **project**: the **second folder** of the Location path split on "/" (leading "/" not counted), **whatever its name**, and every document anywhere under that folder belongs to it ("Projects/123456 Patient Monitor/Design" → 123456, Patient Monitor; "/Projects/PJ-1234 Name/DHF" → PJ-1234, Name; "Projects/Lab Automation/Design" → "Lab Automation"); the project number is the folder's first word when it holds a digit ("pj_77", "PJ 77" read as PJ-77), the rest is the name; without a Location, a document named "PJ-<number> <name>" names its project; a project is counted once with its documents, in every month one of its documents is dated in (CON-73), Pulse Check's Supported line and the Monthly pack list it as "<number> <name>", and its QMS-0025 match and Create Feature use the title "ECP \| <number> <name>" (never "v<number>"). | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-84 | **BRITE** shall have a place at the root of Release Details (above the Azure DevOps Server and Windchill lists) that opens a page saying it is not set up yet, until its exports and release rules are known; it counts nothing and is not part of Pulse Check or the Monthly pack. | D | C | UC-09 |
| CON-85 | A default Windchill stream not imported yet ("＋ ECP", "＋ S&S IT" under Release Details → Windchill exports) shall open the import **named after that stream** (Name prefilled "ECP", title "Import the ECP export"), so that the export becomes that stream's page — and Pulse Check's and the Monthly pack's — whatever the file is called; the plain "Import Windchill export" keeps the file name as default. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-86 | *(Retired — see QV-62)* On ECP's Windchill export page the release table (and its CSV) shall name its columns **Project No** and **Project Name** instead of Release and Release name, since an ECP release is a project (CON-83); S&S IT keeps Release name / Release. | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-87 | There may be **several ECP exports**.
