The logic and folder are saved with the export (this browser and its Git snapshot; unknown values dropped) and can be changed on its page. Every ECP export is listed **on its own** — its own entry under Release Details → Windchill exports (followed by "＋ ECP export" to add another), its own line in Pulse Check (Supported and the Windchill exports table) and in the Monthly pack, under its name — while Create Feature still titles its projects "ECP \| <number> <name>". An export without a setup keeps CON-83 (named ECP / QRE: ECP at the 2nd folder). | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-88 | **Back up and clean up** (Settings → Storage → Shared setup in Git, roles with "Manage shared settings") shall offer **Download backup** — every connection (name, view name, role, server, project, query; never a PAT or the work email) as a dated JSON file in the views-file format — and **Back up and remove all connections…**, which after a confirmation (1) downloads that backup and saves it to Git as a new dated file (`azure-views-backups/azure-views-<UTC time>.json`; queued, GIT-17 — two backups in the same second keep the connections of both, by connection id), stopping with nothing removed when that save fails; (2) writes a reset marker (`azure-views-reset.json`), empties the shared connection setup (`azure-views.json`) and removes every on-prem view's data in Git (`azure-data/`); (3) removes every connection here with its saved data. PATs shall stay per server (a PAT saved only on a connection is first kept for its server). At its next start every other computer with Git storage shall apply a reset marker it has not applied yet — remove all its connections (also ones it changed itself) with their data, once — before taking the connections now in Git; until then it shall not save its old on-prem view data to Git. The correct connections are then added again (Stream mapping, Other views). | T | M | UC-09 |
| CON-89 | People with the **Admin** role in the access policy shall have **View as…** (sidebar footer) to see the app as a chosen **person** or **role**; no one else has it, and without an access policy it is not offered. | T | S | UC-12 |
| CON-90 | Viewing as a **person** shall apply the roles of their work email in the access policy (the default role when they have none or no email is known) and show their own work: My dashboard, My Tasks [Board], Monthly Time Writing and Bulk actions as "me", and their personal views (sections, menu, filters). People come from the fetched Azure DevOps data (Assigned To, with the email when known) and the access policy's members. | T | S | UC-12 |
| CON-91 | Viewing as a **role** shall apply that role's areas and actions only; "me" stays the Admin. | T | S | UC-12 |
| CON-92 | View as shall be **read only** and for this page load only: a banner names who or which role is viewed with **Change** and **Exit View as**; while it is on, every write to Azure DevOps or to Git storage is refused with "Read only while viewing as …", before anything is sent; it is never stored, and a reload ends it. | T | S | UC-12 |
| CON-93 | The **stream mapping and its query logic** — which server, project and query feed DP, CP, IOCC, Content and Tool Validations, their data sets (Pedestal, DHFI, CP names), the shared release naming patterns, and saving the mapping to the shared setup in Git — shall be changed only by people with the action **Change the stream mapping** (`streams.manage`), held by the app access role **Tool Owner** (any number of members, set in Role rights; nobody hard-coded). Admins no longer hold it by default. While no member (and no default role) holds it, people who may manage access may change the mapping, so nobody is locked out. Only someone who may change the stream mapping may change who holds it, and a policy that has a Tool Owner must keep one. Everyone else sees the mapping read-only and may still add their own views and enter their PATs. Without access.json there are no restrictions, as before. | T | M | UC-22 |
| CON-94 | **Role rights as a permission matrix**: every menu area and every action is a permission of its own, in groups (Menu areas; Work items in Azure DevOps — create, assign to others, edit fields, change state / close Features, move to a Sprint or PI, enter hours, comment, bulk create; Release data — Windchill import, release counts, baselines, QE Mapping, export tables; Streams — the stream mapping (CON-93), stream options; Setup — the shared Feature setup; Capacity and time — capacity and absence, bucket rules and thresholds, holidays and common activities, period control and locking figures, historical hours; People and access — edit people, capacity roles, role rights, View as; Reports and sharing — Monthly pack text and setup, table / chart / page defaults, shared setup in Git, master data, audit, KPI tracker; On-Demand — set up the pages, edit their rows, CON-103). Each cell is **Allow**, **Deny** or **Not set** per role. A person gets the union of what their roles allow, less anything any of their roles denies (a denial wins); Not set is not allowed unless another role allows it. The app hides or disables each control without its permission, and refuses a change in Azure DevOps before anything is sent unless every permission it needs is allowed. The matrix can be searched, and **Check access** shows for a person and a permission whether it is allowed and which roles allow or deny it. An access.json written before the split keeps every person's rights: each new permission is allowed where the action it came from was (or for everyone, where the app had no check). Denials are saved in access.json. | T | M | UC-22 |
| CON-95 | The Feature setup shall work in **layers, field by field**: Built in → **All Epics** → the Epic's shared setup → Mine. For the **Feature DoD**, the **optional User Stories** and the **default Original Estimate** of the month Task, an Epic's setup (shared or mine) may set the field or **inherit** it; a new Feature under the Epic gets each from the nearest layer that sets it (Built in: no DoD — typed in New Feature — no optional stories, no estimate; DPF-09). **All Epics** (Admin → Feature setup, in feature-setup.json) holds the defaults; roles allowed "Edit the shared Feature setup" change it. An Epic without a setup of its own uses All Epics when All Epics has anything set (CON-97, v7.37.0), else nothing (DPF-09: no built-in milestone stories). A setup that inherits its Feature DoD does not need one of its own; Enable for everyone keeps the All Epics defaults; files without layers read as before (every field set). | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-96 | *(Retired — see CON-97)* The Feature setup editor of an Epic shall show, for each of those fields, whether it is **Set here** or **Inherited from** my setup / the Epic's shared setup / All Epics / Built in, show the inherited value read-only, and offer **Override here** (copies the inherited value to edit) and **Revert to inherited** (the tooltip names the value it falls back to); "Which setup applies" lists All Epics. | T | S | UC-02 |
| CON-97 | Admin → Epic & Feature setup shall edit an Epic's whole setup on **one screen (Setup per Epic)**, next to a searchable list of Epics — **All Epics (defaults)** first, then every Epic on the board or with a setup, each marked *Uses defaults* or *n of 4 customized* (and *my version*). For the chosen Epic: a **preview** of a new Feature under it (title from its fields and pattern, Description with the DoD and field lines, User Stories, month Task hours), then four sections in the order a Feature is built — **Fields & title** (the Epic setup's fields and title pattern, Copy to Epics), **Definition of Done**, **User Stories** (for the DP and IOCC bulk Epics in two tabs, Release and PU, with the QE Mapping columns) and **Month Task** (default estimate) — each with one **Customize for this Epic** switch: off shows what All Epics gives (or none), on edits the Epic's own value, starting from what applied. All Epics edits the defaults directly. **Use my own version (only me)** edits the person's own copy of the Epic (Fields stay shared), with *Share my version with everyone* and *Remove my own version*; *Reset this Epic to the defaults* removes the Epic's shared setup. **One Save to Git** writes the Epic setup and the Feature setup together (a customized DoD may not be empty); *Discard* drops every change. Value lists stay a section of their own. An Epic without a setup of its own uses All Epics when All Epics has anything set. Older links to the Feature setup, Epic setup or All Epic setups open Setup per Epic. The Epic list shall be at most 600 px tall (or the window's height), with its own scroll bar, the search box kept on top and the list kept in view while the setup scrolls (at most 320 px on a narrow screen). | T | M | UC-02, UC-34 |
| CON-98 | Saving the shared connection setup to Git (the stream mapping, Save to Git, Use as Time Writing, duplicate clean-up) shall **merge** this computer's connections into the setup in Git instead of replacing it: connections in Git that this computer does not have stay, this computer's version wins for the same connection, and only connections removed on purpose (the extra Time Writing connections, duplicates) leave Git. Personal views are never written. An admin's clean-up (CON-88) still empties the setup on purpose. | T | M | UC-08 |
| CON-99 | Settings → Streams shall offer **Removed connections**: **Look in Git history** reads the last 30 versions of the shared connection setup in Git (read only) and lists each connection that was in one of them but is neither in it now nor here (nor duplicates one here), with its query and when it was last there; **Restore** puts it back here (its server's PAT used; never a PAT from Git) and, for roles that may change the stream mapping with a Git token that can save, back into the shared setup for everyone; it is fetched when its server has a PAT. | T | M | UC-08 |
| CON-100 | A connection saved **without a role** (an older or hand-edited setup, also in the shared setup in Git) shall be **read-only** (Reference), never the role that writes; the one exception keeps an older setup working: on this computer, the first role-less connection on Azure DevOps Services that did not come from Git stays the Time Writing connection while no other connection here is one. | T | M | UC-12 |
| CON-101 | Access shall **fail closed**: until a roles file is loaded (access.json read from Git storage, or the copy cached in this browser from an earlier read), everyone shall be **read-only** — every menu area stays visible, but no action is allowed and changes to Azure DevOps are refused before sending — and every page shall show a notice saying why and what to do (connect Git storage). This holds while access.json is loading, when it cannot be read and when Git storage is not connected. **Bootstrap:** when Git storage is connected and the read confirmed that no access.json exists (the read succeeded and the file is absent), the viewer may only set up the first roles file ("Manage access", Admin → People & roles → Role rights → Set up roles), keeping Manage access for themselves (CON-44); nothing else is allowed until it is saved. | T | M | UC-22 |
| CON-103 | **On-Demand rights** (permissions 4): a menu area **On-Demand pages (CoNQ …)** and two permissions in their own group **On-Demand** of Role rights — **"Set up On-Demand pages (pages, columns, options)"** and **"Edit On-Demand data (rows)"** — each Allow, Deny or Not set per role (CON-94). Starting matrix: the area for every role, both permissions for Tool Owner and Admin. An access.json written before permissions 4 (any older version, with or without the split permissions) shall get them as the starting matrix does for **each role it lists** — the area for every listed role, both permissions for a listed Tool Owner or Admin — while a role missing from the file keeps no rights; saved again (permissions 4), a cell set to Not set stays so. Without a roles file the area is shown and neither permission is allowed (CON-101). | T | M | UC-22, UC-37 |
| CON-104 | A person shall be able to hold **several app access roles**: in **People & roles → People** each person's **App access roles** is a multi-select of the roles (nothing ticked = the default role); ticking or unticking one keeps the others, and the roles are saved to access.json in the matrix's order. Their rights are the union of the roles, a denial winning (CON-94). | T | M | UC-22 |
| CON-105 | Inside the write forms, actions that need a right of their own shall also be disabled with the reason (CON-102), not refused only when sending: in the **Edit** form the **Original Estimate** and **Completed Work** of a Task need "Enter planned and actual hours", and **Also move its open User Stories** (when a Feature's PI changes) needs "Move to another Sprint or PI"; in **＋ Time Writing Task** the hours fields need "Enter planned and actual hours" — without it the month Tasks can still be created, without hours, and a visible line says so. The `azureClient` guard stays the final safety net. | T | S | UC-22 |
| CON-106 | **Pages and menu groups** (permissions 5, PGB-08): a permission **"Manage pages and menu groups (Tool Owner)"** (`pages.manage`) in the **On-Demand** group of Role rights, Allow, Deny or Not set per role (CON-94). Starting matrix: **Tool Owner only**. An access.json written before permissions 5 shall get it as the starting matrix does — for a **listed Tool Owner only** (a file without the Tool Owner role gives that role every right, CON-93) — and saved again (permissions 5) a cell set to Not set stays so. While no one holds the Tool Owner role (no member and no default role with "Change the stream mapping", CON-93), the people allowed "Edit roles and members" hold it too, so nobody is locked out; without a roles file nobody does (CON-101). | T | M | UC-22, UC-38 |
| CON-102 | Every button that starts a change in Azure DevOps — create (＋ New Feature, ＋ Feature, ＋ User Story, ＋ Task, ＋ Time Writing Task, Complete children, Create Feature, ＋ Add a new activity and its Add / Save buttons), Edit, a state change or Close, Move (Sprint check), saving hours, Add comment, and bulk Create Features — shall be **disabled** when the viewer lacks the action it needs (CON-94), including the read-only state before the roles file is loaded (CON-101), so no form opens and no send is attempted; a **visible line** next to the button, or once above a table of row buttons (each also with the reason as tooltip), shall say why: "Read only: the access roles are not loaded yet, so nothing can be changed in Azure DevOps." or which permission the role lacks and where an Admin changes it. The write guard before sending stays as the last safety net; nothing changes for a viewer who holds the action. | T | M | UC-22 |

### 5.3 Fetching and saving Azure DevOps data (FET) — UC-03

*Group rationale: the user asked that "every time a Fetch happens, it should save automatically", and that Save means save and refresh.*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FET-01 | When a time-tracking connection is fetched without a query, the app shall read the team's Epic backlog: the team's area paths, every work item in them, and their parents. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-02 | When a time-tracking connection is fetched with a query, the app shall read the query's results and their parent chain. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-03 | The app shall read work items in batches of at most 200 IDs. If any batch fails, it shall import nothing from that fetch. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-04 | When a time-tracking fetch returns one or more items, the app shall replace the saved Azure work items with the result and write an audit entry with action "Sync". | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-05 | When a time-tracking fetch returns no items and saved items exist, the app shall keep the saved items and hold the result as a preview, with "Azure returned no work items, so the saved data was kept. Check the team or query; save the empty preview only if that is expected." | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-06 | "Save preview — replace Azure cache" shall save the preview only when its scope equals the current connection. Otherwise it shall show "The source changed after fetching. Refresh the selected backlog before saving." | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-07 | When a backlog-view, documents or reference connection is fetched, the app shall save its items under that connection's ID in `azureSources`, with the fetch time. | T | M | UC-03, UC-08 |
| FET-08 | When a release-source connection is fetched, the app shall save its items as `releaseSource`, with the fetch time. | T | M | UC-03, UC-08 |
| FET-09 | "Fetch all" shall fetch every connection, one after another. | D | M | UC-03 |
| FET-10 | The header refresh button (↻) shall run "Fetch all" when connections exist. Otherwise it shall refresh the active configuration. It shall be disabled while a fetch is running. | D | M | UC-01, UC-03 |
| FET-11 | The app shall refuse a second Time Writing fetch while one is running, with "A Time Writing fetch is already running." | T | S | UC-03 |
| FET-12 | The data-status chip shall show exactly one of: "Refreshing…", "Unsaved result", "Sample data", "{n} items · {relative time}", "No data". | D | M | UC-01 |
| FET-13 | When a preview exists, the app shall show the preview notice with the buttons "Save preview — replace Azure cache" and "Discard". | D | M | UC-03 |
| FET-14 | When no work items are saved and no fetch is running, the app shall show "No source data loaded yet." with a link to fetch and a "Load synthetic sample" button. | D | S | UC-03, UC-18 |
| FET-15 | When a fetch fails, the app shall show the error followed by "Previously loaded data has been retained." and keep the saved data unchanged. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-16 | When saved work items lack a verified scope for the active team backlog, the app shall hide them and show the scoped-refresh notice. | T | S | UC-03 |
| FET-17 | Fetching shall not write to Azure DevOps. | I, T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-18 | When a connection's query selects fields, the app shall store those field values per item, with identities reduced to their display name. | T | M | UC-08 |
| FET-19 | When a query is fetched, the app shall read Azure DevOps field names through the read-only `fields` operation. On failure it shall fall back to readable reference names. | T | S | UC-08 |
| FET-20 | At every start (opening the app, Force Update, reload), once the shared connection setup from Git is in place, the app shall fetch the active Time Writing connection and the read-only cloud (Azure DevOps Services) connections that have a PAT in this browser, one after another, with the same saving and failure rules as a manual fetch. On-prem connections shall not be fetched at start. Sample data shall not trigger it. A Time Writing fetch requested while one is running shall join the running fetch and report its result, not fail. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-21 | The start-up fetch (FET-20) shall wait only for the connection setup from Git — not for the on-prem views' data, which is read after it — and at most 10 seconds for Git, then fetch with the connections here. It shall run as a background job ("Fetch Azure DevOps at start") shown in the top bar with its result, or why the Time Writing connection was not fetched (no connection, no PAT in this browser, on-premises); with no connection set up nothing is shown. | T | M | UC-03 |
| FET-22 | The top bar shall offer **⟳ Time Writing** next to ↻ Refresh: it fetches only the Time Writing connection in use, now — the same fetch as its Fetch in Admin → Streams — waiting first for a fetch already running, and says when it is saved. ↻ Refresh shall fetch the Time Writing connection **first**, then the read-only connections, so a slow or failing view (an on-prem server without VPN) never holds it up. | T | M | UC-02 |
| FET-23 | A work item shall count as **finished** when its State is **Closed, Resolved, Completed or Done** — one list for every page (Pulse Check, the Monthly pack, Planning Overview, Insights, My Tasks, query views, the Edit form). Removed and Cut are not finished; pages that list open or pending work leave them out separately (owner decision 10). | T | M | UC-02, UC-05 |
| FET-24 | A finished item **without a Closed Date** shall take its close date from the item: its **State Change Date** (the day it entered the finished state), else its Resolved Date; when neither is there, from its **history** — the last change that moved it into a finished state — read once per item version (one read-only GET of the item's updates, at most 200 items per fetch, kept in this browser). Only when nothing tells shall its last-changed date be used (owner decision 10). | T | M | UC-02, UC-05 |

### 5.4 Time Writing naming rules (TWN) — UC-04, UC-05

*Group rationale: story names are free text ("what I wrote randomly"). The Month Task is the anchor.*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TWN-01 | The app shall treat a Task as a Month Task for month M when its title starts with M, given as a full or 3-letter English month name (case-insensitive, optional ".") followed by a 4-digit year, with any of the separators space `,` `.` `_` `/` `-` or none. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-02 | The app shall treat a Task as a Month Task for month M when its title starts with `YYYY-MM`, `YYYY/MM` or `YYYY.MM` (month 1–12, optional leading zero) not followed by a digit. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-03 | The app shall treat a Task as a Month Task for month M when its title ends with M in the forms of TWN-01 or TWN-02. Trailing spaces, `.`, `)` or `]` are allowed. A letter shall not come directly before the month name. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-04 | When a title both starts and ends with a month, the leading month shall be used. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-05 | The app shall not treat a month that appears only in the middle of a title as the Task's month. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-06 | The app shall not treat a word that merely begins with a month name (e.g. "Octopus 2026", "Mayday 2026") as a month. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-07 | The app shall take a Month Task's Feature and Epic from its parent chain, whatever the parent story is called. | T | M | UC-04 |
| TWN-08 | The app shall identify Time Writing stories per the definition in §1.4. | T | M | UC-05 |
| TWN-09 | When placing a new Month Task under a Feature, the app shall choose, in this order: (1) an existing live Task of mine for that month, which is reused rather than created; (2) the one live story titled exactly "{Feature} - Time Writing"; (3) the one story holding my Month Tasks; (4) the Feature's only Time Writing story; (5) a new story. When more than one candidate exists at step 2, 3 or 5, the result shall be a conflict and nothing shall be created. | T | M | UC-06 |

### 5.5 Monthly Time Writing (MON) — UC-04, UC-05

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MON-01 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall provide, in the page header, a Team/Individual switch labelled "Time writing for", defaulting to Individual. The choice shall be remembered in localStorage `demand-planning-monthly-view-v1`. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-02 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* In Individual mode the page shall show only the chosen person's Tasks. In Team mode it shall show every person's Tasks. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-03 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* In Individual mode with no person chosen and no Me, the page shall show "Choose whose time writing to show, or set your Azure email on the Time Writing connection to make "Me" the default." and list no Tasks. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-04 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall show the metrics "Tasks", "Original Estimate", "Completed Work" and "Staged changes" for the selected month. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-05 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall list each Month Task of the selected month with: Epic, Feature (the nearest Feature above the Task), Month Task, State, Original Estimate, Completed Work, Tags. Epic, Feature and Task shall link to Azure DevOps. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-06 | Planning for month M shall be locked when it is locked early for M in Settings → Period control (MON-23), or once its plan lock date has ended: the date set for M, else the default planning lock day of M (5th unless changed, MON-21); the lock date ends at midnight UTC (NAV-51), the same moment for every viewer. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-07 | Actuals for month M shall be locked when they are locked early for M, or once M's actuals lock date has ended: the date set for M, else the default actuals lock day of M+1 (4th unless changed, MON-21); the lock date ends at midnight UTC (NAV-51). Both lock dates belong to M's own entry. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-08 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall show the pills "Planning open"/"Planning locked" and "Actuals open"/"Actuals locked". | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-09 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The Original Estimate input shall be disabled while planning is locked, when the Task is tagged `ad-hoc`, or while saving. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-10 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The Completed Work input shall be disabled while actuals are locked or while saving. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-11 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* Edits shall be held as staged changes until "Save {n} staged task(s) and refresh" is pressed. "Discard staged changes" shall clear them. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-12 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The app shall reject the whole save, writing nothing, when any staged value is empty, not a finite number, or negative, with "Original Estimate and Completed Work must be non-negative numbers." | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-13 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The app shall reject the whole save, writing nothing, when any staged change falls in a locked field, with "A staged change is in a locked field. Unlock the period in Settings before saving." | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-14 | Saving shall write only Original Estimate and/or Completed Work (and Tags when MON-15 applies) to each changed Task, then read the Task back and verify it. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-15 | When planning is locked and a Task with no Original Estimate first receives Completed Work, the save shall add the tag `ad-hoc` to that Task. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-16 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* After a successful save, the app shall clear the staged changes and refresh the Time Writing data from Azure DevOps (FET-04). | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-17 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The save button shall be disabled when there are no staged changes, while saving, or when the active connection has no PAT. | D | M | UC-04 |
| MON-18 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* The page shall show a check panel, titled "Not picked up for {Month YYYY} ({n})" or "Everything picked up for {Month YYYY}". It shall list: (a) open Time Writing stories with no Month Task for the month; (b) Tasks under a Time Writing story with no month in the title; (c) Month Tasks with no Feature above them, with the parent shown. | T | M | UC-05 |
| MON-19 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* In Individual mode, the check panel shall list only the chosen person's stories: those assigned to them, or holding one of their Tasks. | T | S | UC-05 |
| MON-20 | A month Time Writing Task newly created after its month's planning has locked (the MON-06 / MON-07 rule with the Settings periods) — by New Feature, Complete Feature children or Bulk actions → Create month tasks — shall be created with the tag `ad-hoc` and read back with it; the Feature, the User Stories and an existing Task that is reused shall not be tagged. The form and the confirmation shall say so before Save, and the result shall name it. Both transports shall accept only this one tag, only on a new Task. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-21 | Settings → Capacity & periods → Period control shall set the default lock days for every month without dates of its own — **Last day to update the plan** (1–28 of the month, default 5) and **Last day to update actuals** (1–28 of the next month, default 4); the day entered is still open and the month locks from the next day — so the November plan can be updated until 5 November (locked from 6 November) and November actuals until 4 December (locked from 5 December). | T | M | UC-04, UC-14 |
| MON-22 | The locks shall be worked out from the current time on every page (Time Writing, the Edit form, ad-hoc tagging of new Tasks), checked again at least every minute, so a new month starts open when the month rolls over and the previous month locks on its own dates. | T | M | UC-04 |
| MON-23 | Period control shall show, for the header month, planning and actuals each with Open / Locked, its **last day to update** (the month's own date or the default), a date field to set that month's own last day (a later date reopens it), **Use default**, and **Lock now** / **Undo early lock**, with an optional reason; each change is in the audit list with who and why. | T | M | UC-14 |
| MON-24 | The default days and each month's dates, early locks and reason shall be kept with the capacity settings in the Git planning data, so every computer applies the same locks; read back, only valid months (YYYY-MM), dates and days 1–28 are kept. Lock records kept only in a browser by earlier versions are no longer used. | T | M | UC-14 |

| ATM-11 | *(Retired — see ACT-15)* Creating month tasks shall re-read the Feature's children from Azure DevOps and apply TWN-09. It shall create at most one "{Feature} - Time Writing" story and one "{Month YYYY} \| {Feature}" Task assigned to Me's email, and never a duplicate. | T | M | UC-06 |

| ATM-15 | The page shall offer one search box over the pending list that searches every type at once, ignoring the type chip: ID (with or without #), title, type, state, Feature, Epic, Area Path, Iteration Path and tags; every word must match, in any order and case. | ATM-16 | On every page, explanations of how a list, chart or rule works shall sit behind an "ⓘ Info" button next to the heading they belong to, shown on hover, keyboard focus or tap, instead of as text on the page; short status lines (counts, fetch times, totals) stay visible. | D | C | UC-01 |
| ATM-17 | A Feature row on My Tasks [Board] shall expand (▸ / ▾) one level at a time: opening the Feature shows only its direct children (User Stories, and any Bugs or Tasks placed straight under it; any assignee; Removed left out) in ascending ID order; a child with items under it (a User Story's Tasks) shall have its own ▸ and show them only when opened. Each row shows type, assignee, state and iteration; Tasks show planned and recorded hours. My New or Active items shall keep their action; other people's items shall show "Not mine". The outcome shall be shown on the page and the data refreshed. | D | S | UC-06 |

Only on confirmation shall it close them one by one in the staged order of ATM-06 (New → Active → Closed), each write read back; an item in a state that cannot be closed from here is reported, not written; a failed write is reported and does not stop the others; declining leaves them as they are. For a ticked Feature the month Tasks of each later month up to the Target month (at most 24) shall be created like the month's own (ATM-11: re-read, never duplicated); every newly created Task shall get the entered hours as its Original Estimate, except this month's when planning has locked (it is ad-hoc, MON-20). The result shall say how many were created for later months. | T | S | UC-06 |

Such a Feature is marked "Shared · owner {name}" in the list, the user's Task goes under its Time Writing story in the user's name, and My Tasks [Board] shows a notice naming the shared Features without the user's month Task, with a link to Bulk actions. | T | M | UC-04 |

On a Feature shared by several people who each keep their own User Story, each one is offered the Feature, and their month Task goes under **their own** Time Writing story when they have one (two of their own are a conflict to resolve), never under another person's; with none, the existing rules apply (the story titled "{Feature} - Time Writing", else one holding their month Tasks, else a new one). The creation in Azure DevOps applies the same rule. | T | M | UC-04, UC-06 |

The same rule applies when creating in Azure DevOps. | T | M | UC-04 |

A leave Feature without an open story of theirs keeps one row (ATM-24/25). | T | M | UC-04 |
| ATM-27 | Under a person's own Feature of the Team Holidays and Training Epic, each type — **PTO, Training, Sick Leave, Unplanned Leave** — without an open User Story of theirs shall get a row "Story + task missing" in the Leave and training list; ticked, it creates the story **"<Name> \| <Type> \| Time Writing"** (their Azure DevOps name, assigned to them) and that month's Task "<Month> \| <story title>" under it, re-reading the Feature first so a story of theirs of that type is used and nothing is duplicated. A Feature named after one type ("<Name> - PTO", "Training") offers only that type, and its stories without a type of their own are of that type. | T | M | UC-04 |
| ATM-28 | A **Feature** shall become **Active** only when one of its User Stories (any, Removed ones aside) is **Active**: creating a Feature or a month Task leaves its state as it is (a new Feature stays New); a **User Story set to Active** from the app (My Tasks [Board], Edit) makes its Feature Active when the Feature is New, reported with the result; My Tasks [Board] offers **Set Feature to Active** only for a Feature with an Active User Story ("Set a User Story to Active first" otherwise); Edit refuses Active for a Feature without one ("A Feature becomes Active only when one of its User Stories is Active…"); and Sprint check's state checks flag a **New Feature with an Active User Story** ("… the Feature should be Active too", with Set Feature to Active). | T | M | UC-06, UC-28 |
| ATM-29 | **Sprint check** shall show the **Move to current PI + Sprint** panel at the full width of the page, and **State checks** in the look of the backlog tree (ID, type icon and linked title, state dot, Assigned To, the check and its action). Each check row shall expand (› / ⌄) to the items below it — a Feature's User Stories, a User Story's Tasks, Removed ones left out — and an Active Feature without an Active User Story offers **Choose a User Story**, which expands it. The person's own **New** User Stories there offer **Set User Story to Active** (after a confirmation; their New Feature becomes Active too, ATM-28); other people's are shown read-only. Not available with sample data or without a PAT. | T | M | UC-06 |
| ATM-30 | **State checks** on Sprint check shall also flag a **PI mismatch**: an Active Feature of the person whose Iteration Path is in an older PI (or no PI) than the PI of its Active User Stories (the latest of them; PI = the path up to the first segment named PI or Program Increment, compared by number). The row names both PIs and offers **Move Feature to <PI>**, which sets the Feature's Iteration Path to that PI after a confirmation (its state stays); expanding it lists the Active User Stories in that PI. Not available with sample data or without a PAT. | T | M | UC-06 |

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| BKL-02 | The tree shall exclude items in the state Removed. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-03 | The tree shall remember the column preferences in this browser. | T | S | UC-07 |

When the Epic's Feature setup has title fields (BKL-18) they build the title instead, and a setup whose titles use {program}, {service} or {version} needs a "Program \| Service \| Release" title. Without a setup the built-in milestone stories are named after the service and release of a "Program \| Service \| Release" title ("SFV : Review of Deliverables (Core 5.2)"), else after the whole title. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-05 | *(Retired — see BKL-13)* The Feature form shall offer "Release classification" PU or Full Release. It shall set the description to "Feature DoD: DT finished. This is a PU release." or "Feature DoD: IQOQ finished and Checklist uploaded into the Feature." accordingly. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-06 | *(Retired — see DPF-09)* Without an Epic setup the form shall offer the optional milestone User Stories PRD, SFV, DT and IQOQ (SFV, DT and IQOQ ticked by default) with Select all / Deselect all; ticking PRD makes the Feature a Full Release (its milestones and default DoD), otherwise it is a PU. There is no separate PU / Full Release field. They are titled "{milestone} : Review of Deliverables ({Service} {Release})". | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-07 | *(Retired — see BKL-14)* Creation shall always add a "{Feature} - Time Writing" story in the selected Sprint and a "{Month YYYY} \| {Feature}" Task. The Feature shall be assigned to the creator email, and the children shall not be assigned. | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-08 | Creation shall require a dated PI → Sprint selection, and shall refuse if the selection is no longer current, with "The selected PI/Sprint is no longer current. Reload the schedule and review it before creating. No write was attempted." | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-09 | Creation shall be refused without a creator email, with "Configure the Feature creator email in Azure DevOps settings before creating a Feature." | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-10 | "Complete Feature children" shall add only the missing children of an existing Feature, without recreating the Feature. It shall be offered (on the board and on My Tasks [Board]) only where its Epic offers it (BKL-38) and only for a Feature with no optional User Story: no User Story under it other than its Time Writing story (Removed ones ignored). | T | M | UC-07 |
| BKL-11 | After creation, the app shall read every created item back and report "Hierarchy complete: {n} milestone stories, Time Writing story and {month} task verified." | T | M | UC-07 |

The board tab opens first; the choice is kept per view in the browser tab. | D | C | UC-07 |

The release view's "Create Feature" shall have the same required field (empty at first) and stay disabled without it. The Feature's Description shall start with "Feature DoD: {DoD}" (the prefix is not doubled when typed), followed by the optional Description. Creating a Feature whose Description has no DoD text shall be refused before anything is sent to Azure DevOps, with "Enter the Feature DoD (Definition of Done): a Feature is not created without it." The same applies when completing children. An existing Time Writing story is used wherever it sits (not refused for another path, not duplicated), and Bulk actions → Move to current PI + Sprint never moves Time Writing stories. The Feature is assigned to the chosen person (default the creator email). | T | M | UC-07 |

By default the stories are ticked and it is not; it is ticked by default only when there are no stories to offer. Ticked, it is created under the Feature in the Sprint chosen in the form (the current Sprint by default; the Feature's iteration when none is chosen), assigned like the other children; unticked, it is not created. It is not part of the editable setup. Completing children with it ticked adds it when missing; an existing one with that title is reused wherever it sits. | T | M | UC-07 |

The primary button reads Save. What is created and every rule of BKL-04 to BKL-15 stay unchanged. | T | S | UC-07 |

Locked and never written: Work Item Type, Parent and Area Path (always the parent's); the Time Writing story and a month Task keep their iteration; a month Task keeps the month in its title; a month Task's hours follow MON-09/MON-13/MON-15 (estimate locked after the planning lock or when tagged ad-hoc, Completed Work after the actuals lock, ad-hoc tag on late Completed Work without an estimate). A month the person already has a Task for (under the Feature) says so: it is kept and gets only the hours typed. **Add n months** creates them one after another by the one Task rule (BKL-28: under the Feature, the person's own Time Writing story first, ad-hoc after that month's planning lock) and reports them; a failure keeps the Tasks already made. | T | M | UC-07 |

A month the chosen person **already has a Time Writing Task** for under the Feature shall be marked on its row — "There is already a Time Writing Task for {Month} (#id, its hours). The check runs whenever a Feature, User Story or Task is saved in the Edit form and whenever a month Task's hours are saved, re-reads the Feature and its children from Azure DevOps first, and changes each title at the revision just read. Anything else is left alone: stories without a month Task, Tasks named after something else (leave kinds, holidays), stories people titled themselves, removed items and titles too long for Azure DevOps. Where it is off, the action is not offered on that Epic's Features (Backlogs and My Tasks [Board]); single User Stories are still added with ＋ User Story. Each row shows a **tick box**, its type (Time Writing story and Task named as such), the **#ID opening it in Azure DevOps**, the title, the owner and the state; the person's own items (and the item asked about) start ticked, other people's do not. When a month Time Writing Task is ticked, Close waits for **“I have updated the hours…”**. **Close n items** closes the ticked ones children first (New → Active → Closed), each read back; an item above a failure stays open; the result is reported. The person is told which item changed and from which state; a Removed item stays Removed and is named. ### 5.8 Read-only query views and Releases (QV) — UC-08

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| QV-01 | A read-only view shall show its connection name, organization/project, item count (behind its ⓘ Info, QV-62) and fetch time, plus "Refresh from Azure" (or "Fetch from Azure") and "Connection settings". | D | M | UC-08 |
| QV-02 | Before the first fetch, the view shall show ""{name}" has not been fetched yet." and state that nothing is written to Azure DevOps. | D | M | UC-08 |
| QV-03 | The view's columns shall be the query's SELECT fields, in query order. Each entry shall be resolved against the project's field list, whether written as a reference name (any letter case, with or without square brackets) or as a field name such as Title or Assigned To; common field names shall resolve even when the field list cannot be read. Entries that are not fields of the project shall be named in a notice. When the saved data was fetched with other columns than the connection's current query, the view shall show the current query's columns and ask for a refresh. When the query selects only `System.Id`, or has no query, the columns shall be ID, Work Item Type, Title, State, Assigned To, Iteration Path. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-04 | The column headings shall use Azure DevOps field names when read (FET-19), else built-in names, else the reference name split into words. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-05 | Rows shall initially follow the query's ORDER BY, including DESC. Clicking a heading shall sort by that column, and clicking it again shall reverse the order. Empty values shall sort last. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-06 | The view shall show a summary strip with counts by State, by Work Item Type and by person. It shall also show the count of overdue items (open, target date before today) and of items changed in the last 7 days. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-07 | Clicking a State or Type chip shall filter the rows by that value, and clicking it again shall clear the filter. | D | S | UC-08 |
| QV-08 | The search box shall filter the rows to those where any shown column contains the text (case-insensitive). | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-09 | "Export CSV" shall download the filtered rows with the shown columns. Values containing `"`, `,`, CR or LF shall be quoted. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-10 | The table shall show at most 500 rows, with a note when more match. | D | C | UC-08 |
| QV-11 | Read-only view items shall not count in Dashboard, Monthly Time Writing or My Tasks [Board]. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-12 | Every read-only Azure DevOps view shall be its own sidebar page with no tabs inside it. A release-source view shall show, on one page, the Releases list (QV-13) above the documents (QV-14). | D | M | UC-08 |
| QV-13 | The Releases list shall be one flat list with no grouping by stream: one row per Name + Release, where a path segment "Release x.y.z" gives the release: when the Iteration Path has such a segment, the release shall come from it and the name from the Area Path (its last segment; the segment above a release segment if the Area Path has one; the segment above the release in the Iteration Path if the Area Path is the project alone); otherwise both shall come from the Area Path, the name being the segment above the release segment. Each row shall show the approval date, the document count, and "Tracked · {n}" or "Not tracked" depending on whether a Feature, User Story or Task in the Time Writing backlog has a title naming both the name and the exact release number. Each row shall also show Planned (h) and Booked (h): the sum of Original Estimate and of Completed Work, over all months, on the Time Writing Tasks (month Tasks, or Tasks under a Time Writing story) under the Features that track the release, plus any tracking Task itself; Removed Tasks are excluded, negative values count as 0, each Task is counted once, and a release with no such Task shows "—". | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-14 | The documents shall be shown as a query view (QV-03 to QV-10). |  D | M | UC-08 |
| QV-15 | When any item of a read-only view has a release in its paths (QV-13), the view shall show the columns Name and Release after the ID. The QMS-0025 Time Writing backlog shall not use this. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-16 | Every read-only Azure DevOps view (not QMS-0025) shall show the header month picker and, by default, only the items whose month (UTC, NAV-51) is the selected month: the Author Approval Date (configured field, else any approval-date field), else the Changed Date. On a release-source view this filters both the Releases list and the documents; planned and booked hours stay all-months. The period control **Show: {month} / All months** in the page's source bar (QV-62) shall turn the filter off, and a month with no items shall say so (the totals are in the view's ⓘ Info). | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-17 | Every read-only connection, release sources included, shall keep its own fetched snapshot (by connection ID), so each view shows only the results of its own query. A release snapshot saved before this (one shared snapshot) shall be shown only for the connection whose query it was fetched with, or when there is one release source, and shall be dropped at the next release-source fetch. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-18 | Dates of work items (Author Approval Date, Target Date, query date columns) are stored in UTC; they shall be shown as long dates in the viewer's time zone (e.g. "30 September 2026"), while the UTC date decides the month for the ADS month filter, the Releases list and the Dashboard counts (NAV-51). The header month shall start on the current UTC month (NAV-11). A filtered view shall say how many items have no approval date (their Changed Date is used). | T | M | UC-08 |
| OV-10 | Pulse Check (Data Insights; formerly the Dashboard's "Releases" tab) shall start with the summary: one line per read-only view whose documents carry releases in their paths, as "{view} [{count}] : {name} {release}, …" (releases with documents approved in the month, by Author Approval Date in UTC, NAV-51; "[0] : —" when none). Below it a 2×2 section: one quadrant per view with its release and document counts and the Title (from the query) of each document approved in the month, linked, with its release and approval date, and a last quadrant with releases and documents per view and in total. A separate query (e.g. Content) is its own view. | T | S | UC-01 |
| QV-19 | The Releases list shall show, for each release, the QMS-0025 Feature(s) tracking it as links (#ID title); "Not tracked" when none. A Feature tracks a release when its title, in any order and format and with any extra words (e.g. an Art), contains the release name as whole words and the exact version, optionally preceded by "v", "v.", "Rel" or "Release" (e.g. "Art-A, Dose Planning, v.1.1.0.1", "Dose Planning 1.1.0.1"); a version with other digits around it (1.1.0.10, 11.1.0.1) or another version (1.1.0.2) does not count. Versions that differ only by trailing zero parts are the same (3 = 3.0.0.0, 3.1 = 3.1.0.0, 3.1.1 = 3.1.1.0), both for this match and for grouping documents into releases. A Feature matching several releases counts for the one with the longest name. Stories and tasks do not attach releases to Features. Planned and booked hours come from the Time Writing Tasks under the tracking Features. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-20 | *(Retired — see QV-31)* A release that is not tracked shall offer "Create Feature": a QMS-0025 Feature with an editable title, pre-filled as "{view} \| {name} \| v{release}" (the mapping from the Release work item fields is still to be supplied); Create is allowed only while the title still names the release and its version (QV-19), under the Epic named like the view (or one chosen), assigned to the Time Writing creator email, validated by Azure DevOps before saving and verified after; after creation the release shows it as its Feature. Disabled with sample data or without the Time Writing PAT or creator email. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-21 | *(Retired — see QV-31)* The Create Feature title shall be built from three parts that match the Epic setup placeholders ({program} \| {service} \| v{version}): for DP, Program (default DP) · Service (the release name) · Release; for CP, Platform · Name (the release name) · Release, the platform chosen from a list starting with Platform 1.0 and Platform 2.0 and extended with "Add…" (new entries kept in this browser); for other streams, Stream · Name · Release. Changing a part rebuilds the title; the whole title shall stay editable and must still map back to the release. | D | S | UC-02 |
| QV-22 | Release Details → DP shall show only the DP connection's own query as a table: exactly the query's columns in query order (no Name / Release read from the paths), with the query view's summary, search, sorting, month filter and CSV export (QV-03…QV-10, QV-16) — no Releases list, no QMS-0025 Feature match, no Create Feature, no Pedestal or DHFI data. "Columns (n of m)" shall open a chooser listing every column of the query (name and reference name) to tick the ones shown, with "Show all" and "Only the first"; at least one column stays shown, the choice is remembered per view in this browser, and the CSV holds the shown columns. CP, IOCC, Content and Tool Validations are unchanged. The DP Feature matching and DHFI linking are in the backlog (OI-14). | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-23 | A query column written as a bare field name ("[SourceProgram]") whose field has a prefixed reference name ("Custom.SourceProgram") shall show that field's values: when the name is neither a reference name nor a display name of the project, the one field whose reference name ends with ".{name}" is used, both when resolving the query's columns and when keeping the fetched values (also when the field list cannot be read). With several such fields the column stays as written and is not guessed; a written prefix is never replaced. Data fetched before this needs one "Refresh from Azure". | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-24 | Release Details → DP shall filter its query's rows by **DDT Date** (in UTC, NAV-51) for the header month — "Month filter: by DDT Date in UTC" (in the view's ⓘ Info, QV-28), rows without a DDT Date only with "Show: All months" — and show the counts of the month above the tabs (QV-62: Releases in the month, Completed, Supported): **Completed Releases** = the releases whose DDTStatus is Done with the DDT Date in the month; **Supported Releases** = the **unique releases** with at least one milestone Done with its own date in the month — DDT (DDTDate / DDTStatus), DRfH, DRPC or DRPE — each release counted once however many of its milestones fell in the month; a release is its PsName + Release (two rows naming the same release count once), else its work item. The Supported hint shows how many milestones were Done and the split per milestone; folded under the counts, "Supported releases counted" lists each release once with its milestones and their dates, the latest date, ID and Title. "Done" is matched ignoring case and spaces; the fields may carry a prefix (Custom.DDTDate). With no DDT Date in the month the counts stay above "No release has its DDT Date in …"; the counting rules are behind ⓘ "What was counted". | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-25 | Pulse Check and the Dashboard shall count DP from the DP connection's query in the same way: its releases are the Completed Releases and, in place of documents, the Supported Releases ("n completed" / "n supported" in the stream quadrant, the comparison with the previous month and the totals table; "· n supported" per month and in total in the Dashboard's delivery table), each supported release listed once as its titles with the milestones it passed by their team names ("SFV · DT Done", QV-26); the Dashboard's total over a period counts a release supported in several months once. Other streams keep releases and documents. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-26 | The DP milestones shall be shown by the DP team's names while the query fields keep theirs: DT = Design Transfer (DDT), SFV = Start of Verification / Start of Hardening (DRfH), PRD = PRD Milestone (DRPC), RPE = Release Planning Event (DRPE) — in the Supported Releases split ("DT n · SFV n · PRD n · RPE n"), the "Supported releases counted" table (short name with the long name), a legend in the view's ⓘ Info (QV-28), the Pulse Check / Dashboard titles ("DT Done") and the info texts. The counts depend only on the field names (…Date / …Status, prefix allowed), so a changed DP query keeps working as long as it returns these fields; the app adds no project condition, so every row the query returns — from any project of the collection — is shown and counted. | T | S | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-27 | A query view cell whose value is HTML (rich-text fields such as Acceptance Criteria or Description) shall show its text only: tags removed, entities decoded (&amp;nbsp;, &amp;amp;, &amp;lt;, numeric), each paragraph, list item or line break joined with " · "; the full text also shows on hover, the CSV export holds the same text, and search matches it. Values that are not HTML (e.g. "a < b") stay as they are. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-28 | A Release Details / on-prem view shall keep its top short: information about the view — the month filter note (DDT Date for DP, Author Approval Date otherwise, with the count of undated rows), the DP milestone legend, "loaded the data saved to Git…" and the Git save note ("Saved to Git (…)") — sits behind one ⓘ Info next to the view's name, not as banners. Only errors (fetch or Git save failed) and warnings (data fetched with other columns) stay as banners. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-29 | The DP table shall end with a **QMS-0025 match** column: for each row, the Feature and the User Story on the Time Writing board (QMS-0025 items in this browser; removed items and Time Writing stories left out) whose title matches the row's **SourceProgram + Title** — or the Title alone, since not every board title carries the SourceProgram — by fuzzy match at the match threshold (80% by default). The Title holds the name and the version and **both must match** (the NAV-26 score: name 60%, version 40% — the exact version full, the same major.minor a quarter, the same major a tenth); the version is the one in the Title, else the Release field, so a board title without the version or with another one stays below 80%. The search also **combines PsName and Release** (with and without the SourceProgram) and tries both the Title's version and the Release field's, and a board title equal to the one Create Feature gives the row (QV-31; spaces, case and punctuation ignored) is a 100% match, so a Feature created from the row is always found even when the Title differs from the PsName. Shown as "Feature id n%" / "Story id n%" linking to Azure DevOps; with no match, the closest item below the threshold ("closest Feature id · n%") or "no match" / "board not loaded", and **Create Feature** (QV-31). The column is searched and exported to CSV ("Feature 10 (92%); User Story 11 (85%)" or "No match (closest …)"). | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-30 | The DP view's columns shall be rearrangeable: drag a column heading onto another to put it in that place, or in **Columns** drag a column in the list or use ← → to move it one step; the QMS-0025 match column moves and hides like the query's columns. The order is remembered per view in this browser, survives a refresh (a column added to the query later goes right after the column it follows in the query) and is used by the CSV export; **Query order** goes back to the query's order. Sorting by a click on a heading is unchanged. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-31 | Create Feature from Release Details — a DP row without a QMS-0025 match (QV-29) or an untracked release of CP, IOCC, Content or Tool Validations — shall always open the one default Create Feature form ("New Feature", as on the QMS-0025 board and My Tasks [Board]: Parent Epic, Feature title, Area Path, PI / Sprint, Feature DoD, User Stories and the Time Writing task from the Epic's setup, assignee, PAT), never a separate form. It starts with the title "Program \| Service \| Release" — DP: Program = SourceProgram (else DP), Service = **PsName**, Release = **Release** (each falling back to the Title and its version); CP: the release name \| Release (QV-45); other streams: the stream \| the release name \| Release — and the Epic named like the stream (else the first Epic); both stay editable. When it cannot open, the button is disabled and says why (sample data; no Time Writing connection; no Epic loaded). | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-32 | Pulse Check shall show a **Supported Releases** section under the release quadrants, on both the previous-month and the current-month release tabs, **folded by default**: its title and count are shown, and **Show list** opens the list (**Hide list** folds it again; the choice is remembered per viewer in the browser). Opened, for the DP stream it lists each supported release of that month once (QV-24) with its name and release, the milestones it passed in the month (DT, SFV, PRD, RPE, each with its date), "Completed (DT)" when DT is among them, the latest date, a link to the work item and its Title; the count reads "n releases · m milestones" (also while folded); opened with none, "No release passed a milestone in …", or "The DP view has not been fetched yet". | T | M | UC-09 |
| QV-33 | Pulse Check's Releases tab shall show, next to the Releases summary ("Stream [n] : release, release, …"), a **Supported** summary in the same format: per stream the supported releases of the month — DP: its Supported Releases (QV-32); S&S IT: the unique releases of its Windchill export with a document dated in the month (CON-73), "name number" — with the total "{Month YYYY} · n supported releases". It shall be shown also when no Azure DevOps Server view is set up, as long as a stream has supported releases. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-34 | **Tool Validations** shall be counted per **tool**, not per document: a validation has several documents (plan, protocol, report, …) for the same tool, so Pulse Check's Releases line, the Supported summary (QV-33), the quadrant and the Dashboard shall list each tool once for the month, with its document count. Every line follows the page's month filter: the Releases tab shows the month before the header month and the Current month releases tab the header month, for the Releases and the Supported summaries alike. The tool is the Area Path's deepest segment below the project that is neither a release segment nor the stream's own folder ("Tool Validations", "Validation"); without one, the Title before its version, else the Title before its first document word (Validation, Plan, Protocol, Report, Summary, Record, Checklist, IQ, OQ, PQ, Test, Assessment); the version is the Iteration Path's "Release x" or the Title's version when there is one. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-35 | The Tool Validations view (Release Details) shall show its query table without the summary strip — no State or Type filter chips and no People row; search, sort, column widths and CSV export stay. | D | S | UC-08 |
| QV-36 | **IOCC** shall be counted like DP, each release once per month: a **Completed Release** is one whose **Release Notes** or **Verification Report** is signed (Author Approval Date) in the month, and a **Supported Release** one whose **Release Plan**, Release Notes or Verification Report is signed in the month (so, as for DP, the Completed Releases are also Supported). The document type is read from the Title; a release is its Type of Release (Area Path after IOCC), its Title without the version and the document words, and its version, so that the plan, notes and report of one release count once. Pulse Check's Releases line, quadrant (completed / supported), Supported summary and the Dashboard delivery table shall use these counts; unsigned documents and other document types are not counted. | T | M | UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-37 | Release Details → **IOCC** shall group its releases hierarchically: "Group by" (default **Type of Release**), then "then by" for each further level, among Type of Release, Release name and Release (up to three levels, each column once). Each group row shall show its level, value ("(blank)" last) and release count, indented by its level, and collapse on its own; "Collapse all" / "Expand all" shall act on every level, each disabled when it has nothing to do; "No grouping" shows the flat list. A release's name shall leave out the document words (Release Notes, Verification Report, Release Plan, …) so that a release's documents are one release (QV-36). | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-38 | Every table with draggable column widths (the query views, the release tables incl. IOCC, the Windchill releases and the QE Mapping) shall be as wide as its columns — no fixed overall width — so that widening one column widens the table (scrolling sideways) instead of narrowing the others; columns without a dragged width shall fit their content. A **Wrap text** option on each such table (kept per table in this browser, off by default) shall let long cell text wrap so that the table fits the page; off, every cell stays on one line. | T | S | UC-08 |
| QV-39 | A Windchill export page's **Releases** table (S&S IT, and any other export shown) shall end with a **QMS-0025 match** column, searched as on the DP table (QV-29): the release name — and each part of a clubbed release — with and without the stream name, against the release number(s), name 60% and version 40% at the match threshold (80% by default); a board title equal to the Create Feature title is 100%. It shows "Feature id n%" / "Story id n%" linking to Azure DevOps; with no match, the closest item below the threshold (or "no match" / "board not loaded") and **Create Feature**, which opens the default Create Feature form (QV-31) titled "{stream} \| {release name} \| v{release}" (a clubbed release: "{stream} \| {its name as written}") under the Epic named like the stream (else the first Epic). The column is sortable and in the releases CSV. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-40 | *(Retired — see QV-41…QV-45)* **CP** releases shall be read from each asset's **Title**, written "[Release name] [Release No] [Asset name]" with any delimiters: the release name is the Title before its first version and the release number is that version — a dotted number (3.1, 3.1.0), optionally written v3.1, R3.2 or Rel 4.0, or a whole number written with v, R or Rel (v5); the asset name after it is dropped, so all assets of a release are **one release** (name and number, case and trailing zeros ignored). The other naming, "[Asset name] [Release No] ([Release Name])", shall be read too: when the Title ends with a release name in brackets right after the number (e.g. "Test Report 3.1 (Care Pathways)", "SRS - v3.2 (CareLink)", "Labeling 5 (Eclipse)"), the release name is the bracketed text and the number the one before it; brackets elsewhere keep the first naming. Assets of one release in either naming are one release. A Title without a version falls back to the general path rule. Release Details → CP shall list these releases with their asset count, the QMS-0025 Feature found by **fuzzy match** (name 60%, version 40%) at the match threshold, its planned and booked hours, and — without a Feature — the closest one and **Create Feature** in the default form (QV-31) titled with the **release name and number only**, "release name \| vRelease" (e.g. "Care Pathways \| v3.2", no stream in front), under the Epic named CP. Pulse Check shall count CP's releases of the month from the same rule, and its **Supported** summary shall list CP's unique releases with an asset in the month ("CP [n] : release, …"). | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-41 | **CP** shall be reported from its **release-folder tree** (the connection named CP — or starting with CP — whose query is a tree / links query, else the first CP connection; it is listed under Release Details → CP even when its Show in menu is off; with the CP_Release_Folder tree query: Release containers, folders and documents, with the approver slots): a release is a **Release container**, and every Artifact or Document belongs to its nearest Release ancestor (Parent → ID). Release containers are never grouped by codename, version, asset or title, so twelve asset folders under one release are one release. A document whose Parent is not in the data set, a Parent loop, or a document without a Release above it is listed as a data issue with its IDs and not counted. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-42 | A **qualifying QA/QE signature** shall be an approver slot of an Artifact or Document — ApproverNameN, ApproverFunctionN and ApproverApprovalDateN of the same slot number, slots found in the data (1–20 today, more without a change) — with a name, a valid date and a role on the QA/QE list (QAQE for Software, Quality Engineer, QA, QE, QA/QE, QE Software; trimmed, case ignored, no substring match; built in). The document's State and Author Approval Date never filter (a QA/QE signature on an InReview document counts). A dated slot without a name or role is a data issue; a dated QA/QE approval on another work-item type (a Folder) is an exception; an Artifact Review is not counted again. The signature's month is its date in the viewer's time zone. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-43 | For the header month, CP shall count **Supported** = the Release containers with at least one qualifying QA/QE signature in the month on a document anywhere under them, and **Completed** = those whose **main Release Notes** (Release container → Reports or Report folder → a document of Type Release Notes) has a qualifying QA/QE signature in the month; an asset's Release Notes does not complete the release. Each release counts once per month however many documents, signatures or approvers it has; completed releases are part of the supported ones (never added). A main Release Notes also signed in another month is flagged for review; several main Release Notes in one release are a data issue. **Shared containers** (built in: 614437 Clinical Platform Global) are reported apart, not in the counts. Completion history across months is not kept (KNOWN-ISSUES LIM-12). Pulse Check and the Dashboard count CP this way (Completed as releases, Supported in the Supported summary). | T | M | UC-02, UC-08, UC-09 |
| QV-44 | CP release **names** shall come from an optional second CP connection (the CP_Releases data: Release and Content — the other CP connection whose rows carry Content, else one with a Release field; its name and record counts are shown behind ⓘ, and a names connection without any Content is a data issue): the release version is read from the container title (a leading version after an optional "Release", else one right after "Clinical Platform"; never a version in brackets), matched to the Release field (as written, else the first version in it, e.g. "v26.2.0.0 (asset A)") with omitted trailing segments as zero (26.2 = 26.2.0.0, never 26.2.0.1), and the distinct non-blank Content values give the name: one → "Name Version" (blank rows warned), several → conflicting names, none → no name; without the data set, a capitalised word after the version in a "Release <version> <Name> … Clinical Platform" title is a candidate pending review. Names never change the counts and their status is shown. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-45 | Release Details → **CP** shall show, for the header month: the counts Releases in the month, Completed and Supported (QV-62); a **Releases** table of the supported releases with "n releases · m QA/QE-signed documents" (Release name with its name status and warnings, Release = the version, Release Notes signed, Documents = the QA/QE-signed documents, Container ID linking to Azure DevOps, Completed, the matched Feature's **Time Writing Feature**, **Planned (h)** and **Booked (h)** (QV-65), and the **QMS-0025 match** with **Create Feature** titled "release name \| vVersion"), each row opening its signature evidence (document, Type, State, approver, role, slot, date, folder path); the shared activity apart (Container ▸ opening its signature evidence, Why apart, Signed documents right-aligned); the data issues; and **Export evidence CSV**. The whole tree is read (no month filter on the items); the query's rows stay below. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-46 | The release table of every other Release Details view — **IOCC**, **Content** and **Tool Validations** — shall end with a **QMS-0025 match** column, as on the DP table (QV-29), the Windchill releases (QV-39) and CP (QV-45), so that every stream offers Create Feature the same way: per release, the Feature and the User Story on the QMS-0025 board matched by the same search (the release name — for IOCC also its Type of Release with the name — with its release number, the stream as the program; name 60% and version 40% at the match threshold, 80% by default; a board title equal to the Create Feature title is 100%), linking to Azure DevOps; with no match, the closest item below the threshold (or "no match" / "board not loaded") and **Create Feature**, which opens the default Create Feature form (QV-31) titled "{stream} \| {release name} \| v{release}". The "Time Writing Feature" column keeps its match and the planned and booked hours, without a Create Feature button of its own. | T | M | UC-02, UC-08 |
| QV-47 | Every Release Details page with a consolidated result shall show it and the raw data as **two tabs** of the page instead of the raw rows at the bottom: every stream's page — DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations and each Windchill export or search (S&S IT, ECP) — shows **Releases \| Source data (n)** (QV-62): the Releases table (DP: the release list with its QMS-0025 match; CP: the month's releases with their evidence; Windchill: the consolidation, ECP's projects included) \| the query's rows or the imported rows with their search and Export. The consolidated tab opens first; the raw tab shows its row count; the choice is kept per page for the browser tab. A view with no consolidation (an own query) keeps its single table. | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-48 | The actions of a Release Details page shall take little room: the main action as a **small icon button** with its name as tooltip — **↻** Refresh from Azure (Fetch on a page not fetched yet; the progress shown beside it), **↻** Refresh from Windchill, **↻** Import newer export — and the others in a **⋯ menu** (Load from Git, Connection settings, Remove this export…), closed by a choice, a click outside or Escape; the period control **Show: {month} / All months** stays beside them (not on CP, whose report is always the header month). | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-49 | A CP release container titled in the **Platform** pattern "PF1.0 \<Name\>-\<Number\>" or "PF2.0 \<Name\>-\<Number\>" shall take its name — with the variant, e.g. "PF2.0 Carpo" — and its number straight from the title: the number is the part after the **last hyphen** and may hold letters ("A12"); the name may contain spaces and hyphens. Such a release needs no CP_Releases lookup; its Create Feature title is "PF2.0 Carpo \| A12". | T | M | UC-08 |
| QV-50 | Pulse Check shall offer a **Period** — Month (the monthly tabs as before), **Quarter**, **Half-year** or **Year** — in calendar terms (Q1 = January–March, H1 = January–June, the year January–December), with a choice of the latest eight periods up to the one holding the month before the header month. For a quarter, half-year or year it shall show, per stream, the releases of all its months — each release once however many months it appears in, DP's Supported Releases once each, documents added up, the Windchill streams' releases by their dates — against the previous period of the same length (Q1 against Q4 of the year before), with the change, the release list per stream and a total; and the Features and User Stories opened and closed in the period per stream. A period still running says how many of its months have passed. | T | M | UC-26 |
| QV-51 | Pulse Check shall open without recalculating what has not changed: the CP release tree, the release names, each Windchill export's releases and each month's stream releases shall be worked out once per fetched or loaded data set and reused for every month, period and visit; a fetch, import or load from Git recalculates. Reading a release tree of 4,000 documents shall take well under a second. | T | M | UC-26 |
| QV-52 | *(Release names by the patterns of QV-55 first.)* **Content** shall name its releases by the document **Title**, not by a release name and number read from the paths: every document with a Title is a release, one per unique Title (spaces tidied), with its documents and approval date. This applies on the Content Release Details page (the Releases table shows the Title in its **Release name** column and no Release column, QV-62; the query output has no Name / Release columns added), in Pulse Check (Releases summary, the Content quadrant, period totals and comparisons), in the Monthly pack lines fed by Content and wherever the stream's releases are counted. A Feature whose title holds the Title tracks it (Time Writing Feature and its hours). | T | S | UC-26 |
| QV-53 | The **DP** table (Release Details → DP) shall show, after the query's columns, **Month & Year** — the month of the release's DDT Date in UTC (NAV-51; e.g. "Oct 2026"), which the list filters on, or "No DDT Date" (never a Completed Release; listed by its planned milestones per QV-54, else only with *All months*) — and **Milestones** — for DT, SFV, PRD and RPE the month of its date (or "no date") and its status (or "(no status)"), with ✓ when it is Done with a date and so counts as Supported in that month (DT ✓ also as Completed). Both are searched and exported with the table, so a release missing from a month or a count can be traced to its dates and statuses. | T | S | UC-26 |
| QV-54 | A DP release **without a DDT Date** shall be listed in the DP table in each month in which one of its milestones (SFV, PRD, RPE) has its date with the status **Planned** or **ToBePlanned** (case and spaces ignored), its Month & Year reading e.g. "Oct 2026 (SFV Planned) · planned, not counted"; it shall **not count** as a Supported or Completed Release (those need a milestone Done with its date in the month). A release with a DDT Date stays listed by the DDT Date's month only; one with no DDT Date and no planned milestone date is listed only with *All months*. | T | S | UC-26 |
| QV-55 | Release names shall follow **naming patterns** per stream, edited in Settings → Azure DevOps → Stream mapping → **Release naming patterns** (roles with Manage shared settings; applied to the shared release-source settings and saved to Git with the planning data, GIT-14; audited): each pattern is matched against a document's Title ignoring case — `*` any text, `{…}` the release name (it may hold `*` and text), spaces match any spaces — with an optional label as its group; the first that fits names the release, the rest of the Title being the document; a Title none fits keeps the stream's own rule. Built in: **Content** `{*_RELEASE}*` (the name is the Title up to and including _RELEASE, e.g. "ABC_Labeling_RELEASE_IFU_v2" → release "ABC_Labeling_RELEASE", document "IFU_v2"; a Title without _RELEASE stays a release of its own, QV-52); **IOCC** `*Conformance Verification {*}` (label Conformance Verification) and `*Test Tools {*}` (label Test Tools), other IOCC documents keep the IOCC rule. The documents of a release are grouped under it on the release page (one row per release, its documents under it), and Pulse Check, its counts and the Monthly pack use the same names. A pattern is checked as typed (one {…}, at most 200 characters, at most 10 per stream), "Try a Title" shows the release, group and document it gives, and Reset to built-in removes the saved ones. | T | S | UC-26 |
| QV-56 | DP **Supported Releases** shall count only the releases listed for the month (DDT Date in the month, else a Planned / ToBePlanned milestone in it, QV-24, QV-54) that have at least one milestone Done with its date in the month — each release once however many milestones — so Supported is never more than the month's list; a milestone Done in a month the release is not listed in does not count there (Release Details → DP, Pulse Check, the Monthly pack). | T | M | UC-26 |
| QV-57 | **Epics to match Features in** (Settings → Azure DevOps → Stream mapping) shall set, for the streams **DP, CP, IOCC and Content**, the QMS-0025 Epics whose Features and User Stories its releases are matched to — the Board column of their release tables, the DP view, the CP report and the existing-Feature check of Create Feature and bulk create (the 100% rule, DPF-05); a stream with no Epic chosen, and Tool Validations and the Windchill streams always, are matched across the whole board. The Epics are chosen from the board's Epics (search by ID or title); Epics chosen but no longer on the board are named. Changed only by roles with *Manage shared settings*; kept with the shared settings in planning-data.json (Save to Git), checked when read (IDs only, up to 100 per stream), audited. | T | S | UC-02 |
| QV-58 | The **Completed** and **Supported** release counts of **CP, DP and IOCC** for the header month (as Pulse Check counts them) shall be shown on **My dashboard** (Release counts), on each of those **stream pages** (Release Details → CP / DP / IOCC), in **Pulse Check** and in the **Monthly pack**. While a stream has **no data** for the month (not set up, or its view not fetched), the counts an Admin **entered** for that stream and month are used, marked as entered; with none, a placeholder (—) and the reason are shown. An Admin enters them under Settings → Azure DevOps → Stream mapping → **Release counts** (stream, month, Completed, Supported; whole numbers 0–10,000; Remove); real counts replace them once the data is there. Changed only by roles with *Manage shared settings*; kept with the shared settings in planning-data.json, checked when read, audited. | T | S | UC-02 |
