### 5.21 Build and deployment (CI) — UC-19

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CI-01 | Every pull request to main shall run, on Windows: install with a frozen lockfile, lint, version check, unit tests, the web build, and the Windows installer package. | I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-02 | Every pull request to main shall build the macOS universal disk image. | I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-03 | A push to main shall publish the installers, their SHA256SUMS files and `latest.json` to the `desktop-latest` prerelease. | I, D | M | UC-19 |
| CI-04 | A push to main shall run the tests, build the web app, and deploy it to GitHub Pages. | I, D | M | UC-19 |
| CI-05 | The build shall fail when the package version is not `x.y.z`, or when a release tag does not equal the version. | T | M | UC-19 |
| CI-06 | An end-to-end suite (`pnpm test:e2e`: Playwright driving Chromium against the built web app served by `vite preview`, with no network — the synthetic sample and fixtures in `e2e/fixtures` as data) shall automate the UAT scenarios listed in SOFTWARE-COMPONENTS.md §1.10, each named after its test case; every pull request to main and every push to main shall run it and fail when a scenario fails. | T | M | UC-19 |
| CI-07 | CI jobs that run dependency code (install, lint, tests, build, packaging) shall hold a read-only token (`permissions: contents: read`) and shall not persist Git credentials in the checkout (`persist-credentials: false`); only publishing jobs that download already-built artefacts, and the job that writes the release commit (CI-11), which installs nothing and runs only the repository's own scripts with Node.js, shall hold `contents: write`, and workflow steps shall take their inputs from environment variables, not from `${{ }}` interpolation inside `run:`. | I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-08 | The repository shall keep a **Software Bill of Materials (SWBOM)** of every third-party component, generated from `pnpm-lock.yaml` by `pnpm swbom` (`scripts/swbom.mjs`) and never edited by hand: `docs/SWBOM.md` (on the Documentation page) and `docs/swbom.cdx.json` (CycloneDX 1.5 JSON). Each component has its exact version, licence, SHA-512 integrity hash (from the lockfile), whether package.json names it directly, and its scope — *runtime* (a dependency of package.json or anything it pulls in: shipped in the web and desktop app), *desktop runtime* (Electron) or *build / test* (not shipped). `pnpm test` shall fail when the SWBOM does not list exactly the lockfile's components and the product version; the daily package update regenerates it. | T | M | UC-20 |
| CI-09 | Every merged change shall get a semantic version — **major** for a change that breaks existing data, the shared setup files or the way users work, **minor** for a new or changed feature, **patch** for a fix, a dependency update or documentation only — and its entry (version, date, the changes with their requirement IDs and pull requests) at the top of `docs/RELEASE-NOTES.md`. The pull request shall state these in its own change note `docs/changes/<name>.md` (front matter `bump:`, then the release-notes text) and shall not change the version in `package.json` itself: CI shall fail a pull request (other than Dependabot's) without a change note or with a changed version, and `pnpm test` shall fail on a malformed note, or when the newest version in the release notes is not the package version or the versions are not unique and in descending order with an entry each. The version and the entry are written by the release commit (CI-11); each version is tagged and released by CI (CI-10). Configuration items, identification, baselines, change control, release, status accounting and audits are described in `docs/CONFIGURATION-MANAGEMENT.md`. | T | M | UC-19, UC-20 |
| CI-10 | Every push to main whose Windows, macOS and end-to-end jobs pass shall tag the built release commit (CI-11) `vX.Y.Z` (the `package.json` version) and publish a GitHub release of that version with the Windows installer, the macOS disk image, their SHA256SUMS files and the version's entry from `docs/RELEASE-NOTES.md` as its notes, without anyone tagging by hand; when the release of that version already exists it shall change nothing; a `vX.Y.Z` tag pushed by hand shall run the same job and fail when it does not equal the package version. The job that writes the release runs no dependency code (CI-07). | T, I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-11 | After every push to main, before anything is built, a job *Write release commit* shall take the change notes waiting in `docs/changes/` on the newest `main`: the highest bump gives the next version, which it writes to `package.json`; their texts, each followed by "PR #N." from the squash commit that added it, become that version's entry at the top of `docs/RELEASE-NOTES.md`; it removes the notes, regenerates `docs/SWBOM.md`, `docs/swbom.cdx.json` and `docs/TRACEABILITY-MATRIX.md`, and pushes one release commit to `main` (retrying when `main` moved meanwhile). Without notes it commits only regenerated documents that changed, or nothing. The other jobs of the run shall build, test, tag and publish that commit, and their tests shall fail when the committed matrix or SWBOM is not the generated one; a pull request need not commit them. Runs on `main` shall not be cancelled by a later merge. The job shall hold `contents: write` only for this push, install nothing and take its inputs from environment variables (CI-07). | T, I | M | UC-19 |
| CI-12 | Every pull request to main and every push to main shall also run the end-to-end suite (CI-06) with the page on a CPU four times slower (`E2E_CPU_SLOWDOWN=4`: Chromium's CPU throttling for every page the scenarios open), in its own job *End-to-end tests (slow machine)* with a read-only token, and fail when a scenario fails, so a step that passes only because the app is fast (a race between rendering, focus, loading and saving) is caught before users meet it. | T, I | S | UC-19 |

### 5.22 QE Mapping (QEM) — UC-23

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| QEM-01 | Data & governance → QE Mapping shall hold the QE Mapping as a shared table in Git (qe-mapping.json next to the planning data, one commit per save, conflicts refused). Access roles treat it as its own area (default: Admin, Product Owner, People Manager). Anyone whose Git token can save (not with sample data) may upload and change it; others see it read-only, and without Git the mapping last loaded in this browser is shown. | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-02 | "Upload Excel…" shall read an .xlsx workbook (or a CSV, comma / semicolon / tab separated) as it is, without the columns being known in advance: every non-empty sheet becomes a tab, its first non-empty row the column names (an empty name becomes "Column N", a repeated one "Name (2)"), every value text — shared, rich and inline strings, numbers, TRUE / FALSE, formula results, and date-formatted cells as YYYY-MM-DD (with HH:MM when they have a time); empty rows and trailing empty columns are dropped. An old .xls file is refused with "Old .xls files cannot be read …"; replacing an existing mapping asks first. The upload is a draft until Save to Git. | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-03 | The table shall be filterable: a search over all shown columns and, with "Filter by column", a filter per column (contains, any case); the count of matching rows is shown. At most 300 rows are rendered at a time, with "Show 300 more". | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-04 | With the right to change it, a cell shall be edited by clicking it (Enter or leaving the field keeps it, Escape cancels); "＋ Add row" adds an empty row at the top; rows are selected with check boxes (or all shown rows) and "Delete n rows" removes them after confirmation. | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-05 | Each column heading shall have a menu: Rename…, Hide, Move left / right, Group by this / Ungroup and Delete column… (with its values, after confirmation); "＋ Add column" adds a named column; names must be unique per tab. Rename, move, add and delete change the shared mapping; hiding is each viewer's own choice, kept in this browser per tab ("Columns (n of m)" lists every column to show or hide; at least one stays shown). | T | M | UC-23 |
| QEM-06 | "Group by" (a column) shall show the rows in collapsible groups by that column's value ("(blank)" last), with each group's count; grouping and filters are for the current visit. | T | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-07 | "Export Excel" shall download every tab with all its columns and rows as .xlsx (header row bold); "Export CSV" the current tab's shown columns and filtered rows. A mapping over 900 KB is refused at Save to Git with a message, as GitHub reads larger files differently. Changes show the unsaved bar (description, Discard, Save to Git); "Reload from Git" asks before discarding them. | T | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-08 | The QE Mapping table shall be compact: tight cell padding and smaller text, headings on one line, columns as narrow as their content (long text wraps only with Wrap text on, QV-38), so that more columns fit on the screen. | D | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-09 | Each QE Mapping column shall be resizable by dragging the right edge of its heading (double-click resets that column); the widths shall be kept per tab and column in this browser, and "Reset widths" shall return every column to its automatic width. | D | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-10 | When the QE Mapping is grouped into two or more groups, "Collapse all" shall close every group at every level (only the outermost group rows with their counts stay) and "Expand all" shall open them all; each button shall be disabled when it has nothing to do. | D | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-11 | Grouping shall be hierarchical: up to four columns, outermost first ("Group by", then "then by" for each further level, or "Then group by this" / "Remove from grouping" in a column's menu). Each group shall show its column, value and row count, indented by its level, and be collapsible on its own; a value under two different parents is two groups. Changing a level drops the levels after it. | T | S | UC-23 |
| QEM-12 | **＋ Add row** shall open a **pop-up** with one field per column of the tab; **Save** shall add the row (first in the table) and **save the QE Mapping to Git at once** (with any other unsaved change, which the pop-up says), with a commit message naming the row's QE; if Git refuses, the row stays as an unsaved change with the reason. The **QE Name** column (a column named "QE Name", "QE", or a QE person / owner / resource column) shall be chosen from the **Resources list** (everyone with Time Writing Tasks and everyone with a resource profile, Settings → Resources), in the pop-up and when editing its cells (a value outside the list is kept). | T | M | UC-11 |

### 5.23 Monthly pack (PCK) — UC-24

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PCK-01 | Pulse Check shall have a **Monthly pack** tab for the month before the header month (the month presented in the following month's call), showing the two slides as boxes, in this order: the **Performance Pulse** (Summary, Overall status, Key takeaways, Key next steps, Decision required, Risks, Proposed mitigation, Leadership support requested, KPI Pulse) and the **2x2** (Actions/Deliverables Realized, Observations/Challenges & Risks, Focus Areas for Next Months, Support Requested / Topics to Highlight). | D | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-02 | Settings → **Monthly pack** (shared settings) shall keep the pack setup in Git as monthly-pack-setup.json: the slide lines in order, each with a label, the app streams that feed it (DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations, S&S IT) and the Epics whose Features closed in the month count for it; and the KPI Pulse rows, each with a label and what it reads — an Epic metric (open items, items closed in the month, items closed this year, closed share), the actual utilisation of the month or the year, or a value typed per month. Without the file the default lines (Serviceability = S&S IT, Digital Platform = DP, Clinical Platform = CP, IOCC, PPU, Content, Tool validation) and KPI rows apply. Unknown metrics, duplicate ids and invalid Epic ids are dropped on reading. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-03 | Actions/Deliverables Realized shall list, per slide line with at least one entry, the line's releases of the month ("Line - release, release"): the completed releases of its streams (DP and IOCC per QV-32 / QV-36, the S&S IT export's releases of the month, the other streams' releases with documents approved in the month) and the titles of the Features closed in the month under its Epics. Key takeaways shall list the supported count per line ("Line = n"), where a stream's count is its Supported Releases (DP, IOCC) else its releases and an Epic's count its closed Features. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-04 | KPI Pulse shall show each row's value: for an Epic metric the count over every item below the Epic except Tasks, Epics and Removed items (closed = Closed Date, else a finished state by its last change); the utilisation rows as the actual utilisation (booked hours over available hours of the people with bookings) of the month or of January to the month; a typed row its value for the month, editable in the tab. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-05 | The pack shall generate: the capacity line "Capacity: n% utilization (Mon) – QE – n% (Mon)" (QE = people with the role QE) in Observations and, as "Stretched Resources", in Support Requested; the Summary sentence with the month's utilisation; the year-on-year line "YoY support change of ±n% overall – with ±n% in Line, … (Mon YTD vs. last year)" from the supported counts of January to the month against the same months of the previous year (left out when last year has no data); and, when the month's utilisation is 100% or more, the Risks line "Overload: n% actual utilization (QE n%)". | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-06 | Every other box shall be text typed per month, one line per bullet, kept in Git as monthly-pack/<YYYY-MM>.json with the typed KPI values — blank until typed, saved with "Save to Git" (a Git token that can write; refused in sample mode; a concurrent change is refused), and read when the month is chosen. Each box and each slide shall be copyable as text (generated lines first, then the typed lines, "• " bullets), and **Download .pptx** shall write both slides as a 16:9 PowerPoint file (one box per slide area, heading and bullets, no library) named pulse-check-<month>.pptx. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-07 | Every KPI Pulse row shall take a value typed for the month, also a calculated one (utilisation, Epic counts): the typed value overrides the calculation on the slide and in the .pptx, the calculated value stays shown next to it, and "Use calculated" clears the override. A typed utilisation (a number, with or without %) shall also replace the calculated one in the generated lines (Summary, the capacity line, Risks). The override is kept with the month's typed KPI values (PCK-06). | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-08 | Settings → Monthly pack shall take the team's PowerPoint template (.pptx, at most 10 MB, with a Performance Pulse slide holding KPI PULSE and a 2x2 slide holding "Actions/Deliverables Realized"; a file without them is refused, and boxes the template lacks are named) and keep it only in the Git data repository (Storage) as monthly-pack-template.pptx, never in the app's source; replace and remove it there (queued, GIT-17: a template cannot be merged, so the latest save by its time wins and an older replace or remove never overwrites a newer template — the panel says "Not replaced: a newer template saved by someone else is in Storage and is kept."); and a **Department name** (shared setup). Download .pptx shall then fill that template — page 1 the Performance Pulse, page 2 the 2x2 — changing only the text of its boxes, found by their labels and positions: title "<Department>\| Performance Pulse update" and "<Month YYYY> Update", Summary, Overall status (On Track / At Risk / Off Track chosen per month; **Not set** until one is chosen — the word "Not set", the symbol – and a grey circle, never a green On Track nobody judged (owner decision 3); chosen: the word, the symbol ✓ / ! / ✕ and the circle green / amber / red), Key takeaways, Key next steps, Decision required (first line as the headline, the rest below), Risks and Proposed mitigation, Leadership support requested, the KPI PULSE rows in order, and the four 2x2 boxes; text longer than its box shall shrink to fit; every other part of the file is kept, and the file properties shall name nobody. Without a template the plain layout is used, in the same page order. | T | M | UC-24 |
| PCK-09 | Typing in a Monthly pack box shall not recount the data: the releases per line and month, the YoY figures and the calculated KPIs shall be kept while only the typed text or KPI values change, and the boxes shall take the keystrokes at once while the generated lines follow. | T | S | UC-24 |
| PCK-10 | Every typed box of the Monthly pack — the 2x2 (Actions/Deliverables Realized included) and the Performance Pulse, except Overall status and KPI Pulse — shall show its text as **bullets**: each generated bullet (from the data) and each typed bullet has a **+** that adds a new typed bullet **right below it**, and **+ Add bullet** adds one at the end of the box; a typed bullet is edited in place — its text wraps and the field grows with it, one line per bullet (v7.2.1) — **Enter** adds the next one below it, **✕** (or Backspace on an empty one) removes it; the new bullet takes the focus. The slides (.pptx, Copy) show the typed bullets in that place; a bullet placed below a generated bullet that is no longer there goes to the end of the box. The bullets are saved with the month's text in the Git data repository (monthly-pack/<month>.json; the place as `anchors`, only for boxes with a bullet below a generated one) on **Save to Git**; empty bullets are not saved; files without anchors keep their typed lines at the end. Without a Git token that can save, the bullets are read-only (no + and no fields). | T | S | UC-24 |
| PCK-11 | Actions/Deliverables Realized shall end with a **Capabilities (Projects & Services)** bullet, "<label> [n]: Feature, Feature, …": the **unique Features** under the Epics chosen in Settings → Monthly pack (Capabilities bullet: the label and several Epics, shared in Git with the pack setup) that have **actual hours in the month** — a month Time Writing Task of that month under the Feature with Completed Work above 0, from anyone; each Feature once, titles in order. No Epics chosen, or no Feature with hours: no bullet. Typed bullets can be added around it (PCK-10); it is in the .pptx and Copy like the other lines. | T | S | UC-24 |
| PCK-12 | **Key takeaways** shall show the Capabilities count (PCK-11) in its "Supported:" line, after the release lines, as "<label> = n" (e.g. "Supported: Content = 3; Capabilities (Projects & Services) = 5"), n being the number of unique Features in the Capabilities bullet; no Epics chosen or no Feature with hours: no count. It is in the .pptx and Copy like the other lines. | T | S | UC-24 |

### 5.24 Insights workspace (WSP) — UC-25

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WSP-01 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* The menu shall have an **Insights workspace** page under Data Insights, beside the Dashboard, which stays unchanged. The page shall read the header month and offer the Dashboard's Region, Role, Team member and Stream filters (people and hours; releases, baselines and the timeline are not filtered and say so) and two tabs, **Overview** and **Insights & monthly pack**, the tab remembered in the browser. | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-02 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* Overview shall show four KPI tiles with the change against the previous month: the planned utilisation of the month (Settings → Capacity bands, with planned and capacity hours and the actual utilisation), the releases completed in the month (every default stream, counted as in Pulse Check), the number of exceptions (per kind), and the **data completeness** — the share of the month's planned Tasks (Original Estimate > 0, outside absence, shared time and NPRA) that carry a recorded value, with the people without a region or role — followed by the workload and capacity trend of the twelve months to the header month and the completed releases cumulative from January, this year against the prior year (prior year left out when it has no data). | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-03 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* The **exception queue** shall list, high severity first, then baseline, capacity and missing kinds, then by impact: **Over 25% baseline** — a release whose booked hours exceed its current baseline (Baselines) by more than 25% (high at 50%); **Missing baseline** — an open release under a stream with an estimation rule but no scope; **Capacity risk** — a person planned at or above the overloaded band in the month (high at 20 points above it); each row with its stream, region, impact, severity, owner and target date, and chips to show one kind. | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-04 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* **Timeline health** shall count the Features (not Removed) whose Target Date lies between the first day of the current month and six months ahead, per Epic and in total: completed (finished state), overdue (open, date gone), at risk (open, due within 30 days) and planned, with a stacked bar per Epic. | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-05 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* Insights & monthly pack shall show **evidence-based insight cards** computed by rules, not a language model, each with a tone (as expected, worth a look, needs attention, no data), a sentence, the filters and what was checked, a small bar chart and the source rows on demand: planned utilisation against the previous month and the same month last year; people planned over capacity; planned Tasks without recorded hours (elapsed and partial months); releases over their baseline; releases completed against the previous month and last year; streams resting on one person (80% or more of the year's recorded hours); Features past or near their target date. A card without data says so or is left out. | T | M | UC-25 |
| WSP-06 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* Each card with a finding shall offer **Add to monthly pack**: the line joins an insights queue kept in the browser per month, with removal; **Save to monthly pack** shall append the queued lines to the Key takeaways of the header month's Monthly pack in Git (PCK-06 rules: a writable token, refused in sample mode) and clear the queue. The tab shall also show a preview of the month's pack (key figures, the generated capacity, supported and YoY lines, the saved Key takeaways, next steps and observations) and the mock-up's suggested questions as buttons that open the section holding the evidence; there is no free-text question (LIM-09). | T | S | UC-25 |
| WSP-07 | *(Retired — see MYD-12)* For now the Insights workspace shall be hidden: the menu shall not offer it (Data Insights keeps the Dashboard, Pulse Check and PI Review). Its code, rules and saved queue stay in the build so it can be shown again without rework; WSP-01…06 apply when it is shown. | T | S | UC-25 |

### 5.25 My dashboard and team insights (MYD) — UC-26

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |

| MYD-01 | Data Insights shall start with **My dashboard**, for every user (in the tile view the app opens on Home, NAV-42, and My dashboard is one of its tiles; in the classic view the app opens on My dashboard, NAV-46): the person is **always you** (your work email, else the name you chose as yours before an email was set; the Person picker of NAV-47 does not change it); without one it asks who you are. | T | M | UC-26 |
| MYD-02 | **My hours** shall show for the header month the person's planned hours (Original Estimate of their month Time Writing Tasks) against their capacity (the Dashboard's capacity model), recorded hours, utilization (recorded, else planned, ÷ capacity; marked at the overload band), overrun and ad-hoc hours, and a table of the six months up to the header month; a link opens Monthly Time Writing. | T | M | UC-26 |
| MYD-03 | **My open work** shall count the person's New and Active Features, User Stories, Tasks and Bugs (work under the absence Epic left out), the items ready to close and the Features without an active story (the Bulk actions state checks), with links to My Tasks [Board] and, when there is something to fix, Bulk actions. | T | M | UC-26 |

By default Admin and People Manager see Team insights; an access file written before the area existed gives it to Admin and People Manager when they saw the Data Insights, and to no other role; once the file lists the area, it is taken as written. Without access roles (no Git storage or no file) everyone sees everything, as before. | T | M | UC-26 |
| MYD-06 | My dashboard shall offer **Customize**: each section with a tick to show or hide it and ↑ ↓ to move it, **Reset to default**, and for roles with "Manage shared settings" **Use as default for everyone**; the sections then show in the person's order. A section added later appears after its neighbour on the page; one that no longer exists is dropped. With every section hidden the page says how to show them again. | T | M | UC-26 |
| MYD-07 | **Customize menu** (sidebar footer) shall list the pages the person's roles allow, listed under their menu groups, each with **Pinned** and **In menu**: pinned pages appear first under PINNED, hidden pages leave their group (they still open from links); **Reset menu** restores the groups. | T | M | UC-26 |
| MYD-08 | The Dashboard's period, filters and tab and Pulse Check's period shall be remembered per person with their views (in the browser when no personal views file can be written). | T | S | UC-26 |
| MYD-09 | A person's views shall be kept in the browser at once and in the Git data repository as `personal-views/<work email>.json` about 1.5 s after the last change (a conflicting save is retried once on the latest version; a failure keeps them in the browser and says so), read back at start on any computer. An admin's **Use as default for everyone** writes the sections and menu (not filters) to `personal-views-defaults.json` (queued and merged per page with a default saved by someone else in between, the later save winning for a page or the menu both changed — GIT-17), which applies to every page a person has not changed. Files read from Git keep only known shapes: section and page ids of letters, digits and `_:.-`, filters as plain JSON up to 20,000 characters. | T | M | UC-26 |
| MYD-10 | The Team overview shall open on **Overview**: six headline tiles — **Utilization** (planned, with recorded and capacity), **Overload and headroom** (people planned at the overload band or more, unplanned capacity), **Plan accuracy** (team error and bias over elapsed months), **Plan health** (score 0–100), **Delivery** (releases in the period, streams counted) and **Forecast** (this month's projection against plan, people needing attention) — each with a link that opens the tab explaining it (Delivery opens Pulse Check), followed by the demand chart and its findings. Overview's two sections can be customized like My dashboard (MYD-06). | T | M | UC-26 |
| MYD-11 | The Team overview shall have the tabs **Overview**, **Capacity and workload** (rebalancing, workload monitor, month-end forecast, unusual months, utilization by month and month on month, workload by person, role or region), **Streams** (the stream tables and charts, overrun and ad-hoc, momentum, resilience), **Plan quality** (plan health, plan accuracy, planning horizon and recording coverage) and **Definitions**, with the same calculations as before; release and document counts per stream shall be shown on Pulse Check only. Every tab other than Overview shall show a trail "Team overview › tab" leading back. | T | M | UC-26 |
| MYD-12 | The Insights workspace shall be retired: no page, no code; its exception and timeline views are covered by the Team overview's tiles and tabs, its pack preview by the Monthly pack. | I | M | UC-26 |
| MYD-13 | My dashboard shall show **Signed by me**: the number of **releases** and **documents** the person signed, per stream (DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations, S&S IT, ECP) and in total, for a **period chosen from–to by month** with quick picks (this month, this quarter, this year, last 12 months; last 12 months by default). A document is signed by the person when their name (any name their work email carries, words in any order) or work email is in any field (Azure DevOps view) or column (Windchill export) whose name says **Author**, **Reviewer** or **Approver** — not a date, function or role field. Its date is the person's own signature date when the field has one (the date field of the same role and slot), else the document's approval date (Author Approval Date, else Closed Date; Windchill: its date column); a document without a date is counted in no period. A release counts once the person signed any one of its documents in the period. Streams without such fields are named; the documents behind the figures are listed. Read only. | T | S | UC-26 |

### 5.26 Historical hours (HIS) — UC-27

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HIS-01 | Settings shall have a **Historical hours** page for roles with "Manage shared settings": what is imported (source file, sheet, date and importer), the cut-off month, the rows, people and recorded hours counted, and how many Azure DevOps Time Writing Tasks before the cut-off are left out with their hours; **Upload Excel…** / **Replace from Excel…** and **Remove history** need Git storage and a token that can save. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-02 | An uploaded .xlsx (at most 30 MB, read in the browser) shall be read from its **TransformedData** sheet, else the first sheet whose heading row (within the first 10 rows) has a person (QA/QE), a **Month(Date)** and an **Expected** or **Actual** column; per row: person, month (a date or YYYY-MM), Epic No, Stream(Features), Project / Release Title, Service/Project/Release, Expected (planned hours) and Actual (recorded hours), headings matched in any case and extra columns ignored. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-03 | Only rows before the cut-off month (default **October 2026**) shall be imported; every other row shall be listed with its spreadsheet row number and reason — no person (#N/A, TBD, blank), no month, a month from the cut-off on, or no hours — never dropped silently; rows left out by design (a month from the cut-off on, no hours in the month) shall be counted apart from the **rows to check** (no person, no month, an unknown group), which are listed open. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-04 | The import shall be saved to the Git data repository as `history-hours.json` (never in the app's source; nothing written to Azure DevOps) and read from it by everyone with Git storage; read back, only known shapes are kept (person, month YYYY-MM, positive Epic ID, hours 0–1000, at most 20,000 rows) and an unreadable file counts as no history. Saving is queued (GIT-17): when someone else saved the file after it was read here, the import is merged into the file in Git by row (person, month, Feature, Epic, stream, activity, type and tag), the name choices by name and the import details (cut-off, source, sheet, who, when) as one entry — both imports are kept, the later one wins for a row or detail both changed, and rows from the resulting cut-off month on are left out; a newer file is never written over. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-05 | The report pages — My dashboard, the Team overview, Pulse Check and the Monthly pack — shall count, before the cut-off month, the imported hours only (Azure DevOps Time Writing Tasks of those months are left out, they stay in Azure DevOps) and, from the cut-off month on, Azure DevOps only; the imported rows count as Time Writing Tasks of their month (Expected = Original Estimate, Actual = Completed Work) under a Feature per activity and the row's Epic. Pages that write to Azure DevOps never see them. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-06 | Before saving, each name in the file shall be shown with its report name: matched automatically when exactly one name known from Azure DevOps or Settings → Resources has the same words in any order ("Ana Costa" = "Costa, Ana"), else kept as written (people who have left); each can be changed in a list. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-07 | Rows without an Epic No shall be listed per Stream name with their row count, and each stream can be assigned an Epic from the fetched data; an unassigned stream counts under its own name, an Epic No not in the fetched data under "Epic <number>". | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-08 | A new upload shall keep the earlier name and stream choices for the names and streams still in the file; replacing or removing the history shall be a single commit in Git (its history linked from the page). | T | C | UC-27 |
| HIS-09 | People who have left shall appear by name in the reports for the months of their history; their capacity comes from Settings → Resources (region, role, Joined / Left on) like everyone else's. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-10 | A **Feature ID** column (optional) shall place a row under that Feature when it is in the fetched Time Writing data, counting for the Feature's Epic whatever the Epic No says; rows whose Feature ID is not in the data, and rows whose Feature ID and Epic No disagree, shall be listed before saving, and a row without a usable Feature ID is placed by its Epic No, else its stream (HIS-07). | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-11 | Besides an upload from the computer, the page shall offer **From Git storage…**: the .xlsx files in the data repository's folder and its `history/` folder, read through Git storage without downloading them first (no other path is read). | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-12 | Imported holidays, leave and trainings shall count like Azure DevOps ones under the Feature or Epic a row is placed under (leave as absence, trainings as NPRA under the absence Epic), but each row only for its own person: a row under a holiday or common-activity Feature (shared time, CAP) adds its hours to that person's public holidays or reserved time, never summed and spread over everyone in the Feature's scope. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-13 | A group in the person column shall apply the row to each person in the group, like a regional holiday Feature (shared time): **All** — everyone; **All-BLR**, **All-NL**, **All-US** (also written with India, Netherlands, USA) — everyone in that region of Settings → Resources. Under the absence Epic (or a mapped holiday Feature) the hours are a public holiday, otherwise reserved common time (trainings included); a group is never listed as a person, and an unknown group ("All-XYZ") is listed as not imported. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-14 | Epic No and Feature No (also Feature ID) cells shall be read as Excel writes them — "37753", "37753.0", "#37753", or several separated by , ; / & (the first counts); empty, 0, TBD and N/A are none; any other text shall be listed with its row and column before saving. The streams still needing an Epic shall say why: rows whose Feature No is not in the fetched data, or rows with neither a Feature No nor an Epic No. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-15 | The file shall be read with every month; the cut-off month on the import check shall apply at once to every count and list, so a later cut-off (e.g. November) brings the rows of the months before it (October) back without uploading again; only rows before the cut-off are saved. | T | S | UC-27 |
| HIS-16 | An optional **Tag** column shall give a row's tags (several separated by ";" or ",") to its Task like Azure DevOps tags, so "Training" or "NPRA" counts it as training / NPRA (CAP-32) — also under the absence Epic, where the other rows count as leave; the app's own tags ("History import", "Shared: …") cannot be set from it, and it is kept in history-hours.json (at most 80 characters). | T | M | UC-27 |

| ACT-05 | **Your monthly update** shall show the share of activities updated (work and training with actual hours, leave once added) as a ring with completed / remaining, next to the KPI cards, the next last day to update (plan, else actuals; MON-21) with the days left; the **suggested missing items**, below Your activities, list — the person's open Features without a month Task (ATM-23), with Epic and ID — each with **Add**, which puts it in Your activities (unticking it there returns it to the suggestions); nothing is saved until **Save**. | T | S | UC-28 |
| ACT-06 | Every save on Time Writing shall run in the background, write to dev.azure.com only, be refused with sample data, without a PAT or while View as is on, and report what was added, updated and what failed. | T | M | UC-28 |

| ACT-11 | Above the cards, Time Writing shall show **KPI cards** for the person and the header month: in this order **Capacity** (working days less absence, as on My dashboard), **Planned hours** (Original Estimate) and **Actual hours** (Completed Work) — the same figures as Monthly Time Writing for that person (MON), each with a small badge of its share of the capacity, Actual also as a share of Planned — and **Tasks**; **Unsaved changes** only while the person has changes not yet saved. | T | S | UC-28 |

| ACT-13 | *(Retired — see ACT-16, BKL-25)* Creating a Feature on Time Writing (ACT-12) shall also offer **Add a User Story under it**: a **User Story title** (required when ticked, 1–255 characters), a **description** and an optional **comment**; the story is created under the new Feature, assigned to the person, in the team's current Sprint (else the PI), the comment added to its Discussion; a story or comment that fails is reported without undoing the Feature. | T | S | UC-28 |

| OVW-07 | **Delivery health** shall show, for the six months from the header month, the Features by Target date per Epic — completed, open with the date ahead, overdue (open, date passed) — as totals and a bar per Epic with a legend. | T | S | UC-29 |
| OVW-08 | **Generate Monthly Pack** shall open Settings → Monthly pack (MPK), where the pack is set up and downloaded. | T | C | UC-29 |

### 5.30 KPI tracker (KPT) — UC-31

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| KPT-01 | *(Hidden from the menu since v7.11.0 — the page stays in the build, not offered in the menu or Customize menu, until it is shown again.)* Under **Data Insights** (area Team insights), a **KPI tracker** page shall show the team's KPI tracker workbook read only: one sheet at a time (a Sheet choice when there are several), as a table that follows the table default (UI-12), with an export; above it where the data comes from — "Read live from SharePoint at …" or "Showing the copy saved in Git: read from SharePoint at … by …" — and the file's last change in SharePoint when known. | T | S | UC-31 |
| KPT-02 | In the **desktop app**, the page shall take the SharePoint link of the .xlsx file (Copy path or a "/:x:/r/" link; https, a site under /sites/ or /teams/, no user name or password) and read it **read only**: one GET to SharePoint's REST API (…/_api/web/GetFileByServerRelativeUrl('…')/$value) in the app's own SharePoint session, at most 20 MB, the answer accepted only when it is an Excel file. **Sign in to SharePoint** opens the site in a sandboxed sign-in window (https only, no pop-ups) where the company sign-in is completed; it closes once the file can be read. No tenant or client ID and no password are kept by the app; **Sign out** clears the session. A sign-in page, a refusal (401 / 403), a missing file (404) or an unreachable host is reported with what to do. Nothing is ever written to SharePoint. In the browser the page says that reading live needs the desktop app. | T | S | UC-31 |
| KPT-03 | A read by a role with **Manage shared settings** and a Git token that can write shall update the copy in the Git data repository (**kpi-tracker.json**, one commit per read, never a credential; queued and merged by sheet name with a read saved by someone else in between, the later read winning for a sheet both have — GIT-17): the link, the file name, when and by whom it was read, the file's last change, and every sheet with data as text cells — trailing empty rows and columns dropped, at most 20 sheets, 2,000 rows, 100 columns and 1,000 characters a cell (more is cut off and said so). Other people's reads are shown but not saved. | T | S | UC-31 |
| KPT-04 | The page shall show the **copy in Git** whenever no live read was made in this visit — in the browser, without SharePoint access, or when SharePoint cannot be reached — checked when read (schema 1, link checked, cells as text within the limits); a sheet's first row with text is its header (blank headings become "Column N", repeated ones get " (2)"). Without Git storage it says to connect it. | T | S | UC-31 |
| KPT-05 | A newer copy saved by someone else while the page is open shall be named "KPI tracker" in the **Newer data in Git** banner (GIT-15). | T | C | UC-31 |

### 5.31 DP releases: QE and bulk Features (DPF) — UC-32

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DPF-01 | *(Retired — see DPF-09)* The DP table (Release Details → DP) shall show each release’s **Release type** from its version x.y.z.a (the Release field, else the Title’s version; a leading "v" ignored, missing parts 0): **X.0.0.0 Major** and **x.y.0.0 Minor** use the **Release** set, **x.y.z.0 MLD** and **x.y.z.a Platform Update (PU)** use the **PU** set; any other version has no type. The sets are part of the Feature setup of **Epic 13946** (Settings → Feature setup), each a Feature DoD and default User Stories, built in as: Release — DoD "IQ/OQ is complete and Checklist is uploaded into the feature.", User Stories PRD, SFV, DT, IQ/OQ; PU — DoD "DT is finished.", User Stories SFV, DT, IQ/OQ (each "<milestone> : Review of Deliverables ({service} {version})"); editable and saved with the setup in Git. | T | S | UC-32 |
| DPF-02 | The DP table shall show each release’s **QE** from the QE Mapping: the row whose product column names the release’s product (its PsName, else its Program) — exactly (ignoring case, spaces and punctuation), else as whole words either way — and its QE column, the two columns chosen in the Feature setup of Epic 13946 (from the QE Mapping’s columns). | T | S | UC-32 |
| DPF-03 | The DP table shall show the release’s **Possible QEs**: the people assigned to the earlier releases of the same product (same PsName, lower version) in the DP data, newest release first, without the mapped QE and without the release’s own Assigned To, at most five. | T | S | UC-32 |
| DPF-04 | Releases shall be ticked in the DP table (Select all shown, Clear) and **Create Features (n)** — for roles that may create work items for others — shall list for each its Feature title (Program / Service / Release), Release Classification (DPF-08), User Stories, QE and email, or why it is skipped (a Feature on QMS-0025 matches it 100 %, DPF-05; the bulk Epic has no Feature setup, QE Mapping columns not set, no QE, or no email found for the QE on QMS-0025), with the PI / Sprint (chosen when several are current). Create shall make, one after another as a background job, each Feature under Epic 13946 with the DoD and User Stories of its Release Classification value, else of the Epic's setup (DPF-07, DPF-09), the Time Writing story and the month Task, all assigned to the QE; the result names each Feature made and each one not made with the reason; a stop part-way keeps what was made (Complete children adds the rest). Writes go to dev.azure.com only, as every Create Feature. | T | S | UC-32 |
| DPF-05 | In every bulk Create Features (DP, DPF-04; Content, CNB-01), a release whose QMS-0025 Feature matches it **100 %** (the board match of the release, QV-46 / dpBoardMatch) shall not be selectable — its tick is disabled, with a tooltip naming the Feature — and is left out of "Select all"; a Feature that matches above the match threshold but below 100 % shall be named in the pop-up ("a similar Feature #… (n %) exists") and the release is still created. | T | S | UC-32, UC-33 |
| DPF-06 | Every bulk **Create Features** pop-up (DP, IOCC — QV-59 — and Content, CNB-01) shall offer, on each release that will be created, a **drop-down of the person** it is assigned to (everyone with an email on the loaded QMS-0025 board), **prefilled** — DP and IOCC from the QE Mapping (the mapped name shown under it), Content from "Assign all the Features to" (CNB-02) — and changeable per row. A release skipped only because the QE Mapping gave no QE (or no email for it, or its columns are not set) shall be created once a person is picked; any other reason still skips it. The result lists each Feature with its person. | T | M | UC-32, UC-33 |
| DPF-07 | The Feature setup of every Epic shall keep, besides its default DoD and User Stories, **one set per Release Classification value** — a Definition of Done and User Stories — for the values of the Epic's own **Release Classification** List field (EPS-07); an Epic without one has no value tabs (DPF-09). Admin → Epic & Feature setup → Setup per Epic shall show the values as tabs after **Epic default** (a value with its own set marked ●), each with a switch "Own DoD and User Stories for {value}" (on: edit its DoD — empty uses the Epic's DoD — and stories; off: the Epic default), and the preview shall show the value chosen; the fixed Release / PU tabs are no longer shown. The value chosen when a Feature is created picks the set — its own, else the Epic's default (DPF-09: old Release / PU sets are no longer used) — in New Feature (changing the value offers the new set's DoD and stories) and in bulk creation (DPF-08). There are no built-in Release / PU sets (DPF-09). My own version without sets uses the shared ones. | T | M | UC-32, UC-07 |
| DPF-08 | The DP and IOCC bulk **Create Features** pop-ups shall show, on every release row, a **Release Classification drop-down** with the values of the bulk Epic's list (DPF-07), **prefilled** with the value named like the type read from the release number (Major, Minor, MLD, Platform Update) when the list has it, changeable per row; the chosen value picks the row's DoD and User Stories (shown in the row) and is written into the Epic's own Release Classification field (filling a Block field). An Epic without its own field shows no drop-down and writes no Release Classification (DPF-09); a release is never skipped for want of a type. | T | M | UC-32 |
| DPF-09 | There shall be **no built-in Release Classification and no built-in Release / PU (MLD) lists** anywhere in the app: no Release Classification drop-down with built-in values (New Feature — BKL-33 — and the DP / IOCC bulk pop-ups offer only the Epic's own list, else none); no **Release type** column on Release Details → DP; no built-in Release (PRD, SFV, DT, IQ/OQ) or PU (SFV, DT, IQ/OQ) sets for DP or IOCC bulk creation — a bulk Epic without a Feature setup skips its releases with "the … bulk Epic has no Feature setup"; the Release / PU sets saved in a Feature setup are no longer read (and are dropped at its next save); New Feature for an Epic without a setup offers no PRD / SFV / DT / IQOQ milestone stories and no PU / Full Release DoD (the DoD is typed; the Review of Deliverables story and Your own User Story remain); Content bulk writes no "Release Classification : NA" line. The type read from a release number only pre-fills a bulk row's value when the Epic's own list has that name (DPF-08). | T | M | UC-32, UC-07 |

### 5.33 Epic setup (EPS) — UC-34

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EPS-01 | **Admin → Epic setup** (replacing Master data, whose old links open it) shall keep, per Epic of the QMS-0025 board, the **Feature fields** of its new Features — each with a label (its placeholder {label}), a **type** (Text, List, Number, Date), for a List the shared **value list** it offers, **If empty** (Optional, Warn or Block), where it **goes** (Description, Title, or both) and an optional **on-prem field** — up to 12, in an order that can be changed, and an optional **title pattern** of the fields' placeholders (and {feature}). It shall keep **value lists** shared by every Epic (one value per line); a list used by a field cannot be removed. The setup is one file in Git (epic-setup.json), shared with everyone, saved in one commit with a description; the last one read is kept in the browser. Without Git storage, or for people without the permission, it is shown read-only. A preview shows the Title and Description of an example Feature. | T | M | UC-34 |
| EPS-02 | **New Feature** under an Epic with an Epic setup shall ask for its fields in place of the Feature setup's title fields (with or without a Feature setup): a List field as a drop-down of its list, a Date as a date and a Number as a number, required fields marked. The Title shall follow the title pattern, else the Title fields joined with " \| ", else stay as typed when every field goes into the Description; the Description gets "Label : value" for each field that goes into it. An empty **Block** field shall stop the Feature being created, naming it; an empty **Warn** field shall be shown, and the Feature is created; a value not in its list is flagged. Admin → Feature setup shall say that the Epic setup's fields apply in place of its title fields. | T | M | UC-34 |
| EPS-03 | **Bulk creation** (DP, IOCC, Content) shall apply the bulk Epic's Epic setup to each release: values from the release's on-prem items (each field's on-prem field); an empty **Block** field skips the release (shown before creating, with the field); an empty **Warn** field creates it and lists the field in the pop-up and the result; a title pattern sets the Title, with {feature} as the title the stream gives (without one, the stream's title is kept); the Description gets the field lines. | T | M | UC-34, UC-32, UC-33 |
| EPS-04 | Two permissions of Role rights (CON-94) shall control the Epic setup: **Edit the Epic setup** (fields, title pattern, Copy to Epics; by default Tool Owner, Admin and Scrum Master) and **Edit Epic setup value lists** (by default Tool Owner and Admin). An access.json saved before these existed gives them as by default; an older one to the roles that managed shared settings. | T | M | UC-34, UC-22 |
| EPS-05 | **Copy to Epics** shall give the chosen Epics the fields and title pattern of the Epic shown, replacing theirs (e.g. one stream's Epics split by leader), as part of the unsaved draft. | T | S | UC-34 |
| EPS-06 | A **Text** field of the Epic setup that names an **on-prem field** shall be a **drop-down** in New Feature of the distinct values of that field in the fetched on-prem data (the release source and every fetched view; case ignored, sorted, at most 500), prefilled from the release when created from one; a value already set but not in the data stays selectable. Until such data is fetched the field stays a text box saying so. | T | M | UC-34 |
| EPS-07 | The Feature fields are set only in the Epic setup: the **title fields of the Feature setup** (CON-75, BKL-18) are no longer read, shown or saved — Admin → Feature setup shows the Epic setup's fields instead and offers **Remove the old title fields from Git**, which saves the shared setup (and the person's own) again without them. When an Epic's setup has a field named **Release Classification**, it is the only Release Classification New Feature asks for (there is no built-in picker, BKL-33). | T | M | UC-34 |
| EPS-08 | *(Retired — see CON-97)* Epic setup and Feature setup shall be **one Admin page, Epic & Feature setup**, with the sections **All Epic setups**, Feature fields per Epic, Value lists and Feature setup; older links to Feature setup or Epic setup open the right section. **All Epic setups** shall list every Epic with Feature fields or a Feature setup (shared or mine): the Epic (title from the board), its fields (with list / on-prem and Block / Warn), its title pattern and its Feature setup (Shared, Mine, number of User Stories, DoD), searchable, each with **Fields** and **Feature setup** opening that Epic in the section. | T | S | UC-34 |
