### 5.35 User Story templates (STT) — UC-36

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| STT-01 | **Admin → Epic & Feature setup → User Story templates** shall keep a shared library of User Story templates in the Epic setup file in Git (`storyTemplates`, at most 200): each a **title** (placeholders such as {service} {version} or {feature} allowed) and a **description**, tagged with any number of **Epics** and **Release Classification values** (the built-in values and those of the value lists); no tag means any. It shall be searchable, show where each template is linked in the saved Feature setup, and add, edit and remove templates (a linked one after confirmation) for roles allowed "Edit Epic setup value lists and User Story templates" with a Git token that can save; others see it read only. | T | M | UC-36 |
| STT-02 | In Setup per Epic, the User Stories (of the Epic default, a Release Classification value — DPF-07 — or All Epics) shall offer **＋ Add from templates**: first the templates whose tags fit the Epic and the value shown (Epic tags empty or naming the Epic; value tags empty, or naming the value on a value tab), then the other templates. A picked story stays **linked**: it is shown with a Template mark and its description, and New Feature (ticked by default) and the DP / IOCC bulk creation use the template's **current** title and description; **Unlink** keeps a copy that no longer follows it; a removed template leaves the copy saved with the story. **Save as template** on a story of the setup makes it a template (tagged with the Epic and the value shown; none on All Epics) and links the story. A story's description can be edited in the setup. | T | M | UC-36, UC-07 |
| STT-03 | The User Story templates shall be shown as a **list** (the shared table, UI-STANDARDS §5), not as cards: one row per template with **Title**, **Description** (two lines at most), **Epics** ("Any Epic" without a tag), **Release Classification** ("Any value"), **Linked in** (how many places of the saved Feature setup link it) and, for those who may edit, **Actions** — **Edit** and **Remove**. Search, sort, the row count and Export come with the table. **Edit** and **＋ New template** open the template in a pop-up (title required; description optional; Epic and Release Classification tags), whose **Save** puts it in the unsaved changes that **Save to Git** then commits. | T | S | UC-36 |

### 5.36 On-Demand pages (OND) — UC-37

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OND-01 | The menu shall have a group **On-Demand** that lists every On-Demand page placed there by its name (in the order of the menu layout, then in name order, PGB-04; a page may be placed in another group, PGB-02), for viewers whose roles include the menu area **On-Demand pages** (CON-103); Home shall have an **On-Demand** tile with a link to each page. The top bar shall show the open page's name, with its description as the title's tooltip. The last page opened is remembered in this browser. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-02 | **CoNQ** shall exist from the start: while the Git data repository has no On-Demand page (and none was read here before), the menu shall offer **CoNQ** with the description **Cost of Non-Quality**, saying "CoNQ is not in Git yet: it is created with its first Save to Git."; its first Save to Git creates it. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-03 | A page shall be a work table in the shared table (`DataTable` in a `TablePanel`, UI-STANDARDS §5): one column per configured column in their order, then **Actions** (**Edit**, **Remove**); a search over all columns; sort by any column (a Number column as numbers, right-aligned); the count "{shown} of {total} rows"; the viewer's own column choice, order and widths; and **Export** (Excel or CSV, with "Export tables"). A page without columns shows "No columns yet." and what to do. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-04 | Rows shall be added and edited in a **row pop-up** (one way for every row, UI-STANDARDS §3/§4): **＋ Add row** and **Edit** open a field per column by its type — Text (one line, up to 2,000 characters), Long text (a paragraph, OND-22), Number, Currency (an amount and its currency, OND-23), Date, Dropdown (a list of its options) and Multi-select (a tick per option), each with its column's description under it (OND-25); a required column must be filled, the others are labelled "(optional)"; a Number or Date value that is not one is refused, and a row with no value at all is refused, with the reason above the buttons (**Cancel**, **Add row** / **Save**). **Remove** takes a row out. Each change is an unsaved change of the page (OND-09). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-05 | **Set up columns…** (with "Set up On-Demand pages") shall open a pop-up to **add, rename, reorder (↑ ↓) and remove** columns and set each column's **type** — Text, Long text, Number, Currency (with its default currency), Date, Dropdown (one value), Multi-select — whether it is **Required** and its optional **Description** (OND-25); a Dropdown or Multi-select column has an **option list** whose options are added, renamed, reordered and removed there. Column names must be unique (any case) and given; a Dropdown or Multi-select needs at least one option, without blanks or repeats; at most 60 columns and 200 options a column. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-06 | A change of the columns shall not silently destroy values. When it touches values already in the rows, **Save** first lists what happens and asks (**Back**, **Save columns**): a **type change keeps the values** (a Multi-select value of several choices becomes one text "a, b"; a text becomes a one-choice list), and values that do not fit the new type are kept and shown as **"not a number"**, **"not a date"** or **"not in the list"**; a **renamed option** is renamed in every row; a **removed option**'s values are kept and shown as "not in the list"; a **removed column** goes with its values (named with their number). A kept value that does not fit stays marked in the table and in the row pop-up until it is changed. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-07 | Everyone whose roles include the area sees the pages. Changing rows (＋ Add row, Edit, Remove, Save to Git) needs **"Edit On-Demand data (rows)"**; Set up columns… needs **"Set up On-Demand pages (pages, columns, options)"** (CON-103) — or a co-ownership of the page or its group (PGB-09, PGB-10) — and ＋ Add page…, Rename page… and Remove page… are changes of the menu (PGB-05, PGB-07: a Tool Owner, a co-owner of the page's group, or in the On-Demand group the setup right); both also need Git storage with a token that can save and no synthetic sample ("Synthetic sample data is loaded. Saving to Git is disabled."). Without them the buttons are **disabled, not hidden**, with the reason as a visible line above the table (and as each button's tooltip): the missing permission and where an Admin changes it, or, before the roles file is loaded (CON-101), "Read only: the access roles are not loaded yet, so nothing can be changed on this page."; or the Git reason as a notice. | T | M | UC-37, UC-22 |
| OND-08 | Each page shall be **one JSON file** in the Git data repository, `on-demand/<page-id>.json` next to the planning data (schema 1, kind `on-demand-page`: id, name, description, columns with id, name, type, options and required, rows with id and the values by column id — a Multi-select value a list); the menu lists the folder (only files named `<page-id>.json`, a page id of lower-case letters, digits and hyphens). Everything read back shall be validated: a file that is not a page is reported and not shown; an unknown type becomes Text; columns and rows without a valid or with a repeated id, values of unknown columns and values that are not text are dropped; sizes are capped (60 columns, 200 options, 5,000 rows, 2,000 characters a value); the file name wins over the id inside. A page over 900 KB is not saved. The pages last read are kept in this browser and shown while Git storage is not connected. No token is stored; the only personal data beyond what users type into the table are the work emails (or names) of the people who added and changed rows (OND-20, OND-21). Before any page save, GitHub must confirm the repository is private. | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-09 | Changes shall be saved with an explicit **Save to Git**, as on QE Mapping: an **unsaved-changes bar** per page ("Unsaved changes to {page} — not yet in Git", an optional description, **Discard**, **Save to Git**); the unsaved changes of each page are kept while the viewer moves between pages. Saves are the **queued, merged saves** of GIT-17: queued per file with their time (to the second), the file read again before each write and, when someone else saved it meanwhile, **merged** — the name and description as one entry, **columns and rows by their id**; where both saves changed the same entry the **later save wins**, also between a change and a removal (a removed row stays removed unless it was changed later); the column order follows the save that changed it; columns new on either side are kept. The result ("Saved to Git." / "… merged with a change by someone else.") is a **Background jobs** entry (UI-STANDARDS §8); a failed save keeps the changes, says why in the bar and in Background jobs, and a save kept in the queue offers Retry. | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |
| OND-10 | With "Set up On-Demand pages" (in the On-Demand group; elsewhere as PGB-05…PGB-07): **＋ Add page…** opens the Add page pop-up of PGB-05 (type, name, optional description, icon, group) and creates the blank page in Git at once (its id made from the name, unique), then opens it; **Rename page…** changes the name and description (an unsaved change, OND-09); **Remove page…** asks first in a pop-up naming the page, how many rows and columns go with it and that its file is deleted from Git (its history stays in GitHub), then deletes the file. While no page is left, CoNQ is offered again (OND-02). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-11 | Every On-Demand page shall have two tabs, **Data** (the table of OND-03…OND-07, unchanged) and **Insights ({n})**, in the shared result tabs (`ResultTabs`, UI-STANDARDS §7), the chosen tab remembered per page in this browser tab. The Insights tab shall show a heading panel (**Insights**, how many charts and tables and rows, ⓘ, **＋ Add chart / table**, the unsaved-changes bar of OND-09) and then each insight in its own panel in the order set: the type as eyebrow, the **title**, under it what is measured and how it is split ("Sum of Cost (EUR) by Category, stacked by Sites") and its filters ("Filters: Category: Scrap, Rework · Found on: Jan 2026 – Jun 2026", or "All rows"), ⓘ with its counting rules, the line "{used} of {all} rows counted" (with how many match the filters and how many were left out for lack of a number or date), then the chart or table. A page without rows says "No rows to show." | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-12 | Four kinds of insight: **Bar / column** — horizontal bars, a value per category, optionally **stacked** by a Dropdown or Multi-select column (at most 5 stack colours, the rest as "Other"); **Line over time** — a value per **month** of a Date column, every month from the first to the last (also months without rows), optionally one line per value of a Dropdown or Multi-select column (5 + "Other"); **Pie / donut** — each category's share of the sum of the slices, with more than 5 categories the 5 largest and **"Other"** (with "(none)"), so at most 6 slices; **Summary table** — a pivot: rows by one field, optional columns by another, cells the measure, a **Total** column and row. Charts shall be drawn in the app's chart frame (`ChartFrame`, UI-STANDARDS §9) with legend, **Data labels** box, **Table / Chart** toggle (the Table view lists categories × series with totals; a pie also the share in %) and axis names; the value axis starts at zero (to scale; below zero shown when a sum is negative); the colours are the `--viz-series-*` tokens in light and dark mode, "(none)" and "Other" the neutral `--viz-other`; a keyboard and pointer readout per category or month. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-13 | The measure of an insight shall be **Count of rows**, **Sum** or **Average** of a Number column. Categories: a **Dropdown** by its options, a **Multi-select** by each of its values — **a row counts once in each of its values** — a **Text** by its text, a **Date** by month. A value that is empty, **"not in the list"** or not a valid date goes to **"(none)"**; a Sum or Average leaves out rows without a number, and a line rows without a date, and says how many. Totals (of a category, a series and overall) take **each row once**, so with a Multi-select field the parts can add up to more than the total — stated in ⓘ and under a summary table. An Average is the average of the rows of the cell (an empty cell "—"); Average is not offered for a pie or a stacked bar ("Stacked bars add their parts up…"). Order: **largest first** by default for bars, pies and tables, or by option order / month / name; a line always by time; "(none)" and "Other" last. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-14 | Each insight shall have **filters** of its own: on a Dropdown or Multi-select column a set of its values (and "(none)"), a row passing with any of them; on a Date column a range **from / to month** (either open), a row without a valid date failing it; all filters apply together. The filters shall be stated under the insight's title. A filter with nothing chosen, or ending before it starts, is refused in the pop-up with the reason. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-15 | **＋ Add chart / table** and **Edit** shall open a pop-up (UI-STANDARDS §4, eyebrow "ON-DEMAND · {PAGE}") with **Title** (required), **Type**, the category field ("Categories by" / "Months of" / "Rows by", only columns that fit), the optional second field ("Stacked by" / "One line per" / "Columns by"), **Measure** and its Number column, **Sort** and **Filters** (＋ Add filter, ✕), and a **live preview** of the chart or table from the page's rows; **Add chart** / **Save** puts it in the page's unsaved changes (Save to Git, OND-09). Each insight has **↑ ↓** to reorder, **Edit** and **Remove**, which asks first in a pop-up naming the chart. Adding, editing, reordering and removing need **"Set up On-Demand pages"** (CON-103) and Git storage that can save; everyone with the area sees the insights. Without them the buttons are disabled, not hidden, with the reason as a visible line on the Insights tab and as each button's tooltip (CON-102). A page without columns says to set them up first. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-16 | The insights shall be saved **in the page's file**, `on-demand/<page-id>.json`, as a list `insights` (each: id, title, type `bar` / `line` / `pie` / `table`, category column id, optional second column id, measure `count` / `sum` / `average` with its Number column id, filters with column id and values or from / to month, sort `value` / `label`), written only when the page has insights (the file format otherwise unchanged, schema 1). Read back, they shall be validated: an unknown type, a bad or repeated id is dropped; an unknown measure becomes Count; bad months and empty filters are dropped; titles, values and sizes are capped (40 insights, 10 filters); a reference to a column that is not on the page is **kept** and shown as broken (OND-17). Two saves shall be merged as the rest of the page (OND-09, GIT-17): **insights by id**, the later save winning for an insight both changed (also a change against a removal), their order following the save that changed it. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-17 | A column used by an insight is referred to by its id: a **renamed** column is followed (the new name shows); when a column is **removed**, or its type no longer fits (e.g. the stack column made Text), the insight shall show **"This chart cannot be shown:"** with what is wrong ("The column for the categories is no longer on the page (removed).", "“Sites” for the stacks is now a Text column; it needs Dropdown or Multi-select.") and that it can be edited or removed, instead of failing; the other insights and the page still work. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-18 | Every row of an On-Demand page shall have a **key**, made the moment the row is added (before any save) and never changed: the page's **key prefix**, the date (UTC, yymmdd) and **4 random characters** from 31 that cannot be mistaken for each other, e.g. `CONQ-261123-K7Q2`. It shall be unique within the page although rows are added by several people saving on their own (asynchronously): a new key is checked against the page's keys, and a key that still appears twice after two saves are merged is replaced for the second row on the next save. The key is the first column (**Key**), searchable, sortable and exported, and shown in the row pop-up. The **key prefix** is set per page in **Set up columns…** (1–8 letters or digits; CoNQ starts as `CONQ`, a new page from its name) and is fixed once a row has a key. Rows added before keys get theirs, in page order, on the next Save to Git. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-19 | Every row of an On-Demand page shall have a readable **ID**, the page's key prefix and a **sequential number** of at least 4 digits, e.g. `CONQ-0001`, `CONQ-0002` (more digits after 9999). The number is **given on Save to Git** — until then the row shows "on save" — as the **next free number** (the page's highest + 1, in row order), and **never changes** afterwards; a removed row's number is not given again. It shall stay unique when several people add rows and save on their own (GIT-17): numbers are given when the file is written (also the merged file written after the re-read), a number already in Git keeps its row, and a number that a merge still leaves on two rows is dropped from the second when the file is read and that row gets the next free number on the next save. Rows saved before IDs get theirs, in page order, on the next save. The ID is the first column (**ID**, sorted by number, searchable, exported) and is shown in the row pop-up; the random key of OND-18 stays as the row's instant unique key. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-20 | When a row is added, it shall record **Added by** — the viewer's work email (CON-82), else their name, else "unknown" — and **Added on**, the time in UTC (ISO); both are set once and never changed, and shown as the columns **Added by** and **Added on** (the time in the viewer's time zone, NAV-51) and in the row pop-up ("Added {time} by {email}"). Rows added before keep them empty ("—"). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-21 | Every row shall keep an **update log**: adding the row is the first entry ("Added", with its values), and **every saved edit that changes a value** adds an entry automatically — **who** (as OND-20), **when** (UTC) and **what changed**, per field its old → new value — with no comment to type (owner decision). Saving the pop-up without a change adds nothing. The log shall be shown as **History** in the row pop-up on Edit, newest first ("Changed · {time} · {email}", "Cost: 1,200.00 USD → 1,500.00 EUR"); the columns **Last updated by** and **Last updated on** (the newest entry) are offered hidden at first in the table's Columns list and are always exported. The log keeps the **last 100 entries** of a row, each old / new value cut to 500 characters, and is not part of the table export. When two saves change the same row, the later save wins for the values (OND-09) and the **logs are merged** (the union of both by entry id, in time order) so no entry is lost. A `comment` found on an entry in the file is read and shown, never written by the app. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-22 | A column may be of type **Long text**: a paragraph of up to **10,000 characters** (line breaks kept), entered in a text area in the row pop-up with the count of characters, shown in the table cut to **3 lines** with the full text as the cell's tooltip and in the pop-up, and exported in full. Every value is read back up to 10,000 characters, so a Long text column changed to Text keeps its text and marks a value longer than a Text allows ("longer than a Text", OND-06). | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-23 | A column may be of type **Currency** with a **default currency**: an ISO 4217 code chosen from a list (EUR, USD, GBP, INR, CHF, JPY, CNY …) or another 3-letter code typed in (**Other code…**). Each cell holds an **amount and its currency**, the column's default to start with, entered as an amount field and a currency list in the row pop-up; it is kept as "1200.5 EUR" (an amount without a code counts in the column's default), shown and exported as **"1,200.00 EUR"** (the currency's decimals) and right-aligned, sorted by amount. A value that is not an amount is refused in the pop-up and, after a type change, kept and shown "not an amount"; type changes to and from Currency follow OND-06. | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-24 | In the Insights (OND-11…17), **Sum** and **Average** shall also take a Currency column, **without exchange-rate conversion**: a chart or table shows **one currency at a time** — the currency of most rows first and, when the rows hold several, a **Currency** choice above the chart (for the viewer, not saved) — and leaves the rows in other currencies out; the measure names the currency ("Sum of Cost (EUR)") and the "rows counted" line says which currencies were found and how many rows each ("Currencies found: EUR (3 rows), USD (1 row); shown: EUR — amounts in different currencies are not added up."), and how many rows in other currencies were left out. Count of rows is not split. (One currency at a time rather than a series per currency, so that no bar stack, pie or total ever adds amounts of different currencies.) | T | M | UC-37 |
| OND-25 | Each column may have an optional **Description** — how or what to fill, up to **300 characters** — edited in **Set up columns…**, shown as help under the field in the row pop-up (UI-STANDARDS §3) and behind **ⓘ** next to the column's heading in the table. | T | S | UC-37 |
| OND-26 | The page file (OND-08) shall stay **schema 1** and readable by and from older files: the new fields are optional — a column's `description` and, for a Currency column, `currency`; a row's `seq`, `addedBy`, `addedAt` and `log` (entries `{id, at, by, added?, comment?, changes: [{column, from, to}]}`) — and are validated when read (a number that is not a whole number 1…9,999,999 or repeats is dropped; a time that is not an ISO time is dropped; log entries without a valid id or time, or repeated, are dropped; texts and sizes capped). Changing the columns keeps every row's key, ID, Added by / on and log. An app version from before these fields drops them when it saves the page (LIM-19). | T | M | UC-37, UC-12 |

### 5.37 Custom streams (STR) — UC-02, UC-08, UC-37

*Group rationale: the Tool Owner asked to add a Release page for a new stream without a code change. A custom stream reuses one of the release rules the app already has; it is kept next to the stream mapping in Git and found by its name, as the built-in streams are.*

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| STR-01 | Settings → Streams → Stream mapping shall show a panel **Custom streams** listing each custom stream (name, source, release rule, Feature matching, earlier names, Epics, **Edit** and **Remove**) with **＋ Add stream…**, a pop-up (SETTINGS · STREAMS) with **Name** (2 to 40 characters with a letter or digit), **Source** (Azure DevOps query connection, or Windchill export), **Release rule** (STR-03, each with what it gives, an example and what it counts), **Fuzzy Feature matching** (Azure DevOps only), **Earlier names (optional)** (up to 5, comma-separated) and **Epics to match Features in (optional)** (QMS-0025 Epic IDs, for the rules like DP, IOCC and Content; empty: the whole board). **Add stream** shall save the list at once to the shared setup in Git as `custom-streams.json` (next to `azure-views.json`; commit "Add stream: <name> (<rule>)"), record the result in Background jobs and say to map a connection or export named like the stream. The built-in streams (DP, CP, IOCC, Content, Tool Validations, S&S IT, ECP) are not listed there and cannot be changed or removed. | T | M | UC-02 |
| STR-02 | Every place that lists or looks up streams shall use the **built-in streams followed by the custom streams** (one list, `allStreams`): the Stream mapping rows (with a **Custom** mark and the rule's plain name; **Add query** opens the connection form named like the stream), the Release Details menu (Azure DevOps Server, or Windchill exports; ＋ when not set up yet), the Home tile Release Details, the views that are a stream's (not listed under Other views), Pulse Check, the Dashboard delivery counts, the Monthly pack setup's stream ticks and figures, My dashboard's signed documents, Release naming patterns (not for streams like DP) and My views' name check. A connection or export belongs to the stream whose name — or an earlier name — it is or starts with as whole words; where several fit, the **longest name wins** (a custom "DP Labels" keeps its connection from DP). The Get started step "TFS APAC settings", the Admin-entered release counts (CP, DP, IOCC) and the Feature matching Epics of the shared settings stay with the built-in streams. | T | M | UC-08 |
| STR-03 | The release rules offered shall be the app's own, by plain names with an example: **Like DP — release from Area/Iteration path** (name from the Area Path, number from the Iteration Path; DP's milestones and data sets are DP's own and not used; counts Releases in the month); **Like IOCC — grouped by area, name from title** (Type of Release from the Area Path segment after the stream's own name or an earlier name, else the last Area Path segment; Completed and Supported as IOCC counts them; bulk Create Features); **Like Content — each document is a release by its title** (or by the stream's naming patterns; bulk Create Features); **Like Tool Validations — one per tool** (Releases and Supported as Tool Validations); **Like S&S IT — Windchill path**; **Like ECP — Windchill projects** (each export named after the stream read by project, on its own, ECP logic by default on import). CP's release-folder tree rule is not offered. A custom stream's Release page shall have the parts of UI-STANDARDS §10 as its rule gives them: source bar, counts with "What was counted", Releases \| Source data tabs, the common columns with Time Writing Feature, Planned (h), Booked (h) (the QMS-0025 match) and Create Feature, and Select / Create Features where the rule has bulk creation. | T | M | UC-08 |
| STR-04 | A custom stream's **Fuzzy Feature matching** shall decide its Time Writing Feature as for DP and IOCC (a percentage, at the Stream mapping threshold) when on, and the exact title match when off; its **Epics** shall limit the QMS-0025 match (board column, Create Feature's existing-match check) as QV-57 does for the built-in streams; its **earlier names** shall keep connections and exports named after them with the stream. | T | S | UC-08 |
| STR-05 | `custom-streams.json` read from Git shall be **validated**: a file that is not JSON or not a custom-streams file (schema 1) is refused as a whole and the streams last read stay in use, with a warning; each stream is checked on its own — an **unknown rule**, CP's tree rule, a name or earlier name that is a **built-in stream** (or its earlier name QRE), a data set (Pedestal, DHFI) or a reserved page name, or that **clashes with an earlier custom stream** is refused with a clear message (listed in the panel as "Not used — custom-streams.json in Git: <name>: <reason>") while the others are used; at most 30 streams, aliases cleaned, Epic IDs positive whole numbers. The last good file is kept in this browser so the menu and Release pages of custom streams are there at the next start before Git answers. | T | M | UC-02 |
| STR-06 | **Edit** shall change a custom stream and **rename** it, keeping the old name as an earlier name so its connection or export and their data stay with it; **Remove** shall ask first in a pop-up that names what changes (its Release page and lines leave the menu, Pulse Check, the Dashboard and the Monthly pack setup) and that its connection or export and their data are **kept** (then listed under Other views); both are saved to Git at once ("Rename stream: <old> → <new>", "Change stream: …", "Remove stream: <name>"). | T | M | UC-02 |
| STR-07 | Adding, changing and removing custom streams shall need **Change the stream mapping** (`streams.manage`, CON-93) and fail closed (CON-101): for anyone else — and while the roles are not loaded, Git storage is not connected or read-only, the synthetic sample is loaded, or the file has not been read yet — **＋ Add stream…**, **Edit** and **Remove** are disabled and a visible line says why (CON-102: "Your role does not allow “Change the stream mapping …”. An Admin can change this in Admin → People & roles → Role rights."); the custom streams are still shown to everyone and work for everyone. | T | M | UC-22 |

### 5.38 Page builder (PGB) — UC-38

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PGB-01 | A page shall be of a **page type**: **Table** — the On-Demand work table (OND-03…OND-10, OND-18…OND-26) without the Insights tab — or **Table + Insights** — the same with the **Data** and **Insights** tabs (OND-11…OND-17). The types shall be one list in the code (`pageKinds`), so that further types are added as further kinds — **On-Demand Dashboard** (DSH-01) is one; a Release page for a new stream comes later; a type in the file that this build does not know is kept as written and the page shown as Table + Insights. Changing a page to Table hides the Insights tab and keeps its charts in the page file. | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-02 | The **menu groups** shall be the built-in ones — Data Insights, My Work, Testing, I&D Board, Release Details, Other views, On-Demand, Data & governance (the Admin group is not configurable) — which keep their name and place and **cannot be renamed or removed, only configured**, and **groups of your own**, shown after On-Demand in their order. Every group has the settings **Accepts added pages** (on / off; by default on for On-Demand and for a new group of your own, off for the other built-in groups) and its **co-owners** (work emails, several per group). | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-03 | The menu layout shall be **one shared file**, `menu-layout.json` in the Git data repository next to the planning data (not in `on-demand/`, whose every file is a page, OND-08): schema 1, kind `menu-layout`, `groups` by id (a group of your own: `g-<name>` with name and order; a built-in group by its fixed id, e.g. `on-demand`, `data-insights`) with `acceptsPages` and `owners`, and `pages` by page id with `type`, `group`, `order`, `icon` and `owners`. It shall be **validated when read** (not a layout: refused and reported, the layout last read kept; bad ids, a group of your own without a name, emails that are not emails dropped; an unknown icon the default; at most 30 groups of your own, 50 co-owners each), cached in the browser for the menu while Git is not connected, and saved with **queued, merged saves** (GIT-17): merged by group and page id, each field of an entry three ways (a field one save changed takes that change, one both changed the later save's), the **co-owners as sets** — an email either save added stays, an email either save removed (the other leaving it as it was) goes — and an entry one save removed and the other changed: the later save wins (removed and untouched: removed). Before any layout save, GitHub must confirm the repository is private. | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-04 | The **sidebar, Home and the top bar** shall follow the menu layout: an added page is listed in its group (after the group's own entries; On-Demand and groups of your own hold only added pages) by its order, then by name, with its icon; a group of your own is a heading of its own and a Home tile with its pages, and a page in a built-in group is also on that area's Home tile; the top bar shows the page's name, with its description (else "{page}, in {group}") as the tooltip. A page whose group **no longer exists** shall fall back to **On-Demand**. All added pages are viewed through the menu area **On-Demand pages** (CON-103), those in Testing by the Admin role only (ACT-01). **Personal views** (MYD-08): each added page can be pinned or hidden in Customize menu (as `ondemand:<page id>`); hiding "On-Demand" hides them all, as before. | T | M | UC-38, UC-01 |
| PGB-05 | **＋ Add page** shall open a pop-up (UI-STANDARDS §3/§4, eyebrow "PAGES & MENU") from **Admin → Pages & menu** (page action, and **＋ Page** in a group's row), from **⋯** next to a group's heading in the menu (shown only on groups the viewer may add to) and from **＋ Add page…** on a page: **Page type** (Table, Table + Insights), **Page name** (required), **Description** (optional), **Icon** (a small fixed set: ▦ ▤ ◫ ◉ ◇ ◈ ◎ ◔ ⚑ ★ ✎ ☰), **Menu group** — the groups that accept added pages and the viewer may add to, or, for a Tool Owner, **＋ New group…** with its name — and **Page co-owners** (optional). **Add page** creates the blank page file in Git at once (`on-demand/<page-id>.json`), then saves its place in the menu layout (and the new group), each a queued save, and opens the page; the result goes to Background jobs. | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-06 | **Groups of your own** shall be **added** (＋ New group…, a name unique among all groups, built-in ones too, up to 40 characters), **renamed**, **reordered** (↑ ↓) and **removed** — removal only when the group holds **no page** ("Move or remove its N pages first: only an empty group can be removed."), after a pop-up that asks — and every group (built-in ones too) **configured** (Edit: Accepts added pages, co-owners), each a queued save of the menu layout. | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-07 | An added page shall be **edited** in a pop-up — name and description (saved in the page's file; refused while the page has unsaved changes: "Save or discard the unsaved changes to {page} first …"), type, icon, **menu group** (a move: the groups the viewer may move it to) and co-owners — **reordered** within its group (↑ ↓) and **removed** after a pop-up naming the page, its rows and columns and that its file is deleted (OND-10), its place in the menu layout removed with it. | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-08 | A holder of **"Manage pages and menu groups"** (CON-106, the Tool Owner) may create, rename, reorder, configure and remove groups (built-in ones only configured), add a page to any group that accepts added pages, move, rename, reorder and remove any added page and name the co-owners of every group and page. | T | M | UC-38, UC-22 |
| PGB-09 | A **group co-owner** — while the group **accepts added pages** — may in that group add, rename, reorder, move within (between groups only that they co-own) and remove pages, name those pages' co-owners, set those pages up (columns, options, key prefix, insights) and edit their rows; they may **not** create or remove groups, change the group's settings or co-owners, or touch other groups. A group that stops accepting added pages gives its co-owners nothing until it accepts them again. | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-10 | A **page co-owner** (several per page) may set up that page (columns, options, key prefix, insights) and edit its rows, nothing else. The role rights **"Set up On-Demand pages"** and **"Edit On-Demand data (rows)"** (CON-103) keep applying as **role-wide** rights to every such page, and "Set up On-Demand pages" still adds, renames and removes the pages of the **On-Demand** group (OND-10). | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-11 | Ownership shall be matched by the viewer's **work email** (CON-82; with View as, the viewed person's, read only); without a work email nobody is an owner. Rights shall **fail closed**: without a roles file nobody may change anything ("Read only: the access roles are not loaded yet, so nothing can be changed in the menu."), and without Git storage that can save, or with the synthetic sample, the reason of OND-07 applies. Every button the viewer may not use is **disabled, not hidden**, with the reason as its tooltip and once as a visible line above a table (CON-102) — e.g. "Only a Tool Owner (“Manage pages and menu groups”) can create, rename, reorder, configure or remove menu groups. …", "Only a Tool Owner or a co-owner of the group “{group}” can add pages to it.", "“{group}” does not accept added pages. …"; the ⋯ of a group in the menu is shown only where the viewer may add. | T | M | UC-38, UC-22 |
| PGB-12 | **Admin → Pages & menu** (an Admin page shown to everyone) shall show **Your rights here** (Tool Owner or view only, the groups and pages you co-own, your work email), **Menu groups** — a list in the shared table (UI-STANDARDS §5): order, group, kind (Built-in / Custom), accepts added pages, co-owners, added pages, Actions (**＋ Page**, **Edit**, ↑ ↓ and **Remove** for groups of your own) — and **Pages** — group, order, page with its icon and description, type, co-owners, columns, rows, Actions (**Open →**, **Edit**, ↑ ↓, **Remove**); page actions **＋ Add page…**, **＋ New group…** and **↻ Reload from Git**; ⓘ says who may change what and where it is saved; the save queue state with Retry (GIT-17). | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-13 | Pages from before the page builder — **CoNQ** and the pages added under On-Demand — shall **migrate in place**: without a placement in the menu layout a page is a **Table + Insights** page in **On-Demand** in name order; no page file, key, ID, log or insight is changed, and no file is written until someone changes the page's place; a missing `menu-layout.json` is an empty layout. | T | M | UC-38 |
| PGB-14 | The co-owners' **work emails** in `menu-layout.json` are personal data in a shared file of the private data repository: only emails typed in by a Tool Owner or a group co-owner, shown in Admin → Pages & menu and in the page's ⓘ, removed with Edit (the history stays in Git); no other personal data is kept in the file (SM-100). | I, T | S | UC-38 |

### 5.39 On-Demand Dashboard pages (DSH) — UC-38

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. | Trace |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DSH-01 | **On-Demand Dashboard** shall be a third page type of the page builder (PGB-01, `pageKinds`): added with **＋ Add page** (PGB-05) to any group that accepts added pages, with its icon, description and co-owners, its name and description in its page file (`on-demand/<page-id>.json`, no columns or rows). A page keeps its family: **Edit** offers On-Demand Dashboard only for a dashboard and Table / Table + Insights only for a table page, the other choice disabled with the reason ("A table page cannot become an On-Demand Dashboard: its rows would be hidden …"). **Remove page…** (on the page or in Admin → Pages & menu) asks first naming the page and its blocks and deletes its page file and its dashboard file; the data sources are not changed. A dashboard is never a data source. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-02 | An On-Demand Dashboard page shall be **read only** and laid out top to bottom (UI-STANDARDS §1, §7, §9): the page actions (＋ Add block…, Arrange blocks…, Tabs and filters…, ＋ Add page…, Rename page…, Remove page…, ↻ Reload from Git), the **filter row**, the period line ("Period: 2026 (whole year)."), the **KPI tiles** (`Metric`), the **tabs** when the owners made any, the **chart and table panels** of the chosen tab, and "About this dashboard" (ⓘ: what it shows, who builds it, where it is saved, the co-owners). The only write actions are the owners' building actions. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-03 | A block's **data source** shall be the **rows of a table page** — any On-Demand, Table or Table + Insights page except On-Demand Dashboards and the page itself, as saved in Git (not a viewer's unsaved changes), read from Git as those pages are (OND-08), offered by name. A block whose page is no longer there (removed, or not visible) says so ("The table page “…” is no longer there (removed), or you cannot see it.") instead of drawing. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-04 | Table pages shall be the **only kind of data source** (owner decision, Phase 2): a block or filter naming another kind of source in the file is dropped with the reason (DSH-08). The source key (`page:<page-id>`) is one pattern in the code, so that further sources (e.g. the Time Writing hours) can be added later; Team overview, My dashboard and the Insights tab of the table pages are not changed by this page type. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-05 | A **block** shall be a **KPI tile**, a **Bar chart**, a **Line chart**, a **Pie chart** or a **Summary table**, each with a title (up to 120 characters), a data source, a **measure** — Count of rows, Sum or Average of a Number or Currency column (a Currency column one currency at a time, the one of most rows first, never added across currencies, OND-24) — for charts and tables a **group-by** field (and, except a pie, an optional second field), optional **block filters** (OND-14), the **Date column the period applies to** (by default the source's first Date column; none: "Not filtered by the period") and, with tabs, its tab. The aggregation, the "(none)" and "Other" rules and the reference checks are those of the On-Demand insights (OND-13, OND-17). **＋ Add block…** opens a pop-up (eyebrow "DASHBOARD · BLOCK") with a live preview over the current period; the main action (**Add block** / **Save to Git**) is refused with the reason while a field is missing or does not fit. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-06 | Charts shall be drawn in the shared chart frame (UI-STANDARDS §9: legend, **Data labels on** to start, **Table / Chart**, axis names); a **summary table** in the shared table (§5): sorted **largest first** ("(none)" and "Other" last), the **first 10 rows** with **Show all (+N more)**, a Total row, the row count and **Export** (Excel / CSV through the neutralising CSV helper, CON-94). **Arrange blocks…** lists the blocks in order with **Edit**, **↑ ↓** and **Remove** (after a pop-up naming the block); **Tabs and filters…** adds, renames, reorders and removes up to 10 **tabs** (a removed tab's blocks go to the first tab) and up to 6 **filters of the filter row**. KPI tiles are shown above the tabs; with tabs each tile has **Open {tab} →**. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-07 | The **filter row** shall offer **Year → Quarter → Month** as dependent multi-selects (`MultiSelect`; `Period`, `periodMonths`, `narrowPeriod`, offered quarters and months as Team overview, OV-15, OV-16): nothing ticked in Year is the **current year**, nothing ticked in Quarter or Month every one of the years or quarters counted; a block keeps the rows whose month in its Date column is in the period (rows without that date left out and counted). Each **owner-defined filter** lets the viewer tick values of a Dropdown or Multi-select column of one data source (nothing ticked = all) and narrows the blocks over that source. **Reset filters** goes back to the current year with no filter. The years offered are the current year, those found in the blocks' dates and any chosen. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-08 | The dashboard shall be **one file per page**, `dashboards/<page-id>.json` in the Git data repository (schema 1, kind `dashboard`: `tabs`, `filters`, `blocks` by id), created with the first block and saved at once with the **queued, merged save** (GIT-17): merged by block, tab and filter id, each field three ways (a field one save changed takes that change; both changed: the later save's), an entry one save removed and the other changed: the later save wins (removed and untouched: removed), the order following the save that changed it. **Validated when read**: a file that is not a dashboard is refused whole (reported, the one last read kept); a bad block — unknown type, bad or repeated id, no title, unknown data source — is **dropped with a message** on the page; at most **40 blocks**, 10 tabs, 6 filters, 10 filters per block, 200 KB; column references are kept as written and checked when drawn (DSH-10). Before any dashboard save, GitHub must confirm the repository is private. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-09 | Whoever may **set up the page** (PGB-08…PGB-10: the role right "Set up On-Demand pages", a co-owner of the page or of its group) shall **build** its dashboard; renaming, moving and removing the page follow PGB-07…PGB-11. Rights **fail closed** (CON-101: nothing without the roles file; nothing without Git storage that can save or with the synthetic sample) and every builder button is **disabled, not hidden**, with the reason as its tooltip and once as a visible line (CON-102: "Your role does not allow “Set up On-Demand pages (pages, columns, options)”. …"). Viewers still choose their own period, filters and tab. | T | M | UC-38, UC-22 |
| DSH-10 | A block with nothing in the period and filters shall show **"No data for this selection."** with "Change the period or the filters above, or Reset filters …"; a block that cannot be drawn (a column removed or of another type, its data source gone or not available) shall say why ("This block cannot be shown: …") while the other blocks are drawn; each panel states what it measures, the period column, its filters and how many rows it counts. | T | M | UC-38 |
| DSH-11 | The viewer's **tab, period and filter choices** shall be remembered per person (personal views, MYD-08, key `dashboard-page:<page-id>`; without personal views in this browser) and restored when the page opens. | T | S | UC-38, UC-26 |
| DSH-12 | The results of the building actions (add, edit, move, remove a block; tabs and filters; rename and remove the page) shall go to **Background jobs** (UI-STANDARDS §8) naming the page and the file; a save that fails is also kept above the page and in the save queue with Retry (GIT-17); warnings about the data shown (a dashboard file refused or blocks dropped) stay on the page. | T | M | UC-38 |

## 6. Non-functional requirements

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. |
|---|---|---|---|
| NFR-01 | The app shall function with no server component other than static hosting and the external systems in §1.2. | I | M |
| NFR-02 | The app shall keep at least 14,000 work items in IndexedDB and reload them. | T | M |
| NFR-03 | Requests to Azure DevOps from the desktop proxy shall time out after 45 s. Web update checks shall time out after 15 s. | T | S |
| NFR-04 | Every write to Azure DevOps shall be confirmed by reading the item back. When confirmation is not possible, the app shall say the write could not be confirmed and shall not retry automatically. | T | M |
| NFR-05 | PAT input fields shall be password fields, and the desktop proxy shall remove the PAT from any Azure DevOps error text it returns. The redaction shall apply to the PAT as sent (trimmed). | I, T | M |
| NFR-06 | The desktop window shall open at 1440 × 920 px and shall not be resizable below 1024 × 700 px. | I, D | S |
| NFR-07 | The source repository shall contain no production data, tokens or employee details. | I | M |
| NFR-08 | The web app's first download shall hold the shell and the start page only: every other page — and the project documents of the Documentation page — shall be loaded when first opened, with "Loading the page…" shown meanwhile. The main script shall stay under 1 MB (300 KB compressed); v7.43.0: 814 KB (237 KB) against 2.85 MB (830 KB) before. | T | S |
| NFR-09 | The built app (web and desktop) shall carry a **Content-Security-Policy**: scripts only from the app itself (and `file:` for the desktop app, which opens the page from disk), no inline or evaluated scripts, no plugins (`object-src 'none'`), no form posts, base URL fixed, frames only for the Microsoft sign-in, requests only to https hosts (Azure DevOps, on-premises servers, GitHub, Microsoft Graph), images also from data: and blob: URLs; inline style attributes allowed. The local development server does not add it. | T | S |

## 7. Interface requirements

| ID | Requirement | Ver. | Pri. |
|---|---|---|---|
| IF-01 | Azure DevOps: the app shall use REST API version 7.1 by default, or the connection's API version, falling back per DSK-05 in the desktop app. | T | M |
| IF-02 | Azure DevOps writes shall be limited to: creating Features, User Stories and Tasks (title, area, description, acceptance criteria, iteration, assigned to, target date, parent); Original Estimate, Completed Work and Tags on Tasks; and State and Iteration Path on any item. | I, T | M |
| IF-03 | GitHub: the app shall use the repository contents API with a fine-grained token that has Contents: Read and write. | I | M |
| IF-04 | *(Retired — moved to the backlog: SharePoint is not functional; see PROJECT-PLAN.md → Backlog)* Microsoft Graph: the app shall use the `/v1.0/` endpoint for the SharePoint site's default document library. | I | M |
| IF-05 | SharePoint REST (desktop app only, KPT-02): one GET of a file's content (`/_api/web/GetFileByServerRelativeUrl('…')/$value`) in the app's SharePoint session, read only. | I | S |

## 8. Open issues

The open issues (OI-NN: as-built behaviour that is ambiguous, inconsistent or may not match intent, each needing a decision from the product owner) and the known bugs are kept in one register, [KNOWN-ISSUES.md](KNOWN-ISSUES.md). Requirement rows that have an open issue attached refer to it by ID. None of them is changed silently.

## 9. Traceability

The complete trace — use case → user requirement → system requirement → detailed requirement → test case → UAT day → software component — is generated from these documents by `node scripts/traceability.mjs` into [TRACEABILITY-MATRIX.md](TRACEABILITY-MATRIX.md), and `pnpm test` fails when the generated matrix is out of date or a requirement is not covered. The forward trace from requirement to test cases is also kept by hand in [TEST-PLAN.md §7](TEST-PLAN.md#7-requirements-coverage-matrix).
